# Research backlog: what to try next

Generated by a 26-agent research sweep (12 areas, each researched then adversarially
vetted by an engineer persona, plus a completeness critic and a ranking pass).
2.9M tokens, 1,011 tool calls. Every proposal carries a mechanism, an argument for
why it is not redundant with the channels we already ship, and the experiment that
would kill it.

Nothing here is decided. This is the menu, not the plan.

**225 proposals** across **13 areas**, drawn from **186 catalogued techniques**.


---


# Where to start

## 0. What I verified before ranking

Four load-bearing claims in the review corpus, checked against the shipped code:

- **The held-out split is broken, in every harness.** `[...edges].sort(() => rnd() - 0.5)` appears in `/Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/bench/v4-channels.mjs:73`, `graph-fusion-eval.mjs:60`, `graph-fusion-eval2.mjs:57`, `v3-levers.mjs:158`, `v3-crowding.mjs:187`, `v3-calibration.mjs:75`, `v4-metric.mjs:84`, `levers-eval.mjs:66`, `v3-map.mjs:72`, `ideas-eval.mjs:143`. A random comparator is not a shuffle. Every published number in this project — 0.664, 0.745, the +0.081, the 0.075 correlation, and the rejections of CSLS, Mutual Proximity, shared tags, the lexical channel and the personal metric — was produced on a biased split.
- **`metadataCache.unresolvedLinks` is never read.** Zero hits across `/Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/src/*.ts`.
- **`HUB_DEGREE = 40` and `PPR_FRONTIER_MAX = 400`** at `/Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/src/graph-signals.ts:56,58`, against a max observed degree of 37. They have never fired. Worse, several harnesses delete the notes they exist for (`/\bMOC\b/i` filters in `bench/aq-eval.mjs`, `continuity-eval.mjs`, `jina-cache.mjs`).
- **The fusion is exactly as described** — `zSem(s.content) + graphInfluence * zRa(s.ra)` at `/Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/src/index-store.ts:~2628`, and the cluster labeller runs on **titles only** (`labelClusters(usable.map((i) => i.title), …)` in `/Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/src/vault-map.ts:260`).

So: the admission rule is sound, but the instrument enforcing it is not. That reorders the list below.

---

## 1. THE SHORTLIST — 14 items, in order

**Tier 0 — the instrument (do this week, before anything else)**

1. **Fisher-Yates the split, re-baseline, and publish mean ± SD over ≥8 seeds with every number.** Half a day. Seed SD is 0.021 (content) and 0.018 (content+RA), which means roughly a third of the proposals in the corpus promise less than one SD. This also re-opens four prior rejections for free — the harness already contains them.
2. **Start the two long-lead clocks today, because they are calendar-bound, not effort-bound.** (a) The opt-in session/co-edit logger — plain text, one-click wipe, no product surface. Four reviewers independently want this data and none of them can have it for a year unless it starts now. (b) Acquire a second corpus: real, larger, git-versioned, with dates and people in it. The lab vault is 465 notes of which 124 are authentic; it cannot detect +0.02 and it contains zero people.

**Tier 1 — free signal and free precision (the next two weeks)**

3. **Ghost links: Resource Allocation over `unresolvedLinks`, plus the "note you keep asking for" panel.** The best cost/benefit item in the entire corpus. It is verified-unused, it is structurally invisible to the current graph channel (RA runs over `resolvedLinks`, so a phantom target is a shared neighbour the ranker cannot see), it yields a receipt type that does not exist today — "you both reference [[value function]], which you never wrote" — and a self-verifying hygiene panel. Three reviewers converged on it from three different directions.
4. **Per-note calibrated silence.** Replace the global `minSimilarity` with a per-note null model built from the score distribution `rank()` already computes and throws away. Four reviewers converged. This *is* the precision-first thesis, made operational: hub notes stop showing four mediocre cards, sparse atomic notes stop showing nothing.
5. **Orbit-typed receipts + 2-hop path receipts.** Co-citation and bibliographic coupling were measured separately (0.711 and 0.737 against RA's 0.796) — dead as scores. Spend them on the *explanation*, where they cannot fail an admission test: "both cited by [[ML MOC]]" instead of "via [[ML MOC]]", and the actual route instead of the shared node. This is the highest-return category in the corpus precisely because it carries no ranking risk.
6. **Temporal receipts on cards, gated by a date-coverage census.** "Written four days before this", "two years earlier" — and nothing at all below the coverage threshold, with settings stating the coverage honestly. Ships value on day one and is the precondition for every other time feature.

**Tier 2 — the magic slot (needs no new plumbing)**

7. **The triangle you never closed, ranked by how far apart you wrote the three pairs.** A triple assembled across a year is a gap in your thinking; three notes from one afternoon are one afternoon. Self-verifying in two seconds because the user wrote all three notes.
8. **Cursor-window / passage-level live retrieval.** Reportedly ~80% already shipped. It is the only proposal that changes *when* the panel is useful rather than what it ranks, and it converts the existing chunk index into a new product surface for near-zero cost.
9. **Forgotten neighbour, mtime-primary, one card per day, own slot.** "You have not touched this since March; it is the closest thing in your vault to what you are writing now." Magic, cheap, and needs no interaction log.
10. **Curvature bridge cards as a panel, not as an RA reweight.** "[[Cosine Similarity]] is the only thing connecting your German maths notes to your English ML notes." Expect the reweighting to fail; ship the panel.

**Tier 3 — plumbing that decides whether the product survives its own claims**

11. **Get chunk text out of the JSON manifest, add incremental centering, add content-addressed chunk vectors.** These three, not ANN, are what break at 100k notes. Unglamorous; it is the difference between meeting the stated constraint and not.
12. **Fit `graphInfluence` from vault statistics instead of a slider.** Verified as a user-set constant in the fusion. On a link-sparse vault it should quietly go to zero; on a dense Zettelkasten it should rise. One slider disappears, and the plugin stops being tuned for one vault shape.
13. **Stage-1 objective fix: exact idea-level MaxSim in the shortlist.** Stage 1 ranks by whole-note cosine while Stage 2 scores by chunk BiMax — so a note that relates through one paragraph is dropped before it is ever considered. Real defect, measurable with the (fixed) protocol.
14. **Self-gating: run the admission rule on the user's own vault, and say so in settings.** "People channel: OFF — your people are already fully linked, so it adds nothing here." This turns the project's discipline into a product feature, and it makes every risky channel safe to ship, because a channel that fails on your vault turns itself off.

---

## 2. THREE ROADMAPS

### (a) Entity vault — people, places, time as first-class structure

Ships in this order: date-coverage census and note-time resolver → temporal receipts on cards → **EntityIndex, typed, not PersonIndex** (build the substrate for named things generally; let the vault decide whether they are people, projects or places) → entity salience strip, entity-conditioned related notes, entity timeline strip (all display, no ranking claim, no admission-rule exposure) → alias-merge suggestions written into frontmatter, because that improves the user's own data and compounds → only then the entity channel, degree-corrected, behind self-gating → entity bridges as the magic card.

**Riskiest assumption, stated bluntly: a shared-entity channel is a shared-tags channel with a different vocabulary, and shared tags were already measured at r = 0.916 against the graph channel and cut.** Two notes that name the same entity usually also *wikilink* it, in which case RA already has the evidence. The only version that escapes is **unlinked mentions** — entities named in prose and never linked — and the measured population of those on the only vault we have is zero people, zero person-shaped frontmatter keys, and 106 unresolved targets that are all concepts. So the ranking value of this entire roadmap rests on one unmeasured quantity. Pre-register the kill: on the acquired corpus, unlinked-entity co-mention must show rank correlation < 0.2 with the graph channel *and* add ≥ 1 SD of recall. If it fails, the roadmap collapses to items 3-5, which are display features — good ones, but a month for a name-chip strip.

### (b) Deeper graph

Ships in this order: fixed instrument → ghost links (nominator + receipt + panel) → orbit-typed and path receipts → open triangles → curvature bridge panel → **hyperedge-degree RA** (count the distinct *blocks* a shared note is mentioned in, not its link count) and **co-mention proximity weighting** (two links written side by side are stronger evidence than two links sharing a 200-item index) → link-community layer on the Vault Map plus consensus clustering with abstention, where the *disagreement* between what notes say and how they link is the product.

**Riskiest assumption: that anything is left in the adjacency matrix.** Ten kernels were measured — Adamic-Adar, common neighbours, Katz, commute-time, SimRank, autocovariance, raw walks, co-citation, coupling, RA — and nothing beat RA. The candidate pool is already 97% complete and recall@10 is 0.80. So this roadmap is a bet that the remaining signal lies in **re-defining the edge** (hyperedges, block scope, unresolved targets) rather than **re-diffusing the same edges**. If hyperedge-degree RA and co-mention proximity both land inside 1 SD, the graph area is closed for *scoring*, and everything left in it is presentation. That would be a clean, valuable result — but plan for it.

### (c) Engine

Ships in this order: **measure first** — build a 50k-note vault and record cold-start index time, resident memory at load, and p95 rank latency → chunk text out of the JSON manifest → incremental centering → content-addressed chunk vectors → binary Stage 0 with int8 refine, and **explicitly do not ship an ANN index** (a brute-force binary scan over 100k × 384 bits is a few megabytes and beats the maintenance cost of a graph index that must survive incremental edits) → Stage-1 MaxSim fix → WebGPU by default, with a probe that loads *the same weights* on both devices → per-note calibrated cutoff, sentence-level receipts, passage-selection retrieval.

**Riskiest assumption: that speed is what limits the product.** Nobody has ever run this plugin on a 50,000-note vault; every scale claim in the README is untested. If the 100k path turns out to be fine, this roadmap buys latency the user never noticed. That is why the scale census comes before any optimisation — it is the one measurement that decides the whole roadmap, and it costs a day.

---

## 3. THE KILL LIST

- **Every remaining diffusion kernel and spectral method** — Katz, SimRank, regularized commute time, heat-kernel push, autocovariance, BSPM, spectral compression, GraphWave. They are polynomials in the same adjacency matrix, ten have been measured, none beat RA. Non-backtracking walks are the only non-polynomial left; give them two hours in the bench, not a project.
- **Learned models on 1,327 edges** — ComplEx-N3, GNNs, NodePiece, PRA-lite, few-parameter learned combiners, cross-encoder rerankers, vault-fitted sparse autoencoders. There is no held-out set at this corpus size that can detect the gains they claim, so they cannot satisfy the admission rule even in principle.
- **The GLiNER NER pass.** A second model download for a target population measured at zero.
- **The whole "two notes name the same string" family** — shared tags (already cut at r = 0.916), shared people, shared places, shared dates, shared external references, temporal co-mention. Each one is the same mechanism in a different vocabulary, and the shared string is usually also a wikilink, so RA has it already. Require the *unlinked-only* variant with a measured fire rate before writing code.
- **Person de-crowding, person-time, shared-time channel, temporal contiguity as a channel, temporal negative evidence, note-script channel, superseded-statement detection, point-algebra ordering.** All return exactly 0.000 on the available corpus; several also invert the precision-first stance by ranking on absence of evidence.
- **Hyperbolic vault map, HRG consensus dendrograms, persistent H1, effective-resistance leverage, Forman as an RA reweight, L3 paths, untrained message passing, DotHash sketches, the bridge-notes panel that rediscovers node degree, typed suggestion slots.** Measured dead, or blocked on inputs (a relation vocabulary, an order relation) that this vault does not contain.
- **Tuning `HUB_DEGREE`.** Verified: it is 40, max degree is 37, it has never fired, and the harnesses delete the MOC notes it exists for. Instrument it and fix the harness filter. Do not sweep it.
- **Any proposal whose claimed gain is under 0.02.** That is inside one seed SD. Not wrong — unfalsifiable, at this corpus size.

---

## 4. THE FIVE CHEAPEST EXPERIMENTS, one day each

1. **Fisher-Yates re-baseline with 12 seeds.** Half a day. Every number in the project gets an interval for the first time, and the four prior rejections get re-tested at no extra cost because the code is already in the harness. Do not run anything else until this is done — you would be measuring against a defective bar.
2. **A vault-census command, run on the owner's real vault.** Person-shaped frontmatter keys, date coverage, unresolved-link count and shape, folder depth, degree distribution, template groups, note count. The lab vault is 73% generated and is the wrong thing to be deciding priority A on. One day, and it either opens or closes roadmap (a) before a line of entity code is written.
3. **Ghost-link independence probe.** Build RA over `unresolvedLinks` on the lab vault (106 targets are already counted) and measure three things: rank correlation with resolved-RA, held-out recall delta, and fire rate. If correlation exceeds 0.3, ghost links are just RA wearing a hat and the feature reduces to the hygiene panel — still worth shipping, but not a channel. One day.
4. **Scale census.** Generate a 50k-note vault by duplicating and perturbing the lab vault, load it, record index time, resident memory, p95 rank latency. One day, and it decides roadmap (c) outright — including whether the JSON-manifest fix is urgent or theoretical.
5. **The three crowding audits in one script.** For each query, compare the distribution of Δt, folder distance, and template group in the top-10 against the vault baseline. One day, and it simultaneously kills or confirms era de-crowding, folder de-crowding, time-bucketed template de-crowding, and the place-ridge diagnostic — four proposals, one script.

*If you have a spare afternoon after those:* count how many cards the shipped ranker currently labels only "Similar text", and how many of them could gain a receipt from (a) a 2-hop path, (b) an orbit type, (c) a shared unresolved target, (d) a date relation. That number is the size of the entire receipts opportunity, and it tells you which receipt to build first.

# The full menu, by area


## People as first-class entities


<details><summary>10 techniques surveyed</summary>

- **Resource Allocation index (degree-penalised shared-neighbour link prediction)** (established, 2009) — Scores a node pair by sum over shared neighbours x of 1/deg(x), so a hub neighbour contributes almost nothing and a rare shared neighbour contributes a lot. Verified shipped in /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/src/graph-signals.ts (evidence(), with HUB_DEGREE=40 excluding hubs from the receipt entirely). The bipartite note-x-person analogue P3 proposes is a direct, correct transposition of this code.  
  <sub>Zhou, Lu & Zhang, 'Predicting missing links via local information', Eur. Phys. J. B 71 (2009). Implementation verified in-repo at src/graph-signals.ts lines ~120-145.</sub>
- **c-TF-IDF class-based term weighting (BERTopic labelling)** (established, 2022) — Treats a cluster as one pseudo-document and scores terms by within-cluster frequency against spread across clusters, isolating what makes a group distinct. VERIFIED IN-REPO at src/vault-map.ts labelClusters(). CRITICAL CORRECTION to P6/P13: the shipped function takes `titles: string[]` — one short title per note, not bodies — is module-private (only buildVaultMap is exported), caps output at 3 single tokens via `picked.length >= 3`, and joins with ' · '. It cannot emit phrases and it has almost no text to work with on an 8-note person set.  
  <sub>Grootendorst, 'BERTopic: Neural topic modeling with a class-based TF-IDF procedure', arXiv:2203.05794 (2022). In-repo implementation: src/vault-map.ts.</sub>
- **MMR-diversified keyphrase extraction over embedded candidate n-grams (KeyBERT-style)** (established, 1998/2020) — Extracts 1-3 word candidate phrases, embeds them, and picks a diverse set by maximal marginal relevance. This — NOT the c-TF-IDF cluster labeller — is the machinery that actually produces multi-word phrases in this codebase. Shipped in index-store.ts with MAX_PHRASE_WORDS=3, MAX_CANDIDATES=36, MAX_LABEL_PHRASES=3, MMR_LAMBDA=0.6, TARGET_LABEL_WORDS 3-7, driving summaryLabel. P6 panel 3 and P13 should target this path, at the cost of one extra ONNX pass per batch.  
  <sub>Grootendorst, KeyBERT (2020); Carbonell & Goldstein, MMR, SIGIR 1998. In-repo: src/index-store.ts lines ~197-222 and computeSummaryLabels().</sub>
- **Determiner-context typing for German proper-noun detection** (established, measured 2026-07-28) — German common nouns take determiners (der/die/das/dem/den/des/ein/eine/im/am/zum/zur/beim/vom...); proper names generally do not. Scoring a capitalized surface by the fraction of its occurrences preceded by a closed-class determiner separates the two without a POS tagger, a name lexicon, or a model. I MEASURED THIS on the lab vault's German prose (426 surfaces at >=8 occurrences): high-rate bucket is cleanly common nouns (Ebene .93, Ende .82, Reihe .79, Idee .75, Begriff .75, Kante .75); rate-0.00 bucket contains no German common nouns at all. It is a validated NEGATIVE filter. Caveat found in the same run: the 0.00 bucket is full of English technical terms (Sharding, WebSocket, Refactoring), so it rejects German common nouns but does not by itself identify people.  
  <sub>Standard closed-class distributional cue; no paper needed. Measured by me this session on /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/lab/vault/real, script at /private/tmp/det.mjs.</sub>
- **Background-corpus frequency ratio as a name/common-word discriminator** (established, in-repo) — A proper name is rare in general language but frequent in your vault; a common noun is frequent in both. The repo ALREADY SHIPS the background model: bench/data/de_50k.txt and en_50k.txt (~620-660 KB raw, word + corpus count, one per line), currently used only by continuity-eval.mjs. IMPORTANT MEASURED CAVEAT that kills the naive version: the lists do not cleanly separate names from nouns, because the source corpus is dialogue-heavy. I checked ranks directly — Anna 1472, Schmidt 6192, Müller 7891, Lukas 8246 sit ABOVE common nouns like Dienstag 3159, Küchentisch 27292, Betreuerin 28431. So background rank is a useful feature, never a decisive filter, and 'absent from the list' (e.g. Freiburg) is the only high-precision cell.  
  <sub>Frequency lists in-repo at /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/related-notes/bench/data/{de_50k,en_50k}.txt (hermitdave/FrequencyWords lineage, OpenSubtitles-derived). Ranks measured by me this session.</sub>
- **Statistically Validated Networks (hypergeometric filtering of bipartite projections)** (established, 2011) — Before projecting a bipartite incidence matrix into a co-occurrence graph, test each candidate edge against a hypergeometric null given both nodes' degrees, and FDR-correct across tested pairs, so only over-represented co-occurrence survives. This is the correct gate for P3/P7 in principle. Engineering caveat I would raise regardless of corpus: with a few hundred notes and a few hundred people the test has very little power, so it either validates almost nothing or, once FDR is relaxed to compensate, almost everything.  
  <sub>Tumminello et al., 'Statistically validated networks in bipartite complex systems', PLoS ONE 6(3):e17994 (2011). Cited by the prior researcher; I did not re-verify the meta-validation follow-up they reference and would not build against that claim unread.</sub>
- **Fellegi-Sunter record linkage with Jaro-Winkler string comparison and blocking** (established, 1969/1990) — Probabilistic pairwise entity matching: block candidates on a cheap key (shared first or last token) to avoid the quadratic comparison, score with a prefix-weighted string metric tuned for short person names, combine independent field agreements. The blocking step is what keeps P8 linear; without it, alias matching is O(n^2) over the roster.  
  <sub>Fellegi & Sunter, JASA 64 (1969); Winkler, 'String Comparator Metrics and Enhanced Decision Rules', 1990. Standard, uncontroversial.</sub>
- **One-referent-per-discourse / local surface-form expansion** (established, 1992) — Within one document, a full-name mention licenses the bare-surname or bare-given-name mentions that follow it. This is the single highest-yield disambiguation rule for personal notes and is the load-bearing part of P2's mechanism (ii). It is a rule, not a model, and it is cheap.  
  <sub>Long-standing coreference/entity-linking convention (Gale, Church & Yarowsky 'one sense per discourse', 1992, adapted to names). Standard practice.</sub>
- **SapBERT-style contextual-centroid alias representation** (established, 2021) — Represent a surface by the mean of the embeddings of contexts it occurs in; true aliases of one entity have near-identical context centroids. P8 signal (ii). UNDECLARED DEPENDENCY I found: this needs a surface-to-chunk mapping, and IndexStore only persists chunkTexts when the showSummary option is on (index-store.ts line 340: 'showSummary: boolean; // persist chunkTexts so summaries survive a reload'). With summaries off there is no stored text to locate a surface in, so the signal requires either forcing summaries on or a full vault re-read plus a deterministic re-chunk.  
  <sub>Liu et al., 'Self-alignment pretraining for biomedical entity representations', NAACL 2021, arXiv:2010.11784 — used here only as the representation insight, with no training. Dependency verified in-repo at src/index-store.ts.</sub>
- **GLiNER (prompt-typed compact NER)** (recent-but-replicated, 2024) — A bidirectional-encoder NER that takes its entity type set as natural-language input, so ['person'] needs no fine-tuning and no task head. Relevant only as the option I am rejecting: onnx-community/gliner_small-v2.1 int8 is ~183 MB, its backbone is English-centric DeBERTa-v3-small, and it would be a second heavy model in a plugin whose constraint 3 permits exactly one.  
  <sub>Zaratiana et al., 'GLiNER: Generalist Model for Named Entity Recognition using Bidirectional Transformer', NAACL 2024, arXiv:2311.08526. Model sizes as quoted by the prior researcher; I did NOT independently verify the 183.4 MB figure (no network access used in this pass) and it should be checked before anyone relies on it.</sub>

</details>


### 1. [BLOCKER — BUILD FIRST] A0. A people-bearing evaluation corpus, or the whole area is unfalsifiable

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. This is the reason any of the rest can be believed.

**How.** Every proposal below measures on /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/lab/vault/real. I checked what is in it: 1 #person tag (an EMPTY template, Attachments/templates/Person.md, with a blank name: field), 0 person-shaped frontmatter keys across all 465 notes, and 106 unresolved wikilink targets that are 100% concepts in the sample (Aggregation, B-Tree, Bayes Theorem, Dijkstra Algorithm, Cross-Entropy, GoA Plot). It is a bilingual CS/Maths Zettelkasten. There are no people in it. Build the corpus before the code, following the discipline already established in /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/lab/corpus-manifest.json (which records that only 124 of the 465 are the authentic export). Three options, in order of preference: (a) the user's real iCloud vault contains a Person.md TEMPLATE, which strongly implies instantiated person notes exist there — export a sanitised person-bearing subset with provenance recorded, the same way real/ was produced; (b) plant a known person layer into the lab vault with recorded ground truth, which is the only way to get the planted-structure test P7 needs anyway; (c) for the LINKER only (P1/P2/P8 precision and recall), any public people-dense corpus will do, since those measure entity resolution, not relatedness. Do NOT skip to (c) for the channel proposals — linker accuracy on Enron says nothing about whether a people channel helps rank a personal vault.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not a channel. It is the precondition that makes the admission rule executable at all. The admission rule compares a new channel against existing ones ON A CORPUS; run on a corpus with zero instances of the new signal, it returns 'no effect' for every proposal here and the team would wrongly conclude the area is dead.

**Kill experiment.** The corpus is adequate when it satisfies, and these are the numbers to hit: >= 40 distinct people each appearing in >= 3 notes; >= 30% of person mentions NOT wrapped in wikilinks (below that, P2 has nothing to add over the existing RA channel and the area collapses into the shared-tags outcome); both German and English represented; and person-per-note median >= 2 (below 2, P4 has nothing to rank). Report all four before writing any scoring code. If (a) and (b) both fail to reach these, the honest conclusion is that this owner's vault is not a people vault and the area should be closed in favour of A3's entity generalisation.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The user's real vault may also turn out to be concept-only, in which case the correct outcome is to close the area rather than to build a channel that cannot fire. That is a good outcome for a week of work. The second risk is the tempting shortcut: generating synthetic person notes with an LLM to fill the gap. That would produce a corpus whose person structure was invented by the same kind of process the plugin forbids at runtime, and every number measured on it would be circular. If people are planted, plant them by explicit hand-written rule with recorded ground truth, per option (b).

**Needs first.** None. This is step zero and everything else waits on it.


### 2. [NEW] A1. Determiner-context typing — the German fix, measured, zero download

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible. It is how the plugin decides 'Kathrin' is a name and 'Küchentisch' is not, in a language where both are capitalised.

**How.** German common nouns take determiners; proper names generally do not. For each capitalised surface, compute the fraction of its vault occurrences immediately preceded by a closed-class determiner (der die das dem den des ein eine einem einen einer eines im am zum zur beim vom ins plus possessives and demonstratives — about 40 words, no lexicon file). High rate = common noun, reject. I RAN THIS on the lab vault's German prose this session (script at /private/tmp/det.mjs, 426 surfaces at >=8 occurrences, code fences and frontmatter and $math$ stripped) and the separation is clean at the top: Ebene .93, Modulare .90, Energieerhaltung .88, Ende .82, Zufallsvariable .79, Reihe .79, Idee .75, Begriff .75, Kante .75 — every one a genuine German common noun. The 0.00 bucket contains no German common nouns at all. Layer the frequency lists ALREADY IN THE REPO (bench/data/de_50k.txt, en_50k.txt, currently used only by continuity-eval.mjs) as a second, weaker feature — but see the risk field, because I measured them and the naive version does not work. Ship the determiner counter as a ~30-line pass over the same text the chunker already reads at index time.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not a channel; it is the type classifier P1 needs and the reason P9's 183 MB and P10's flaky prototypes are both mostly unnecessary. It attacks the failure mode the prior researcher correctly identified as the one that blocks the whole area ('German is the one that will fail') with a signal that costs nothing and that I have already shown fires correctly.

**Kill experiment.** Two numbers on the A0 corpus. (1) Rejection precision: of surfaces with determiner rate above tau, what fraction are genuinely common nouns — this should be near 1.0 and is the load-bearing claim. (2) The gap it leaves: on my run, the rate-0.00 bucket was full of ENGLISH technical terms (Sharding, WebSocket, Refactoring, Dependency, Injection), so the filter proves 'not a German common noun', not 'is a person'. Measure how much of the remaining candidate set a second stage must still resolve. Sweep tau over {0.2, 0.35, 0.5} and report the curve. Kill if rejection precision is below 0.95 — a filter that eats real names is worse than no filter.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Two real ones, both found in the run. (1) The signal only exists in PROSE. Atomic-note titles, headings and list-item starts are bare nouns with no determiner, so a common noun that mostly appears in headings scores 0.00 and survives the filter. Occurrences in titles, headings and list-leading positions must be excluded from the denominator, and in an atomised vault that may remove most of the evidence for short notes. (2) Background frequency is NOT the clean second signal it looks like: I checked de_50k ranks directly and names outrank common nouns — Anna 1472, Schmidt 6192, Müller 7891, Lukas 8246 all sit above Dienstag 3159, Küchentisch 27292, Betreuerin 28431. Only 'absent from the list entirely' (Freiburg) is high-precision. So use frequency as a weak additive feature or not at all; do not build a threshold on it.

**Needs first.** A0 for the measurement. The mechanism itself can be prototyped today on existing text.


### 3. [NEW] A3. EntityIndex, typed — build the substrate for entities, not for people

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Same cards and receipts the person proposals promise ('via Anna M.'), except the receipt can also be a project, an organisation or a place — whichever kind of named thing your vault actually contains.

**How.** Make the roster typed from the start: Entity {surface, type: person|project|org|place|unknown, evidence[]}, with type assigned by A1's filter plus prototype scoring plus frontmatter. Every downstream mechanism — P2's postings, P3's degree-corrected bipartite RA, P7's bridges, P8's alias merge, P4's salience — is type-agnostic and runs unchanged; only the prototype and the receipt wording change. The engineering argument is that the ONLY named-entity type this corpus can currently test is PROJECT, and it has plenty: SurfacerAI, Proofwork, Parralax, JL-Studios, LectureSyncAI, T2000 Studienarbeit, Code Garden, Easy Tikz, Hub Sidebar, Hex Code Framework, GoA. I found these in Personal/Projects.md and as unresolved link targets. They have exactly the properties the person proposals want: low degree, recurring across notes, frequently unlinked, and they bridge otherwise unrelated content. So you can build and measure the entire P1-P3-P7-P8 stack NOW, against a signal that exists, and people become one more type the day A0 delivers a corpus that has them.

**Why it is not redundant.** Same independence argument as P3 and it inherits P3's obligation to prove it: entity mentions are not wikilinks, so the channel sees pairs the RA channel over resolvedLinks cannot. But it is testable today, which P3 is not. It is also the honest answer to the shared-tags precedent: if the user links every project note every time, entity overlap will correlate with RA at ~0.9 and the whole area dies cheaply, before P1-P8 are built.

**Kill experiment.** Run A4's probe on projects first. Then the standard admission rule on the existing 465-note held-out-links protocol — which CAN return a signal for projects even though it cannot for people. Require delta >= +0.02 recall@10 over the 0.745 content+RA baseline and Spearman < 0.30 against both content and RA, measured on the same candidate pairs the 0.075 link-graph correlation came from. Mandatory ablation, exactly as P3 specified: linked-only entity mentions versus linked + unlinked. If unlinked mentions add nothing on projects, that is strong prior evidence they will add nothing on people either, and it costs one experiment instead of eight.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Projects may be too few to power the test — I counted roughly a dozen in Personal/, which is thin. And project mentions may be much more reliably wikilinked than person mentions would be (this owner clearly links projects: Personal/Projects.md is nothing but wikilinks), which would make projects a pessimistic proxy — a null result on projects would not definitively kill people. Say that out loud before running it, so a null is read as 'inconclusive, need A0' rather than 'area dead'. Retyping risk is real too: 'Transformer' is a concept, 'Proofwork' is a project, and the boundary is genuinely fuzzy for a Zettelkasten.

**Needs first.** A1 for typing. Testable on the existing lab vault without A0.


### 4. [NEW] A4. Kill-first independence probe — one afternoon, before P1-P8 exist

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. It is the experiment that decides whether to spend the next month on this area.

**How.** Do not build the roster, the linker, the SVN gate and the channel and THEN discover the correlation is 0.9, which is exactly how the shared-tags channel was lost. Build a deliberately crude proxy first: take the top ~200 recurring capitalised surfaces after A1's filter, with no disambiguation, no alias merging and no abstention — deliberately noisy — form the note-x-surface incidence matrix, score pairs with the bipartite 1/deg RA transposition, and compute Spearman rank correlation against the shipped content channel and against GraphSignals.evidence().ra on the same candidate pairs used for the 0.075 measurement. Nothing ships; it is a bench script alongside bench/v4-channels.mjs. Crucially, a crude proxy is the RIGHT instrument here: noise in the roster ATTENUATES correlation, so a high measured correlation is conclusive (the clean version will only correlate more), while a low one is merely permissive and licenses building P1 and P2 properly.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is the admission rule executed before the build instead of after. The prior researcher put this capability at P14, as a runtime feature shipped to users after the whole stack exists; the builder needs it at step zero, offline, as a bench script. Different artefact, different week, and this one can cancel the other thirteen.

**Kill experiment.** It IS the measurement. Decision rule fixed in advance: Spearman >= 0.6 against RA -> the area is the shared-tags channel again under a new name, close it and write up why. Between 0.3 and 0.6 -> proceed only on the entity generalisation (A3), where receipts have standalone navigational value even if the ranking lift is marginal. Below 0.3 -> build P1 and P2 properly. Self-validation, per the project's own recorded lesson that reimplemented harnesses measure the wrong code: the probe must call the real IndexStore and the real GraphSignals, and must reproduce content 0.664 / content+RA 0.745 on the lab vault before its correlation numbers are believed.

**Most likely reason it fails.** On the current corpus the probe will run over concepts and projects rather than people, so it answers 'is a mention-overlap channel independent here' rather than 'is a PERSON channel independent'. That is a weaker question, and a permissive result must not be over-read as clearance for the person-specific claims. The other risk is that a crude roster is SO noisy the correlation is attenuated toward zero and the probe waves through an area that would have failed cleanly — mitigate by reporting the correlation at three roster-quality tiers (all capitalised surfaces, A1-filtered, A1-filtered plus frequency floor) and requiring the trend across tiers to be flat.

**Needs first.** A1. Independent of A0 — this is the one experiment worth running before the corpus exists.


### 5. [CORRECTED] P1. PersonIndex: a roster built from the vault, no model

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible on its own. It is the list of people the plugin believes exist in your vault, built from what Obsidian already knows.

**How.** Additive weighted evidence, as proposed, with two corrections. CORRECTION 1, and it is not a quibble: source (b), 'targets of unresolvedLinks — a [[Anna]] with no note behind it is a strong person signal and is free', is measurably false on this vault. I extracted all 488 distinct wikilink targets against 464 basenames; 106 are unresolved, and every one in the sample is a concept — Aggregation, B-Tree, Bayes Theorem, Church-Turing Thesis, Computational Complexity, Cross-Entropy, Dijkstra Algorithm, Eventual Consistency, Foreign Key, GoA Plot. Weighted as 'strong', this source alone would fill the roster with the vault's entire concept vocabulary and poison P3, P6 and P7 exactly as the proposal's own risk field warns. Demote it to a candidate GENERATOR with zero standing weight: unresolved targets enter the candidate pool and must then earn their place from other evidence. CORRECTION 2: replace source (d)'s reliance on a 20k-name lexicon as primary with A1's determiner filter as primary — a name lexicon cannot cover non-Western names and will silently make the roster monocultural, whereas the determiner signal is a negative filter that does not care what culture a name comes from. Keep source (a) #person/frontmatter class, source (c) person-shaped frontmatter keys, and source (e) folder locality as-is. The normalize() reuse from src/title-index.ts is correct and verified — NFC then toLocaleLowerCase then whitespace collapse, exactly right for German sharp-s. The invalidation story (create/delete/rename plus metadataCache 'changed' for aliases) is also correct and mirrors what TitleIndex already does.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct as stated: it is the substrate, not a channel, and its value is precisely that frontmatter and unlinked mentions cover the people the RA channel over resolvedLinks cannot see. That argument survives; only the unresolvedLinks half of it does not.

**Kill experiment.** The proposed measurement cannot be run. 'Hand-label the 150 most frequent capitalised surfaces in the 465-note lab vault as person / not-person' yields, on the vault I measured, approximately zero positives — precision and recall are undefined on an empty positive class, and 'report German and English precision separately' is doubly undefined. Move it to the A0 corpus unchanged; the thresholds (kill if precision < 0.90 at recall >= 0.60, report German separately) are well chosen and should be kept verbatim. Add one cheap check runnable TODAY that the proposal omits: what fraction of roster candidates come from each evidence source, so the unresolvedLinks poisoning above is visible as a number rather than an argument.

**Most likely reason it fails.** German, as the researcher said — but A1 turns that from a blocker into a measured, solved sub-problem, at least for rejecting common nouns. The residual risk after A1 is the one my run exposed: the surviving candidate set is dominated by English technical terms, which a German-focused filter cannot touch. The real risk now is the corpus, not the language.

**Needs first.** A1 for typing; A0 for any honest measurement.


### 6. [CORRECTED] P2. Unlinked person mentions, with abstention

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The plugin notices when a note talks about someone without linking them, and says so only when it is sure. Two people sharing a first name means it says nothing.

**How.** Mechanism is right and the abstention design is the best-judged part of the original set — surface variants, longest-first alternation, one-referent-per-discourse to pin bare forms, co-mention context, margin threshold with an explicit 'ambiguous' outcome, postings stored as (note, person, salience, first-offset). Keep all of it. CORRECTION to the cost model: the proposal names the wrong bottleneck. It worries that 'the alternation regex is compiled per active note today', but I read src/title-index.ts and that is a non-issue — compiledFor(activePath) recompiles per note only because it EXCLUDES the note's own surfaces to prevent self-linking. A person alternation needs no such exclusion, so it compiles ONCE for the whole vault and a few hundred surfaces is a trivial alternation. The actual bottleneck is elsewhere and is much worse: mention extraction needs note BODIES, and IndexStore does not keep them. chunkTexts are persisted only when showSummary is on (index-store.ts line 340), so with summaries off there is no text in memory to match against. A vault-wide mention pass therefore means vault.cachedRead over every file — on 100k notes that is a full disk re-read, not a regex cost. Budget it as an incremental, idle-only, once-per-note-version pass keyed on mtime, riding the same idle machinery indexing already uses, and make it resumable.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct: it measures mentions, not links, and abstention is the precision-first rule applied to entity linking rather than to ranking.

**Kill experiment.** Sound design — 200 spans stratified by language and ambiguity, >= 0.95 attribution precision, report abstention rate and true-mention recall, German false-positive rate on capitalised common nouns as the go/no-go. Keep it verbatim, move it to the A0 corpus. Add one thing the proposal omits and that the cost correction above makes necessary: measure wall-clock for the full-vault mention pass on a synthetic 100k-note vault, on battery, and report resumability after a mid-pass quit. A correctness result that ships an eight-minute stall is not a pass.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Heavy abstention leaving the channel too sparse to matter — genuine and unchanged. Added risk from the cost correction: the full-body re-read may force showSummary on as a de facto dependency, which changes the index footprint for users who deliberately turned summaries off. Decide that explicitly rather than discovering it.

**Needs first.** P1, A1, A0


### 7. [CORRECTED] P3. The people channel: degree-corrected shared-person evidence

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Cards that appear because two notes are about the same people, with the receipt 'via Anna M.' — including when neither note links the other and neither links Anna.

**How.** The scoring transposition is right and is the cleanest idea in the set: PersonRA(s,t) = sum over shared p of 1/deg(p) is a direct transposition of the shipped RA in src/graph-signals.ts onto a different node type, and it inherits the degree penalty that makes RA work. Two corrections. CORRECTION 1, factual: the integration note says 'add WhyKind people to the union at line 272 of index-store.ts' — line 272 is correct and the union is there ('linked' | 'shared-tags' | 'co-cited' | 'semantic' | 'graph'), but the work does not stop at the type. The reason is ASSIGNED in a fixed priority chain at index-store.ts line ~3118 ('linked > shared-tags > co-cited > semantic'), and there is a rescue path at line 2652 that special-cases reason kinds by name. A new kind must be placed in that priority order deliberately — placed below shared-tags it will almost never be the displayed reason, and the receipt is the whole user-facing point. CORRECTION 2: consider dropping the SVN hypergeometric gate for v1. With a few hundred people it has very little power, it adds a null model and an FDR correction and a density calibration to tune, and the 1/deg weighting plus a hard degree cap already does most of the work of suppressing hub people. Ship the simple version, measure, and add the gate only if false positives are the measured failure.

**Why it is not redundant.** The argument is right and the proposal is admirably honest that it must be verified rather than assumed. A4 verifies it before any of this is built, which is the correction that matters most.

**Kill experiment.** As written, this measurement CANNOT return a signal. Masking 20% of wikilinks in a vault with one empty person template gives a person channel that contributes exactly zero to every pair; the delta is 0.000 by construction. Move to the A0 corpus, keep the admission thresholds verbatim (delta >= +0.02, Spearman < 0.30 against both content and RA, on the same pairs as the 0.075 measurement) and keep the mandatory linked-only versus linked+unlinked ablation, which is the single most informative experiment in the entire area. Add a methodological caution the proposal misses: held-out WIKILINK recall is a biased yardstick for a people channel, because it rewards recovering links the user actually typed, and users type links to people mainly in vaults where people are already well linked — precisely the regime where this channel is redundant with RA. Report a second, person-specific target alongside it (held-out co-mention pairs) so the channel is not judged solely on a metric that structurally favours its competitor.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The entity-oriented-ranking plateau the researcher cites is the right worry and their regime counter-argument is reasonable but unproven. The larger risk now is measurement theatre: running this on the current lab vault would produce a clean 0.000 that looks like a decisive kill and is actually a statement about the corpus. Guard against that explicitly in the write-up.

**Needs first.** A0, A4, P1, P2


### 8. [CORRECTED] P4. Person salience strip: who this note is actually about

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** A row of two or three name chips at the top of the panel: the people this note is about, not everyone it mentions.

**How.** Right, and the reuse claims check out, which is more than can be said for P6 and P13. Feature (3) 'presence in title or H1-H2 breadcrumb' is real: index-store.ts carries `heading?: string; // breadcrumb "Note > H1 > H2"` per chunk (line ~356) built by a section splitter at line ~530. Feature (5) 'spread across distinct IDEA units' is also real: `ideaOf?: number[]` per chunk (line 262), with ideas coalesced to >= MIN_IDEA_WORDS=100 and capped at MAX_IDEA_WINDOWS=8. Both are free. Keep the fixed-weight linear scorer and keep the refusal to train anything. One correction: hover-reveals-first-mention-sentence needs the sentence text, which runs into the same chunkTexts-only-when-summaries-on constraint as P2 — either take the receipt from P2's stored first-offset plus a targeted cachedRead of that one note (cheap, one file, on hover) or drop the hover.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct — a facet on the active note, not a channel, existing so P5 and P6 are one click away.

**Kill experiment.** The design is right (50 notes with >= 3 attributed people, owner labels about-them versus merely-mentioned, P@1 and P@3 against a frequency-only baseline, ship only if it beats frequency-only). The proposal's own precondition is the thing to run first, and I have already run it: people-per-note in the lab vault is 0. So this is unmeasurable here, and by its own stated rule ('measure people-per-note first; if the median is 1, skip this') it does not get built until A0 delivers a corpus with median >= 2. Keeping the cheap-baseline-wins clause is important and should not be negotiated away.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Unchanged and now quantified: in an atomised vault every mentioned person is salient, so the ranking has nothing to do. Median people-per-note is the gate, it is currently 0, and it must be >= 2 before a line of this is written.

**Needs first.** A0, P1, P2


### 9. [KEEP] P5. Person-conditioned related notes

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Click a name chip and the panel becomes 'notes related to this one, that also involve Anna'.

**How.** Keep as written. The performance claim is correct and worth stating plainly: restricting the Stage-1 shortlist to a posting list shrinks the candidate set BEFORE the quadratic-in-chunks Stage-2 BiMax, so this makes ranking cheaper on a 100k vault, not dearer. The second entry point (a command with a person picker, usable with no active note) is the right call and is the part that makes it feel like a feature rather than a filter.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct — a slice of existing ranking, so the admission rule does not apply. Its value is navigational.

**Kill experiment.** The task-based protocol is the right instinct, but 15 tasks with the owner as the only subject and the mtime-based 6-month filter as the only guard against recall-from-memory is a weak instrument, and the outcome will be dominated by whether the owner remembers writing the note. Strengthen it cheaply: pre-register the 15 tasks and the success criterion before building, randomise facet-on/facet-off order, and report per-task times rather than a mean over n=15. Keep the kill condition (must beat plain Obsidian search) exactly as stated.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Correctly identified: this is a worse backlinks pane whenever the person has a note, and the entire differentiator is unlinked mentions plus salience filtering. So P2's unlinked-mention recall is the gate — if the linked-only versus linked+unlinked ablation in P3 shows unlinked mentions add nothing, this feature loses its reason to exist at the same moment P2 does.

**Needs first.** A0, P1, P2, P4


### 10. [CORRECTED] P6. Person dossier: everything about one person, assembled and labelled

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** Open a person and get: every note they are central to in time order; who they appear with more often than chance; a few phrases describing what you write about when you write about them; and the two or three notes that mention them but do not fit the pattern.

**How.** Panels 1, 2 and 4 are right. Panel 3 is described wrongly and will not work as specified. I read src/vault-map.ts: labelClusters() is (i) module-private — only buildVaultMap is exported, (ii) fed `titles: string[]`, one short TITLE per note and never the body, and (iii) hard-capped at three SINGLE TOKENS joined with ' · ' by `if (picked.length >= 3) break`. Run it on a person's 8 notes and you are doing c-TF-IDF over roughly 8 short title strings, which is noise, and you get at most 3 bare words, not the 'three to six phrases' promised. The machinery that actually produces multi-word phrases in this codebase is the separate KEYPHRASE path (index-store.ts, MAX_PHRASE_WORDS=3, MAX_CANDIDATES=36, MMR_LAMBDA=0.6, TARGET_LABEL_WORDS 3-7) that computes summaryLabel by embedding candidate n-grams and MMR-picking. Retarget panel 3 at that path with the person's note set as the document and the rest of the vault as background. It costs one extra ONNX batch pass, not 'almost nothing' — the original's cost claim was based on the misidentification. Panel 4 (notes furthest from the person's own centroid) is the genuinely magic part, needs no new machinery, and should be built first as a standalone probe of whether the whole dossier is worth assembling.

**Why it is not redundant.** The claim 'a person's note set is a legitimate cluster for a labeller that already ships' is the load-bearing novelty and it survives the correction — it is just a different shipped labeller than the one named.

**Kill experiment.** The blind protocol is right (20 people, 3-6 phrases, owner rates apt/vague/wrong without knowing whose they are, ship at >= 70% apt and <= 10% wrong) and the boilerplate kill condition is well chosen. Keep both, move to the A0 corpus. Correct one number: the >= 8 notes minimum floor was set for c-TF-IDF over titles; against the keyphrase path the input is body text, so the floor should be set in WORDS not notes — measure the phrase-quality curve against total body words and set the floor where it flattens. Panel 4 keeps the existing rn-eval blind judged-precision harness at >= 60% surprising-or-forgotten.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The scope-creep warning is the right one and should be enforced structurally, not by discipline: the dossier must be a pure projection of the index with no store of its own, so there is nowhere for CRM features to accrete. Second risk, now sharper: retargeting panel 3 at the embedding-based keyphrase path means dossier labels cost ONNX time, so a dossier for a person with 60 notes is not free and needs the same lazy-on-demand drainer treatment summaryLabel already has (LABEL_DRAIN_BATCH=8, 250ms debounce).

**Needs first.** A0, P1, P2, P4, and the KEYPHRASE labeller — not the vault-map cluster labeller


### 11. [KEEP] P7. Person bridges: the connection that exists only through someone

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** An occasional card: these two notes share no wording and no links, but both are about Kathrin, and Kathrin appears in only four notes. Plus: you have never written about these two people together, yet their notes are entangled.

**How.** Keep both emitters as written. The design principle — define the feature by what it stays silent about, requiring centered cosine below the panel's own ~0.2 cutoff AND RA(s,t) = 0 AND a low-degree bridging person — is the strongest reasoning in the set and is the correct transposition of the shipped Surprising Connections v2 rule from wording to people. The degree cap on the bridging person is essential and the Gini diagnostic over bridging persons is exactly the right instrument to keep it honest. One note on the SVN gate, same as P3: at a few hundred people it has almost no power, so treat the gate as optional v2 and let the degree cap carry v1.

**Why it is not redundant.** Independent by construction rather than by measurement, because the emitter's precondition is that both existing channels score the pair at zero. That is a genuinely stronger argument than a correlation number and it is correct. It cannot be redundant; it can only be wrong or trivial.

**Kill experiment.** Keep verbatim: rn-eval blind judged precision, 30 emitted bridges, owner judges genuine/trivial/wrong blind to mechanism plus a separate did-you-already-know flag, ship at >= 0.80 genuine and >= 25% did-not-know, matching the bar Surprising Connections v2 cleared. Keep the Gini diagnostic. The synthetic planted-bridge vault is not merely a supplement here, it is the primary instrument, because the real vault will not have the statistical power to distinguish 'validated almost nothing' from 'validated almost everything' — and building that planted vault is the same work as option (b) in A0, so do it once and use it twice.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Unchanged and correctly stated: most low-degree shared people are coincidences, and a small vault cannot tell a working gate from a dead one. Worth adding that the failure mode is asymmetric and in the plugin's favour — a gate that validates nothing produces silence, which is the precision-first outcome the project prefers, so the dangerous direction is a gate tuned loose to make the feature 'work'. Fix the target emission density in advance and do not tune it upward after seeing results.

**Needs first.** A0, P1, P2, P3


### 12. [CORRECTED] P8. Alias merge suggestions with three-signal agreement

*moderate · high confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** An occasional one-line prompt: 'Anna M. and @anna — same person?' with the sentence each came from. One click, answered once, written into the person note's aliases so Obsidian itself benefits.

**How.** Keep the structure — three signals, merge only on two-of-three agreement, hard cannot-link on same-sentence co-occurrence, blocking on shared first or last token to keep comparisons linear. The Jaro-Winkler plus initial-expansion plus nickname-table stack is standard and correct, and writing the answer into vault frontmatter rather than plugin state is the right instinct. Two corrections. CORRECTION 1, an undeclared dependency: signal (ii) needs 'the already-stored int8 chunk vectors of the passages containing the surface', which requires a surface-to-chunk mapping the index does not have. chunkTexts exist only when showSummary is on (index-store.ts line 340), so with summaries off there is no stored text in which to locate a surface, and the signal needs either a forced dependency on summaries or a re-read plus a deterministic re-chunk. Declare it before building. CORRECTION 2, a governance point the proposal glosses: writing to `aliases:` in the user's frontmatter is a MUTATION of user notes, and the plugin is otherwise read-only apart from explicitly user-initiated link insertion. A merge confirmation is user-initiated so this is defensible, but it must be a visible, single, undoable edit, and it must never fire without a click — no auto-merge above any threshold should ever write to a file unprompted.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct: it is correctness infrastructure, not a channel. With aliases unresolved, P3's degree counts are wrong, P6's dossiers split in half and P7 emits garbage. The observation that one user click beats any amount of unsupervised clustering is the right instinct for a plugin with no training data.

**Kill experiment.** Keep as written: 60 pairs hand-labelled with a third hard cases, precision >= 0.98 for auto-merge, >= 0.70 acceptance on prompted merges. The three-way ablation on the real store is exactly right and matches this project's recorded doctrine that reimplemented harnesses measure the wrong code. Given CORRECTION 1, run the ablation on signal (ii) FIRST and separately — if the distributional signal does not pay, the summaries dependency disappears with it and the feature gets simpler and cheaper.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The researcher's own doubt about the distributional signal is well founded, and the ablation ordering above turns that doubt into the cheapest experiment rather than the most expensive. Prompt fatigue is real and the one-per-session cap is right. The unflagged risk is the write path: an auto-merge that is wrong is invisible, corrupts every downstream count, AND has now edited the user's note — which is why precision >= 0.98 should gate the PROMPT threshold too, not only the auto-merge threshold.

**Needs first.** P1, A0 for the labelled pairs


### 13. [DEAD] P9. Opt-in compact NER pass (GLiNER-small int8)

*expensive · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Would have been an optional extra download: 'find people I never linked'.

**How.** Dead. Not deferred, not gated behind a setting — out of scope by the project's own constraint list, and the cheap alternatives now have measured support that this does not.

**Why it is not redundant.** It adds no channel and its only value is roster recall, which is exactly why it cannot justify its cost when a 30-line determiner counter delivers a validated slice of the same benefit.

**Kill experiment.** Not worth designing. If anyone revisits this, note that the 183.4 MB int8 figure came from the prior researcher and I did NOT verify it (no network access was used in this pass) — it should be checked before it is quoted again.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead for four reasons, any one sufficient. (1) It violates hard constraint 3, which permits exactly one heavy model — 'the ONLY heavy model is a small multilingual sentence-embedding model'. A second 183 MB ONNX model is not a cost trade-off inside the constraints, it is outside them, and the proposal's own hedge that 'the constraint list tolerates one model download, not casually two' concedes this. (2) The runtime shape is wrong: GLiNER is a span-scoring transformer pass over document text, far heavier per note than the sentence encoder's per-chunk pass, and the sequential load-unload-reload dance proposed to stay under the 4 GiB WASM cap means thrashing 183 MB in and out of the heap during idle indexing on a laptop on battery. (3) The backbone is English-centric DeBERTa-v3-small, so it is weakest exactly where the problem is — German — and multilingual variants are larger, not smaller. (4) General NER's notion of 'person' includes every book author and historical figure quoted in a Zettelkasten, which is the opposite of what a personal person-graph wants; on the measured lab vault, which is a CS textbook Zettelkasten, this would return Dijkstra, Floyd, Warshall, Bayes, Jacobi and Church as people. Confidence set to speculative per the review rule.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 14. [CORRECTED] P10. Prototype-vector person typing — demoted to fallback

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible. A way to decide 'Kathrin' is a person and 'Küchentisch' is not, without a new model.

**How.** Mechanism unchanged — one prototype vector per type from a few label strings plus the mean of confirmed person-note vectors (the ProtoLE data-driven half), embed the candidate inside a short window from its real occurrence, subtract the corpus centroid with the existing mean-centering, assign only on a margin over the runner-up. Demoted from primary to fallback for two reasons. First, A1's determiner filter now has measured support on the actual German text of this vault and costs zero embeddings, whereas this costs one embed per candidate surface; the cheap thing should be tried first and this should only be built for the gap A1 measurably leaves — which, on my run, is separating proper names from English technical vocabulary, NOT the German common-noun problem this was designed for. Second, the researcher's own stated failure mode is sharper than they credit: the model is paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2, trained on paraphrase pairs at SENTENCE granularity, and mean-centering (which index-store.ts applies to every chunk row via centerChunksInPlace) removes the dominant shared direction — plausibly including much of whatever 'proper-noun-ness' the prototypes would key on. Retarget the prototypes accordingly: PERSON versus ORG/PROJECT/CONCEPT is the discrimination worth attempting, since A1 already handles PERSON versus German common noun.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is a type classifier, not a channel, and it is the zero-download comparator that made P9 unjustifiable. That role is now filled more cheaply by A1, so this is the second fallback rather than the first.

**Kill experiment.** Keep the three-way comparison design but change the systems compared, since one of them is now dead: (a) A1 determiner filter alone, (b) A1 plus this prototype scorer, (c) A1 plus a name lexicon. Drop GLiNER from the comparison entirely. 300 candidate surfaces from the A0 corpus, half German, hand-typed, macro-F1, German reported separately. Ship the cheapest system within 0.05 macro-F1 of the best — that clause is the best thing in the original proposal and should be kept verbatim. Add a pre-check that costs an hour and can kill it before the labelling: compute prototype margins on 50 known surfaces and check the margin distribution is bimodal rather than flat. If it is flat, stop.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The honest failure mode the researcher named — margins too flat to threshold after mean-centering — is the most likely outcome, and it is now compounded by the fact that A1 already solves the sub-problem this was primarily aimed at. Downgraded to speculative because its expected marginal value over A1 is small and unproven, not because it is unsound. It stays on the list only because the one-hour margin pre-check is genuinely cheap.

**Needs first.** A1 first. Only build if A1 leaves a measured gap.


### 15. [DEAD] P11. Person de-crowding of the vector space

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Would have been invisible: notes stop looking similar merely because they involve the same person.

**How.** Dead as a shipped feature. What survives is a one-hour diagnostic that must run BEFORE anyone writes subtraction code, and that I would run as follows: for each person above a degree threshold, compute the first principal component of their notes' centered chunk vectors by the power iteration already in vault-map.ts pca2(), and compare the variance it explains against the variance explained by the first PC of a random note set of the same size. If person groups show no more shared direction than random groups, there is nothing to subtract and the feature does not exist. That measurement is worth an hour; the feature is not worth a week.

**Why it is not redundant.** It modifies the content channel rather than adding one, so the admission rule binds harder — it must improve content-only held-out recall unaided. That framing was correct and is the reason it fails: it sets a bar the effect almost certainly cannot clear.

**Kill experiment.** The proposed measurement is unrunnable on the current corpus for the same reason as P3 — no people, so no person groups, so no directions to subtract and no curve to sweep. The alpha sweep over {0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0} and the kill-if-content-recall-drops-on-either-model rule were well designed and should be reused verbatim IF the diagnostic above ever shows a real shared direction.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead for three reasons. (1) Almost certainly already absorbed: template de-crowding groups notes by SHARED HEADINGS (index-store.ts subtractTemplateDirections, MIN_TEMPLATE_GROUP=3, notes sharing >= 2 cached headings), and meeting or 1:1 notes about the same person are exactly the notes that share headings — so the person direction and the template direction are largely the same direction, already subtracted. The researcher flagged this and it is the correct read. (2) It actively fights P3: subtracting the person direction from the content channel while simultaneously adding a person channel is double-counting in opposite directions, and the joint measurement needed to disentangle them costs more than either feature is worth. (3) It is unmeasurable on the lab vault, which has zero people, so there is no way to find out cheaply. Confidence set to speculative per the review rule. Note the shipped template de-crowding is measured and works (panel crowding 8.17 -> 3.35 of 10 end-to-end through rank()); this is not a criticism of that mechanism, only of applying it to a grouping that does not exist here.

**Needs first.** None — run the one-hour diagnostic, expect a null, move on.


### 16. [DEAD] P12. Person-time: who you were around, and when

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Would have been a card reading 'same weeks as Anna and Tomás, March 2024'.

**How.** Dead on its own stated precondition, and I measured it rather than predicting it. The proposal says: 'what fraction of the 465-note vault has a trustworthy date (filename or frontmatter, not ctime)? Abandon if under 60%.' Measured on /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/lab/vault/real: 18 of 465 filenames are date-shaped (3.9%) and ZERO notes carry a date, created, datum or erstellt frontmatter key — the complete frontmatter key inventory for the entire vault is tags (462), aliases (428), and one each of words, name, chapter and book. So trustworthy date coverage is 3.9% against a 60% bar, short by a factor of fifteen. The six genuine daily notes that do exist (2026-05-02 through 2026-06-18) live outside real/ and number six. ctime is not a fallback here, per the recorded constraint that iCloud sync rewrites it.

**Why it is not redundant.** Time genuinely is orthogonal to both existing channels, and the proposal's own sharpest line is the one that condemns it: 'the real risk is that it is orthogonal AND uninformative — orthogonal noise is still noise, and the correlation half of the admission rule would pass while recall falls.' That is exactly what a 3.9%-coverage temporal channel would be.

**Kill experiment.** No measurement to run. The precondition check WAS the measurement, it has now been executed, and it failed by 15x. The permutation-test design against week-permuted null histograms was methodologically the most careful thing in the original set and should be salvaged for reuse if a daily-notes or journal vault ever becomes the target corpus — it is the right way to handle bursty histograms and it should not be thrown away with the proposal.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead because the corpus has no time in it. The deeper and more durable reason, which will not change even with a better corpus from A0: this is an ATOMISED PERMANENT-NOTE vault, and atomic concept notes are written whenever, about whatever — 'Eigenwerte' has no date because it has no occasion. The Uitvlugt & Healey temporal-contiguity warrant is about episodic memory encoding, and a Zettelkasten of mathematical concepts is close to the least episodic corpus imaginable. This works beautifully for a daily-notes or journal vault and is inapplicable to this one. Confidence set to speculative per the review rule.

**Needs first.** None — do not build for this vault.


### 17. [CORRECTED] P13. Role sketches without relation extraction

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Under a person's name: 'thesis, Betreuerin, Freiburg, Dienstags' — a few words saying what this person is to you, each clickable to the notes it came from.

**How.** The shape is right and the refusal to reach for a dependency parser is correct — window plus c-TF-IDF genuinely is the cheap 80% and it cannot hallucinate, because every phrase is a literal token with a note list behind it. Corrections. FIRST, the same misattribution as P6: this does not reuse vault-map.ts's labeller, which is title-only, module-private and capped at three single tokens; it needs a NEW c-TF-IDF over ±6-token mention windows with other people's windows as background. That is maybe 60 lines, but it is new code, not reuse, and the proposal's cost estimate should say so. SECOND, an undeclared dependency shared with P2: mention windows require note BODIES, which the index does not retain unless showSummary is on — so this rides on whatever re-read strategy P2 settles. THIRD, on German compounding, A1's frequency lists give a cheaper mitigation than a compound splitter: rather than splitting 'Masterarbeitsbetreuerin', match candidate tokens by longest-common-substring against other candidates above a length floor, so the compound and its head co-cluster without any morphological analysis. Crude, but it is a day rather than a project.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct — a label, not a channel, existing to make P6 legible at a glance.

**Kill experiment.** Keep as written (20 people, owner rates each phrase apt/vague/wrong blind, >= 70% apt and <= 10% wrong, hard kill if top phrases are boilerplate or variants of the person's own name). Move to the A0 corpus. Add the German-coverage pre-check the proposal itself calls for and make it a gate rather than a note: what fraction of people clear the >= 3-distinct-notes floor for at least three tokens, reported separately for German and English. If German coverage is under half of English, the compounding mitigation above has not worked and this should be English-only or not at all.

**Most likely reason it fails.** German compounding fragmenting counts below the three-note floor is the right primary risk and the mitigation above is unproven. The larger risk is that this is a decoration on top of P6, which is itself downstream of P1, P2 and P4 and of a corpus that does not exist — so it sits five dependencies deep and should be the last thing built in the area, if at all. Downgraded to speculative on those grounds rather than on the mechanism, which is sound.

**Needs first.** A0, P1, P2, plus a NEW window-level c-TF-IDF — not the vault-map labeller


### 18. [CORRECTED — PROMOTE] P14. Self-gating: the plugin runs the admission rule on the user's own vault

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** In settings: 'People channel: ON — measured +0.04 link recall on your vault', or 'OFF — your people are already fully linked, so it adds nothing here.' The plugin tells you why a feature is or is not running for you.

**How.** The best proposal in the set and the only one whose value does not depend on the person area succeeding — promote it and widen its scope. Correction 1, scope: build it as a GENERAL per-vault channel gate from the start, not as a people-channel gate that is later generalised. The measured fact that motivates it is not about people at all; it is that the tags channel correlated 0.916 with the graph channel IN THIS VAULT, which is a property of this owner's linking habits, not a universal constant. Every channel the project ever adds wants this. Correction 2, cost: a full held-out-links re-rank in the worker is not affordable on a 100k-note vault — it re-ranks thousands of notes through the full funnel including Stage-2 BiMax. Bound it explicitly: sample notes rather than sweeping all above ~10k, cap total wall-clock, run only on explicit user request or on a long idle, never on battery, and make it resumable. Correction 3, fallback: a vault with too few wikilinks has no held-out set, and the proposal correctly says default OFF-with-explanation. Add the cheaper half-measure it misses — correlation-only gating needs NO ground truth at all (sample 2000 candidate pairs, correlate the new channel against existing ones, disable above 0.5), so a link-poor vault can still get the redundancy half of the admission rule even when the recall half is unmeasurable.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is the mechanism that makes everything else safe to ship, and it converts the project's hardest-won lesson — that channel usefulness is vault-dependent — from a lab finding into a runtime property. It is also the only proposal here that is worth building even if A0 shows this owner has no people.

**Kill experiment.** It IS the measurement, and the self-validation gate is exactly right: the in-plugin harness must reproduce the lab numbers on the lab vault within ±0.01 (content 0.664, content+RA 0.745) before any number it reports is believed. Hold that line hard — this project has already recorded the failure mode where reimplemented harnesses measured something other than the real IndexStore, so the harness must drive the real IndexStore and the real GraphSignals, not a reimplementation. Add one gate the proposal omits: run the harness twice on an unchanged vault and require the two results to agree within ±0.005, because a gate that flips a channel on and off between runs is worse than no gate.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Runtime cost and battery on large vaults, correctly identified and addressed by the sampling and budget corrections above. The UX risk is real and the researcher's own answer is right — hide the numbers behind a details toggle and lead with the plain-language verdict. One risk they do not name: a self-gate that silently disables a channel makes support and bug reports much harder, because two users on the same version now have different rankings for reasons neither can see. Whatever the gate decides must be visible in settings and in whatever diagnostics output exists.

**Needs first.** The existing bench harnesses. Buildable NOW against the shipped RA and content channels, without waiting on A0 or on any person code — and that is the argument for building it first.


## Places, geography and spatial structure


<details><summary>15 techniques surveyed</summary>

- **Resource Allocation index** (established, 2009) — Scores a node pair by shared neighbours weighted 1/deg(x). Already the shipped scoring channel (src/graph-signals.ts); every new place score in this area is deliberately shaped to mirror it, and its 1/deg penalty is the precedent for the df-discount.  
  <sub>Zhou, Lu, Zhang, 'Predicting missing links via local information', European Physical Journal B 71:623-630, 2009</sub>
- **Linear probe for spatial structure in text embeddings** (recent-but-replicated, 2024) — Shows text-model activations carry linearly decodable spatial (and temporal) coordinates. This is the single biggest threat to the whole area: if a 384-dim note vector already predicts coordinates, every place channel here will correlate with content and be cut. It is why proposal 9's ridge diagnostic must run before anything else is built.  
  <sub>Gurnee & Tegmark, 'Language Models Represent Space and Time', ICLR 2024, arXiv:2310.02207. NOTE: cited from prior knowledge; the session's web-search budget was exhausted so I could not re-verify the venue/id.</sub>
- **Geographic word profiles (gazetteer-independent toponym resolution)** (established, 2015) — Derives a term's geographic identity from the distribution of contexts it appears in rather than from a dictionary lookup. The basis of proposal 2's locative log-odds.  
  <sub>DeLozier, Baldridge, London, 'Gazetteer-Independent Toponym Resolution Using Geographic Word Profiles', AAAI 2015. NOTE: cited from prior knowledge, not re-verified this session.</sub>
- **Multi-scale spherical location encoding (sphereC / Sphere2Vec)** (recent-but-replicated, 2023) — PE(x) = [sin(phi), cos(phi)cos(lam), cos(phi)sin(lam)] gives a unit 3-vector whose dot product equals cos(great-circle distance / R) exactly, with a multi-scale stack for resolution. Correct maths; I argue below it is over-engineered for personal-vault coordinate distributions.  
  <sub>Mai et al., 'Sphere2Vec: A General-Purpose Location Representation Learning over a Spherical Surface for Large-Scale Geospatial Predictions', ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing, 2023, arXiv:2306.17624. NOTE: cited from prior knowledge, not re-verified this session.</sub>
- **Spatial co-location participation index** (established, 2001) — PI(p,c) = min over features of the fraction of that feature's instances participating in the pattern; anti-monotone, so Apriori pruning extends it to k-tuples cheaply. The scoring core of proposal 14.  
  <sub>Shekhar & Huang, 'Discovering Spatial Co-location Patterns: A Summary of Results', SSTD 2001. NOTE: cited from prior knowledge, not re-verified this session.</sub>
- **Training-free entity salience features** (established, 2014) — First-mention position, mention count and title/headline presence dominate learned salience models. Gives proposal 5 a salience term with no training data, which matters because a single user cannot supply labels.  
  <sub>Dunietz & Gillick, 'A New Entity Salience Task with Millions of Training Examples', EACL 2014. NOTE: cited from prior knowledge, not re-verified this session.</sub>
- **Term subsumption for concept hierarchy induction** (established, 1999) — Y subsumes X when P(Y|X) is high and P(X|Y) is low over documents.
