The basis of proposal 4's text-derived containment DAG — which I mark dead below, on small-sample and redundancy grounds, not because the technique is wrong.  
  <sub>Sanderson & Croft, 'Deriving concept hierarchies from text', SIGIR 1999</sub>
- **Wu-Palmer taxonomic similarity** (established, 1994) — 2*depth(LCA)/(depth(a)+depth(b)) — turns exact hierarchy match into graded partial credit. Used in proposals 4/5, and (my addition 16) applies unchanged to the folder tree.  
  <sub>Wu & Palmer, 'Verb semantics and lexical selection', ACL 1994</sub>
- **Optimal 1-D segmentation by dynamic programming** (established, 2005) — Exact O(n^2) partition of an ordered sequence minimising within-segment cost plus a per-segment penalty. The episode segmenter in proposal 8; trivial at any vault size.  
  <sub>Jackson et al., 'An algorithm for optimal partitioning of data on an interval', IEEE Signal Processing Letters 12(2), 2005</sub>
- **Wilson score interval** (established, 1927) — Lower bound on a binomial rate that does not let a rare candidate win by luck. The right admission gate for proposal 2's locative rate; standard and two lines of code.  
  <sub>Wilson, 'Probable inference, the law of succession, and statistical inference', JASA 1927</sub>
- **Benjamini-Hochberg FDR control** (established, 1995) — Controls false discovery rate across many tested pairs. Necessary but not sufficient for proposal 14 — the block-permutation null over episodes is the part that actually matters, because notes from one trip are not independent samples.  
  <sub>Benjamini & Hochberg, JRSS-B 57(1), 1995</sub>
- **Geohash (prefix-as-parent spatial encoding)** (established, 2008) — Base32 interleaved-bit encoding where a prefix IS the containing cell, so parent/child needs no computation and no library. Worth storing as a string; the sorted-array index built on top of it is not (proposal 12).  
  <sub>Niemeyer, 2008, public-domain specification (geohash.org)</sub>
- **Environmental context-dependent memory** (established, 2001) — Meta-analytic support that reinstating encoding context aids recall, with the benefit concentrated on weak memories. This is the honest citation for proposals 7 and 17. CAVEAT: the researcher's stronger claim — that spatial cues specifically beat temporal, thematic, person and object cues — I could not verify and would not put in a design doc.  
  <sub>Smith & Vela, 'Environmental context-dependent memory: a review and meta-analysis', Psychonomic Bulletin & Review 8(2), 2001</sub>
- **DBSCAN density clustering** (established, 1996) — Groups coordinates into 'personal places' at eps ~150m without a preset cluster count. Fine, though dense geohash-7 cells get the same result with no code.  
  <sub>Ester, Kriegel, Sander, Xu, KDD 1996</sub>
- **GeoNames admin-1 / country tables** (established, 2026) — CC-BY 4.0 toponym data. I argue below for the ~250-country + ~4000-region slice (well under 100 KB) rather than cities15000, because that is the granularity where an external list beats the vault's own text.  
  <sub>geonames.org export tables, CC-BY 4.0</sub>

</details>


### 1. 1. CORRECTED — prevalence probe first, and do NOT build a synthetic place corpus

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing user-facing. This is the go/no-go for the entire area.

**How.** I re-ran the probe on /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/lab/vault and confirm the researcher's numbers, plus three they missed. 528 notes; ZERO image attachments of any kind (the only 14 non-.md files are .obsidian config and the plugin's own ort/*.wasm); zero location:/geo:; 24 date-stamped filenames, all under Daily/, and only 2 notes with an ISO date in the body. The naive detector reproduces exactly: Bayes 14, Fermat 12, Taylor 9, Richtung 7, Hand 5, Green 5, Python 4, Kapitel 4 — zero true toponyms in the top 30. The thing they missed is decisive: lab/corpus-manifest.json declares paths = 124 authentic notes and answer_paths = the 465-note real/ tree = 'authentic + user-directed generated growth'. The lab corpus is already ~76% generated. Building a further 300-800 note synthetic travel journal on top of that means measuring a place detector against a corpus in which a generator decided where the places are, and resting the whole area's justification on data written to justify it. That is circular; do not build it. Replace with two things. (a) bench/place-prevalence.mjs — a fifteen-minute script run over the owner's real vault and any other real vaults obtainable, counting exactly four quantities: notes with location:/locations: frontmatter, inline geo: URIs, referenced image attachments carrying a GPS IFD, and distinct proper nouns appearing in multi-word locative cues. That count is the go/no-go and costs nothing. (b) Keep the negative control, which is the genuinely valuable half: bench/place-null.mjs asserting the shipped detector admits candidates on the maths vault at the shipping threshold. Correct their bar: they propose '<=3 candidates', but the naive detector already produces 30+ with zero true positives, so the assertion must be ZERO, not three.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. It is the gate that decides whether anything below is buildable, and it swaps an expensive, self-confirming corpus build for a cheap count.

**Kill experiment.** This is the measurement. Kill condition for the WHOLE area: if the prevalence probe returns fewer than roughly 30 place-bearing notes across every real vault available, drop proposals 2-15 entirely and keep only additions 16-19, which need no geography at all.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The probe comes back near-zero and the owner wants the area anyway. Then the honest answer is that this is a feature for a vault they do not have, and it should be built when they have one — not underwritten by a generated corpus.

**Needs first.** None. Must precede everything else in this area.


### 2. 2. CORRECTED — locative-CUE lexicon, not locative-preposition (bare 'nach' is unusable)

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** The plugin learns which words in your vault are places, from how you write about them. No download, no dictionary, works in any language you have written a few sentences of.

**How.** The log-odds plus Wilson-lower-bound machinery is sound and I would build it. But their preposition list is broken on this vault in a way the log-odds cannot repair, and I measured it. In German mathematical prose 'nach X' overwhelmingly means 'according to X' or 'by X's theorem': the top locative-slot hits in the probe are Bayes, Fermat, Taylor, Green, Binet, Euler, Tschebyscheff, Sylvester — surnames, every one, and none of them a person being spoken to. Their proposed person/place discriminator keys on speech verbs ('sagte', 'telefoniert'); 'nach Fermat' has no speech verb anywhere near it, so the discriminator contributes zero and the surname survives as a 'place'. The fix is to drop bare prepositions and require MULTI-WORD LOCATIVE CUES with an unambiguous motion or residence reading — DE: unterwegs nach | angekommen in | zurueck in | gefahren nach | geflogen nach | wohne in | uebernachtet in | Tage in; EN: arrived in | flew to | drove to | stayed in | back in | days in | based in. Precision over recall, which is the house rule. Candidate generation additionally from metadataCache.unresolvedLinks — verified NOT used anywhere in src/ today, so that is ~20 lines of new plumbing, not free — and from note titles. Every admitted surface must then be normalised and registered through the existing TitleIndex (src/title-index.ts), not a parallel map: it already does NFC plus locale-aware German lowercasing, a MIN_SURFACE_CHARS=3 precision guard, and ambiguity detection, and a second copy is how the two silently diverge.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is a detector feeding 5, 7, 13 and 19, not a ranking channel. Its independence claim is against the alternative detector — capitalisation plus gazetteer lookup — which the probe shows is unusable in German (every noun is capitalised) and blind to vault-private places.

**Kill experiment.** Keep their precision-recall curve with the operating point pinned at >=0.90 precision, but run it on whatever real place-bearing vault the prevalence probe finds; if none exists, this is unmeasurable and must not ship on faith. Mandatory negative control on the maths vault: zero admissions at the shipping threshold. Kill below 0.80 precision at any usable recall on real (non-generated) notes.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Their organisation/venue polysemy risk stands ('at Google', 'in Python'). Added on top: multi-word cues are an order of magnitude rarer than bare prepositions, so recall may collapse to near-zero even on a genuine travel journal. That is the price of precision-first here and it may simply be too high — in which case the detector is correct and useless, which is a real outcome.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1 (a real vault to set the threshold on).


### 3. 3. CORRECTED — ship the frontmatter/geo: parsers; gate the EXIF path behind an opt-in

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** If any of your notes have coordinates — Map View frontmatter, a pasted geo: link, or (opt-in) a photo you dropped in — the plugin knows where those notes are, with no setup.

**How.** The frontmatter and geo: parsers are about 80 lines, carry no risk, and should ship. The EXIF half needs three corrections they missed. (a) 'read only the first ~64 KB of each file' is not available to this plugin: it reads through app.vault.adapter.readBinary (index-store.ts:1382, main.ts:553) and Obsidian's adapter has no range read — it returns the WHOLE file as an ArrayBuffer. A 6 MB JPEG is 6 MB resident. At 5k photos that is a real 4 GiB-heap concern unless processing is strictly serial with one buffer alive at a time and an explicit per-idle-slice cap. (b) The owner's active vault is iCloud-backed (shell access is TCC-blocked), so touching every attachment forces materialisation of evicted files. That must sit behind an explicit opt-in toggle with a 'this will download your photos' warning, not silently inside the idle indexer. (c) Node fs is unavailable; everything goes through the adapter. Storage is a per-note set of {lat, lon, source, precision, sectionId}, ~40 bytes per anchor, appended to the existing index.json/index.bin pair (index-store.ts:1342-1520) — which does change the persisted shape and needs a format-version bump, so their 'no index format change' framing understates it. Fold in the surviving half of proposal 4 here: store a 9-char geohash alongside each anchor, so containment is prefix equality and the hierarchy costs nothing.

**Why it is not redundant.** Coordinates are ground truth no amount of text similarity produces. Two notes written 40 m apart can share zero vocabulary and zero links. Highest-precision place signal available, and it costs only parsing.

**Kill experiment.** Correct the kill condition: there is no test data — the lab vault has literally zero images — so the number that decides this is proposal 1's prevalence probe on real vaults, not a corpus coverage figure. Once such photos exist, round-trip 50 known-coordinate images and assert <10 m error. Kill the EXIF half if real-vault photo coverage is below ~5%.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Coverage is the whole risk and the probe says it is currently zero. EXIF GPS is stripped by most phone-to-cloud paths, so even a photo-heavy vault may be geo-blank. And the iCloud materialisation cost is paid by the user in bandwidth and disk for a feature that may then find nothing — which is why it cannot be on by default.

**Needs first.** None for the parsers; proposal 1 decides whether the EXIF half is worth writing.


### 4. 4. DEAD as a standalone — geohash parenting folded into 3; text-induced subsumption killed

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Would have inferred that Alfama is in Lisbon is in Portugal from your own notes.

**How.** Split verdict. The coordinate half is correct and nearly free — a 9-char geohash makes containment prefix equality — so it moves into proposal 3 and stops being its own item. The text-derived subsumption DAG is killed. It is not that Sanderson-Croft is wrong; it is that the inputs do not exist and the output would be redundant. Naming the cluster with the existing c-TF-IDF labeller is a nice touch and survives inside proposal 3.

**Why it is not redundant.** The geohash half is not independent of anything — it is a storage encoding. The text half claimed independence it cannot have (see risk).

**Kill experiment.** For the surviving geohash half, no separate measurement: it is a key, not a signal, and is exercised by proposals 7 and 18. The text half needed hand-labelled containment precision >=0.85 on 60 induced pairs, which cannot be produced without proposal 1's corpus.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD, three reasons. (i) The subsumption test needs support >=4 notes per pair; on a 500-note personal vault with maybe 30 places the cells are single digits, and the researcher already concedes it will confidently invert relations. (ii) More decisively, a containment DAG induced from note co-occurrence is a function of the same co-occurrence structure the link-graph channel already exploits, so Wu-Palmer scores over it will correlate with RA — this is the shared-tags failure (0.916 correlation, cut) wearing a hat. (iii) A wrong containment is silent and uncheckable by the user, which breaks the rule that every card carries a receipt verifiable in two seconds. 'Berlin contains Germany' would then corrupt every downstream score with no way for anyone to notice.

**Needs first.** n/a


### 5. 5. CORRECTED — the unlinked-mention place channel, with a fire-rate gate the z-norm demands

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Notes that name the same place rank higher for each other, with the receipt 'both mention Lisbon' — and the plugin does not double-count places you already wikilinked.

**How.** The 'unlinked mentions only' restriction is the right instinct and I would keep it exactly: a [[Lisbon]] IS an edge in resolvedLinks, so a naive shared-place channel would be a strict subgraph of the graph channel and would be cut for the reason shared tags were. The df-discounted score shaped like RA is also right. Two mechanism errors to fix. (i) They say 'z-normalised across the candidate pool and summed into the ORDER, exactly as RA is'. Look at what zNormaliser actually does (bottom of src/graph-signals.ts): it returns () => 0 when sd < 1e-9, so an ALL-zero column is safely inert. But a column that is zero on 92% of the pool and nonzero on 8% has a healthy standard deviation, and those 8% get z-scores near +3 and dominate the fused order. That is not graceful degradation — it is a channel that fires rarely and violently, and it is precisely the coverage scenario they predict. Correction: gate the channel on a minimum non-zero fraction of the candidate pool, or apply it as a bounded additive bonus rather than a z-score, and decide which BY MEASUREMENT before judging the channel — otherwise the recall number measures the artefact. (ii) The channel must respect noteAreas/areaMatch (index-store.ts:2673-2690) and the HUB_DEGREE=40 convention, or an activated isolated tag namespace leaks through the place channel exactly the way it would have leaked through the graph channel.

**Why it is not redundant.** By construction it excludes wikilinked mentions, so it cannot re-derive the link graph the way shared tags did. Partial redundancy with content is still expected (Gurnee & Tegmark), which is what proposal 9's diagnostic exists to quantify first.

**Kill experiment.** The existing admission rule: rank correlation against content and against RA (bar: the lexical channel's 0.30, which was cut), plus delta held-out link recall@10 for content+graph vs content+graph+place. Report coverage — the fraction of held-out pairs where both notes have a detected place — alongside, and report the fire-rate gate's chosen threshold. Kill on correlation >0.30 with either channel, or delta recall <=0, or fire rate below the gate.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Coverage remains the whole story, and after killing the synthetic corpus there is no protocol on which to run this until a real place-bearing vault exists. Their second risk is also sharp and stands: the mentions that survive the 'not a wikilink' filter are exactly the ones the user did not consider important enough to link, i.e. the low-salience tail.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1, 2, 3. Do not start before proposal 9's ridge diagnostic reports.


### 6. 6. CORRECTED — ship the one-line decay kernel; build sphereC only if it wins

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Notes written near each other in the world rank closer.

**How.** Two mechanism errors. (i) 'Append a small side-vector and reuse the cosine kernel already written' does not work. The shipped cosine (src/vector-math.ts) runs over the note mean of dimension dims (384 for MiniLM); appending 12 geo dims makes geography 12/396 of the norm and it is drowned by the text before it can say anything. It must be a separate channel with its own dot product and its own z-normalisation — more code than 'append', and subject to the same fire-rate problem described in proposal 5. (ii) The multi-scale argument is a continental-benchmark argument. On a personal vault the coordinate set is a handful of tight clusters plus one or two trips; geodesic distance is close to two-state. So invert their build order: ship the one-line exp(-d/tau) kernel FIRST, with tau set from the vault's median nearest-other-note coordinate distance (their auto-scaling idea, which is good and survives), measure it, and build the 12-dim sphereC encoding only if the decay kernel wins AND its residual is demonstrably scale-limited. Their own kill condition already says this; I am making it the default path rather than the fallback, because the cheap version is a day and the expensive version is a week.

**Why it is not redundant.** Content similarity has no access to metres, and this disagrees with proposal 5 exactly where it matters — the same city named differently, or two unnamed coordinates. That argument is sound; it is the implementation that needed correcting.

**Kill experiment.** Same admission test, restricted to the sub-pool where both notes have real (non-inferred) coordinates, with that pool size reported prominently. Ablate S=1 (decay kernel) vs S=4 vs S=6 only if the decay kernel clears the bar at all. Kill if the decay kernel fails; record it as a negative result and do not build sphereC.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Coverage, again, inherited from proposal 3. Plus the conceptual risk they name honestly: on a personal vault geodesic distance may be effectively binary (home vs away), in which case 12 dimensions is 11 too many and a two-state flag is the truthful version.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3. Explicitly NOT proposal 10 — inferred coordinates must never enter a ranking channel (see proposal 10).


### 7. 7. CORRECTED — 'You were there too', ranked the way Surprising Connections already ranks

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** A small section under the related notes: 'Also written in Lisbon — 3 notes, Nov 2023', listing notes you would never have thought to look for, each with the place as its receipt.

**How.** This is the best idea in the set and I want to build it. One mechanism error, and the codebase already contains the fix. They rank members by INVERSE content similarity to the active note. surprisingConnections (index-store.ts:2829-2900) does not do that: it uses non-obviousness as a GATE — excluded if either note is in the other's content top-10, checked in BOTH directions — plus a CONTENT_FLOOR of 0.05 so a pair with no topical thread at all is dropped, and then ranks by RA. Copy that shape exactly: gate on mutual-top-10 exclusion, keep a content floor, and rank by recency or place salience. Anti-similarity ranking with no floor selects the single most unrelated note in the group, which is exactly how a serendipity feature becomes a noise feature — they flagged that risk themselves without noticing the shipped answer to it. Keep their three hard gates (Wilson lower bound, df below a hub cutoff mirroring HUB_DEGREE=40, render nothing below two qualifying notes), and add reuse of noteAreas/areaMatch so an isolated area cannot leak into the section.

**Why it is not redundant.** Defined as the complement of the similarity list rather than a re-ranking of it, and travel/journal notes are the vault's orphan population so it is not the graph channel either. Verification is instant and physical: 'yes, I was there' takes under two seconds and needs no reading.

**Kill experiment.** Not link prediction. Blind judged precision (P@5) plus the 'surprising' rate on the Surprising Connections v2 protocol, against its own bar of P@5 1.00 and 36% surprising. Third and decisive: FIRING RATE, the fraction of notes for which the section renders at all. Kill below P@5 0.7 or below ~15% firing rate on a real journal vault.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Firing rate on an ordinary knowledge vault is near zero, so this must be self-disabling rather than a setting the user has to find. And if the hub gate has to be tightened until nothing shows, the feature is dead — note that addition 17 delivers the same product shape at ~100% coverage without needing geography, and is the version I would build first.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1, 2, 3.


### 8. 8. CORRECTED — build episodes on TIME plus content drift first; place is an optional extra term

*moderate · high confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** The plugin recognises that eight notes belong to one stretch of work and says so: 'same stretch: 8-19 Nov 2023'. You never tagged anything.

**How.** The DP segmentation is right and cheap — O(n^2) in dated notes, trivial at any vault size, one parameter instead of T-DBSCAN's three. But the dependency chain is backwards, and this is the biggest re-scope in the list. Their version needs place AND date, the intersection of two scarce signals, and place is the scarce one: the probe finds 24 date-stamped notes and zero places. Invert it. Segment on TIME plus CONTENT DRIFT alone: segment cost = within-segment dispersion of the centered note means (already stored as centeredMeans and already computed), plus a per-segment penalty lambda, plus infinite cost across a date gap greater than G days. That needs zero place detection, works on any daily-note vault at full coverage, and is measurable on the existing lab vault today. Place, where a vault has it, becomes one additional term in the segment cost rather than a prerequisite. Reframed this way it stops being a place feature — which is correct: hand the segmenter to the TIME area and let the place area consume it. Each episode still gets a name from the existing c-TF-IDF labeller and still becomes a candidate 'isolated area' definition via the shipped tag-namespace partition machinery.

**Why it is not redundant.** Episode membership is neither content nor links: one sitting spans a restaurant, a work idea and an argument, mutually dissimilar by construction — and the probe confirms this shape directly, since one real daily note covers hash tables, a novel manuscript and a tax appointment in four bullets. It also differs from proposal 7, which groups by place across all time where this groups by contiguity in time.

**Kill experiment.** Owner labels 10-15 episodes on the EXISTING vault's dated notes — possible today, no new corpus. Report Adjusted Rand Index and boundary F1 against the DP output, lambda chosen by cross-validation on a held-out half. Then blind judged precision on the 'same stretch' cards. Kill below ARI 0.6: a boundary in the wrong place is a visible, embarrassing error because the user knows exactly when their trip or project started.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Write-date versus event-date is a genuinely hard ambiguity with no offline fix — someone writing up a trip three weeks later breaks contiguity exactly when the note is most interesting. Content drift partially rescues this (the write-up still looks like the trip) which is another argument for the time+content version over time+place. Second: on a vault with few dated notes the segmenter has almost nothing to segment; measure the dated-note fraction first.

**Needs first.** None for the time+content version. That is the point of the correction.


### 9. 9. CORRECTED — run the ridge diagnostic now; the de-crowding correction has a blocker they missed

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Prevents the failure where every note you wrote in one place ranks every other note from that place first.

**How.** Two halves, and I am promoting one and flagging the other. THE DIAGNOSTIC is the best cheap experiment in the entire set and should run before a line of proposal 5 or 6 is written: closed-form ridge from centered note means to the note's 3-vector position, evaluated by leave-one-out great-circle error against a centroid baseline. If a 384-dim vector predicts coordinates well, place is already inside the content channel and every place channel here will correlate with it. One guard they did not state: a ridge from 384 dims to 3 with fewer than roughly 50 coordinate-bearing notes will interpolate perfectly and report a meaningless win, so it needs a minimum-n gate — and on the data that exists today, n is 0. THE CORRECTION has a real blocker. dequant.setTemplateDirs() (index-store.ts:794, 810, 846) takes ONE Map<string, Float32Array> — a single direction per path — and subtractTemplateDirections (index-store.ts:2315) writes dirs.set(path, shared) per group, so a note belonging to both a template group and a place group keeps whichever group ran last. 'Exact reuse with a different grouping key' is therefore not a re-parameterisation; it requires changing the dequant path to compose multiple directions (Gram-Schmidt the place direction against the template direction, or store an array per path), and that touches the code that produced the shipped 8.17 -> 3.35 result. MIN_TEMPLATE_GROUP=3 and the component ceiling max(30, 5% of vault) at index-store.ts:2418 are the existing precedents for the minimum-group-size guard they ask for.

**Why it is not redundant.** A correction, not a channel, so the admission rule does not apply directly. Its value is that it should INCREASE proposal 5's incremental recall by removing from the content channel the part of 'placeness' that channel already carried — the argument that made template de-crowding worth shipping.

**Kill experiment.** Crowding measured through rank() by bench/v3-integration.mjs ('of a place-group note's top 10, how many are same-place'), before and after, with held-out link recall required to be UNCHANGED — the template precedent exactly. Any place de-crowding PR must additionally re-run the template crowding number and show it unmoved, because the multi-direction change touches that path. The diagnostic reports leave-one-out median great-circle error versus the centroid baseline, with n stated.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Place groups on a personal vault may be three notes, and a shared direction estimated from three vectors is noise — hence the minimum-group guard, with which this may almost never apply. The diagnostic half is risk-free and worth running regardless. The correction half carries a regression risk to a shipped, measured result, which is the reason to keep it behind the diagnostic.

**Needs first.** Diagnostic: proposal 3 only, plus >=50 coordinate-bearing notes. Correction: proposal 3 plus the multi-direction composition change.


### 10. 10. CORRECTED — inferred places are display-only, structurally barred from every ranking channel

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** You geotagged one photo on Tuesday; the plugin says the other four notes you wrote that day were probably in the same place — and says 'probably'.

**How.** The interpolation itself is right: for each dated note without a place, assign only when both temporal neighbours agree, each gap is <=1 day, and both sit in the same episode; never across an episode boundary or a multi-day gap; one sorted pass. The correction is to the one thing they left to 'UI-layer discipline', which is not a mechanism. An inferred place must be STRUCTURALLY barred from entering any ranking channel: proposals 5 and 6 read only source in {frontmatter, geo, exif}, enforced by a separate field rather than a confidence float a caller can quietly ignore. Display-only is where a 'probably' is honest, because the user can check it against their own memory; a ranking receipt reading 'probably Lisbon' is not checkable in two seconds and breaks the product's core rule.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel; it multiplies the coverage of the DISPLAY features. Listed separately because coverage is the honest risk on nearly everything else here.

**Kill experiment.** Hold out 20% of the notes that DO have real coordinates, backfill them from the remaining 80%, report the fraction assigned and, of those, the fraction within 25 km of truth. Require >=0.9 correct at whatever assignment rate results. Then re-run proposal 7 with backfill on and off; the delta in FIRING RATE is the headline number.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Their own risk is correct and the mitigation was the weak part, which the display-only constraint fixes. The consequence of that fix is that the value claim shrinks: it no longer multiplies ranking coverage from 3% to 30-40%, it multiplies coverage for the section in proposal 7 only. That should be stated plainly rather than carried forward as the earlier number. A day trip out of the city still breaks the same-day assumption exactly when the note is most interesting.

**Needs first.** Proposals 3 and 8.


### 11. 11. DEAD — already shipped, in a strictly more general form

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD because the plugin already does this for ALL titles, not just places. src/title-index.ts maps every normalised title and alias surface in the vault to its owning files, compiles a longest-first alternation regex, and enforces by construction that only surfaces resolving to a real existing note ever enter it (MIN_SURFACE_CHARS=3, NFC plus locale-aware German folding, explicit ambiguity detection). src/link-glow.ts then renders those unlinked prose occurrences with a one-click 'Click to link' affordance, first occurrence only, skipping wikilinks, code fences and frontmatter. computeInsights already emits suggestedLinks on top of that. Restricting this machinery to detected places can only REMOVE suggestions; it cannot add one. *cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

This is several hundred lines of bit-interleaving in a 620 KB bundle to optimise something that is not on any critical path.

### 13. 13. CORRECTED — cut the gazetteer to countries and admin-1 regions, and bundle it

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The plugin can place your text-only notes at country and region level — 'Portugal' becomes a coordinate — with nothing downloaded and nothing leaving your machine.

**How.** 'The gazetteer proposes, the vault disposes' is the right architecture and the gate should stay exactly as written: a gazetteer hit is admitted only if the string ALSO cleared proposal 2's locative-cue test. That single rule is what kills Nice/Reading/Mobile/Bath and, from the probe, Koenigsberg-in-a-graph-theory-note. But cities15000 is the wrong file. The researcher concedes it has no neighbourhoods, no venues and no rural places — the granularity a personal vault actually cares about — while carrying 25k strings of false-positive fuel at exactly the granularity the vault's own lexicon already handles. Cut it to roughly 250 countries plus roughly 4,000 admin-1 regions with their common alternate names. That is well under 100 KB before compression, small enough to BUNDLE rather than download (so there is no second optional-asset story to explain alongside the jina model), it is the granularity where an external list genuinely beats the vault's own text, and country and region names are the least ambiguous toponyms there are. Everything finer comes from the vault. The population-prior / one-sense-per-discourse / spatial-minimality disambiguation stack barely applies at this granularity, which is a feature, not a loss.

**Why it is not redundant.** It supplies the missing input to the coordinate-based proposals rather than adding a channel. Its own contribution is measurable purely as a coverage delta.

**Kill experiment.** Resolution accuracy on the hand-labelled gold set — but at country/region granularity, 'within 25 km' is the wrong tolerance; use 'correct country/region' instead. The decisive number is the false-positive rate on the maths lab vault, which must be exactly zero: note the probe shows Green, Taylor and Hand all survive a naive detector there, so this bar is not automatic. Coverage delta: fraction of notes gaining coordinates, gazetteer on versus off. Kill below 0.85 vault-gated resolution accuracy.

**Most likely reason it fails.** A country-level coordinate is a very coarse anchor — it puts every Portugal note at the same point, which makes proposal 6's decay kernel degenerate and proposal 7's grouping too broad to be a discovery. The honest position is that this unlocks proposal 7 at country granularity ('also written in Portugal') and does essentially nothing for proposals 6 and 18. If country granularity is judged useless, the answer is not to reach back for cities15000 — it is to accept that this vault has no geography.

**Needs first.** Proposal 2 (the gate). Only worth building if proposal 3's coverage comes back low.


### 14. 14. CORRECTED — run the null test today, on tags, before anything place-shaped exists

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** An insight, not a card: 'Every note you have written about your novel, you wrote in Portugal.' You did not know that.

**How.** The participation index with block permutation over episodes is the right construction, and the researcher's honesty about the place/time confound is the best thing in their write-up. My correction makes it measurable NOW instead of after four prerequisites. Their own null-vault test is the strongest part of the proposal — so run it FIRST, on the existing lab vault, with TAGS as the place surrogate. The vault has 462 tagged notes and zero places, and tag x vault-map-cluster is a pair of features derived from the same underlying content, so the true number of genuinely surprising rules there is near zero by construction. If the participation-index plus Benjamini-Hochberg plus block-permutation pipeline emits more than one rule on that pairing, the significance gate is broken and you have learned it for free, today, with no place data and no new corpus. That converts an unmeasurable speculative proposal into a cheap methodology test whose result is useful to the product whether or not place ever ships. Keep support >=5, lift >=2, block permutation over episodes (from the corrected proposal 8, which is now available without place), and BH at q=0.05.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is a statement about the JOINT distribution of two feature populations, which neither existing channel can express: content similarity compares two notes, the link graph compares two notes, this compares two populations. Same structural move that made Surprising Connections work — the gate is a property of the pair-set, not a score threshold on a pair.

**Kill experiment.** Stage 1 (today): the tag-surrogate null test above; kill the whole approach if it emits more than one rule at q=0.05 where the truth is zero. Stage 2 (only if stage 1 passes and place data exists): judged P@5 and 'I did not know that' rate on the Surprising Connections v2 protocol, against P@5 1.00 / 36% surprising.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The confound is severe and they state it correctly: place and time are almost perfectly entangled in a personal vault, so 'you only write about X in Y' is usually 'you wrote about X during the two weeks you were in Y'. Block permutation is the right correction and it will destroy most apparent findings. The honest outcome may be that a 500-note vault contains zero statistically defensible rules — but the corrected version at least finds that out this week, for free.

**Needs first.** Stage 1: the corrected proposal 8 (time-only episodes) and the shipped vault map. Nothing else.

### 16. 16. ADDED — the folder tree is the spatial structure that actually has coverage

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Your related notes stop being dominated by whatever folder you happen to be reading in, so a note filed somewhere else in your tree gets a fair chance at the list.

**How.** The one containment hierarchy every vault already has is the folder tree, and the plugin currently uses it only for EXCLUSION (excludeFolders, index-store.ts:1564). It is user-authored, free from TFile.path, and covers 100% of notes in 100% of vaults, and it supports every operation the researcher wanted from a place hierarchy: an LCA, a depth-normalised Wu-Palmer similarity, a df-discounted shared-ancestor weight. Two uses, in order of expected value. (a) DE-CROWDING KEY: group by immediate parent folder — or by the deepest ancestor with >=MIN_TEMPLATE_GROUP members and <= the existing max(30, 5% of vault) ceiling — and subtract the shared direction, using subtractTemplateDirections unchanged except for the grouping function. Same code, same guards, same chunk-level application, and it needs the same multi-direction composition fix flagged in proposal 9. (b) DIVERSITY CONSTRAINT on the rendered top-k: cap how many of the ten cards may come from the active note's own folder. I deliberately do NOT propose folder co-membership as a positive similarity channel, and the codebase says why: a same-folder structural boost was already tried and REMOVED (index-store.ts:189-193, 'in an atomic-note vault the whole library lives in ~3 folders, so it fired for nearly every candidate — constant noise'). That comment is also the argument FOR this proposal: a signal that fires on nearly every candidate is the textbook definition of a crowding artefact, and the shipped response to a crowding artefact is to subtract its direction, not to score with it.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel, so the admission rule does not apply — it is a correction and a constraint, exactly like template de-crowding. Its independence claim is about COVERAGE: it delivers the hierarchy machinery of proposals 4 and 5 on every note of every vault, where the geographic version delivers it on approximately none.

**Kill experiment.** Measurable today, on the existing harness, with no new corpus and no detector. Run bench/v3-integration.mjs's crowding metric with the folder key ('of a note's top 10, how many share its immediate parent folder') before and after, and require held-out link recall@10 UNCHANGED — the exact bar template de-crowding cleared (8.17 -> 3.35 crowding, recall flat). Measure the baseline first: real/Concepts holds 140 of 465 notes, so I expect folder crowding to be high. Kill if recall drops at all, or if baseline folder crowding is already low.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Folder structure encodes topic, so subtracting the folder direction may remove genuine signal rather than an artefact — that is precisely what the recall-unchanged bar is for, and failing it is a real possibility, not a formality. Second, many vaults are flat (one folder, or none), in which case this does nothing; measure the folder-depth distribution before building. Third, it collides with template de-crowding on the single-direction-per-path limitation, so it cannot ship before that is fixed.

**Needs first.** The multi-direction composition fix described in proposal 9, if it is to coexist with template de-crowding.


### 17. 17. ADDED — writing sessions as pseudo-places: the spatial-context cue without any geography

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** 'Written in the same sitting — 4 notes, Tuesday evening.' The notes you wrote alongside this one, which you would never have thought to look for.

**How.** The cognitive result underwriting proposal 7 — that reinstating encoding context supports recall, with the benefit concentrated on weak memories — does not require GEOGRAPHIC context. It requires an episodic context the user can recognise. A writing session is one, and it is free: TFile.stat.mtime, already read throughout index-store.ts (e.g. :2059, :1866). Cluster notes by mtime with a single gap threshold — a new session starts after a gap of more than G minutes, G around 90 — and treat each session as a pseudo-place with the same salience, df-discount and de-crowding treatment a real place would get, so everything downstream of proposal 7 works unchanged on it at essentially full coverage instead of near zero. Surface as the same gated section: >=2 and <=12 members, mutual-top-10 exclusion and a content floor copied verbatim from surprisingConnections, noteAreas/areaMatch respected, receipt 'written alongside this, Tue 14 Nov, 21:04-22:30'. This is the version of proposal 7 I would build first, and if proposal 1's prevalence probe comes back empty it is the only version that ever gets built.

**Why it is not redundant.** A sitting spans topics by construction, and the probe confirms this directly: one real daily note covers hash tables, collision resolution, a novel manuscript and a tax appointment in four bullets. So it is not the content channel. Sittings are typically unlinked, so it is not the graph channel. And it is not recency, which the panel already has as a display line (showRecency): recency ORDERS by time, this GROUPS by co-occurrence in time, and the two disagree on exactly the notes that matter — an old cluster of four notes written together outranks four unrelated notes from yesterday.

**Kill experiment.** Blind judged precision on the Surprising Connections v2 protocol — P@5 and the 'I did not know that' rate, against the 1.00 / 36% bar — plus FIRING RATE, which unlike proposal 7 should be near 100% and is the entire reason to prefer this. Runnable on the existing lab vault this week, with no new corpus, no detector, and no gazetteer. Kill below P@5 0.7. Ablate G (30 / 90 / 240 minutes) and report sensitivity.

**Most likely reason it fails.** This is the one that can break loudly, and the failure is checkable. Obsidian's mtime is EDIT time, not authoring time: a vault restored from backup, cloned from git, or freshly synced through iCloud has had every mtime rewritten to within seconds, which would collapse the whole vault into one 'sitting' and put a confidently wrong receipt on every note. It needs the same shape of guard as the template component ceiling at index-store.ts:2418 — reject any session whose member count exceeds a small fraction of the vault, and disable the feature outright when the vault's mtime distribution is degenerate. Second, softer risk: a user who writes in one long daily session gets one session per day, and the section is true but uninformative.

**Needs first.** None. This is the cheapest magic-slot candidate in the area and the only one that does not depend on proposal 1 coming back positive.


### 18. 18. ADDED — place as a VETO, never as a score

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing directly visible. It removes a specific kind of wrong card: two unrelated notes that pair only because both happen to be travel notes.

**How.** The admission rule keeps killing positive channels for correlating with content — BM25 at 0.30, shared tags at 0.916 — and nobody proposed the one use where correlation does not disqualify a signal. A signal can be independent and useful as a VETO even when it is worthless as a score. Concretely: when BOTH notes carry a high-confidence, non-inferred place anchor (source in {frontmatter, geo, exif}, or a lexicon hit above proposal 2's 0.90-precision operating point) and those anchors are mutually exclusive at the coarsest hierarchy level (different geohash-3 cells, or two toponyms with no common ancestor), that is evidence AGAINST the pair belonging to the same episode. Use it first and only inside the grouping features — proposals 7, 8 and addition 17 — to split a group that similarity alone would have merged, and only if measured to help, as a demotion in the main panel. Never as a promotion. A veto composes with the existing funnel at the cheapest possible point: it runs on the shortlist, needs no z-normalisation, and cannot suffer the sparse-column blow-up described in proposal 5 because it never adds mass.

**Why it is not redundant.** It occupies an architectural slot the admission rule does not test. A filter cannot be redundant with content the way a positive channel can, because its contribution is measured as precision at fixed recall rather than as recall delta — a channel that removes only wrong answers is admissible at any correlation. It is also the move that fits 'silence beats a wrong answer' most directly, and it is the cheapest possible use of a low-coverage signal: a veto that fires on 3% of pairs costs nothing on the other 97%, whereas a score that fires on 3% of pairs distorts the whole column.

**Kill experiment.** Not recall@10. Apply the veto to the shipped top-10 and report, on the blind judged protocol, how many REMOVED cards the judges had rated wrong versus right. Kill it if it removes more right than wrong cards, or if it fires on fewer than a handful of pairs per vault — in which case it is correct and inert, which is a fine outcome to record and not ship.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The failure mode is that it removes GOOD cards. Two notes anchored to different cities can be strongly related — the same idea, thought about twice, in two places — and that is arguably the most interesting pair in the vault. This is why it must never touch the main panel unless the judged measurement explicitly says otherwise, which I do not expect it to. Confined to episode splitting it is much safer, because there the user's own memory is the ground truth and they will notice immediately if it is wrong.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 (real coordinates) or proposal 2 at its high-precision operating point. Explicitly NOT proposal 10 — an inferred place must never veto anything.


### 19. 19. ADDED — place-string note creation: the one gap link-glow cannot reach

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** 'You have mentioned Lisbon in nine notes and there is no Lisbon note. Create one?' — with the nine sentences as the evidence.

**How.** src/title-index.ts is explicit that only surfaces resolving to a real EXISTING note ever enter its alternation — that is exactly what makes the inline glow precise, and exactly why it can never surface an entity that has no note yet. That is the single real gap proposal 11 was gesturing at, and it survives its death. Take the place lexicon's high-precision admissions (proposal 2), subtract every surface TitleIndex already owns, keep those above a document-frequency floor, and offer them in the existing Vault Insights report (renderInsights, main.ts:690) as a 'places you write about but have not written down' section, each with its containing sentences as the receipt. Creating the note is one file write, and from that moment every future occurrence glows through the shipped path with no new code — the feedback loop proposal 11 wanted, but reaching a surface the shipped machinery genuinely cannot.

**Why it is not redundant.** Deliberately not independent as a ranking signal — it is a feeder into the link graph, which is the strongest measured channel (+0.081 recall). But it is not redundant with link-glow, which by construction cannot fire on a string that has no note, and that is the whole justification.

**Kill experiment.** Blind judged precision of the top 20 offered strings, bar >=0.9, because it proposes writing a new file into the user's vault and that is the higher bar. Then the downstream number that actually decides whether entity detection pays for itself here: after creating the notes and letting the user link a few, re-run held-out link recall@10 with the augmented graph. Report the hub effect the researcher correctly identified — a new 'Lisbon' note accumulating 40 inbound links crosses HUB_DEGREE=40, at which point RA's 1/deg term discounts it to nothing AND pprFrom's hub guard refuses to expand through it, so the feedback loop can genuinely eat its own gain. Measure that, do not assume it either way.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Creating notes is the most invasive action on this list, and a vault full of one-line place stubs is clutter that RA then correctly ignores — worst of both. Entirely gated on proposal 2 clearing 0.90 precision, which on German multi-word cues is not established and may not be reachable. If proposal 2 lands below that bar, this must not ship at all: a wrongly suggested note is a file the user has to delete.

**Needs first.** Proposal 2 at >=0.90 precision.


## Time-aware graphs and temporal link prediction


<details><summary>13 techniques surveyed</summary>

- **Resource Allocation index** (established, 2009) — RA(s,t) = sum over shared neighbours x of 1/deg(x). The degree penalty is what makes it beat plain common-neighbour counting on graphs with hubs. Already shipped as the plugin's structural channel in src/graph-signals.ts:122-146.  
  <sub>Zhou, Lü, Zhang, 'Predicting missing links via local information', Eur. Phys. J. B 71:623 (2009), arXiv:0901.0553</sub>
- **Kleinberg two-state burst automaton** (established, 2002) — Infinite-state (in practice two- or three-state) HMM over an arrival-time stream; a DP finds the optimal state sequence, yielding intervals where the arrival rate is s times the base rate, penalised by a state-transition cost gamma. O(n * states^2). This is the correct algorithm for P7 and it is genuinely cheap.  
  <sub>Kleinberg, 'Bursty and Hierarchical Structure in Streams', KDD 2002 / Data Mining and Knowledge Discovery 7(4):373-397 (2003)</sub>
- **Temporal (delta-)motifs** (established, 2017) — Counts of 3-edge motifs on 2-3 nodes whose edges all fall inside a delta window, giving each node a role profile. The counting algorithm is efficient in the paper's regime but the constant is dominated by high-degree nodes, and it is not cheaply incrementally maintainable, which is why I downgraded P8.  
  <sub>Paranjape, Benson, Leskovec, 'Motifs in Temporal Networks', WSDM 2017, arXiv:1612.09259</sub>
- **CTDNE — time-respecting random walks** (established, 2018) — Restricts random walks to edge sequences with non-decreasing timestamps, so walks follow causally plausible paths. The definition P9 borrows. Critically, it degenerates when many edges share a timestamp, which is exactly the batch-imported-vault case.  
  <sub>Nguyen et al., 'Continuous-Time Dynamic Network Embeddings', WWW '18 Companion</sub>
- **Temporal Katz / online decayed centrality** (recent-but-replicated, 2018) — Accumulates exp(-(now - t)/tau)-weighted time-respecting walks with an O(k * deg) per-edge-arrival update, so it is maintainable on a stream without rebuild. Source of P3's decay weight at k=1 and P6's multiplicative count decay.  
  <sub>Béres, Pálovics, Benczúr et al., 'Temporal walk based centrality metric for graph streams', Applied Network Science 3:32 (2018), arXiv:1810.05867; and 'Node embeddings in dynamic graphs', Applied Network Science 4:64 (2019)</sub>
- **EdgeBank — the memorisation baseline** (established, 2022) — A pure-memorisation heuristic (predict an edge iff it was seen before, optionally within a window) that matches or beats trained temporal GNNs on much of the standard benchmark suite. This is the specific baseline P2 should name when it says 'memorisation floor'; the first researcher described it without citing it.  
  <sub>Poursafaei, Huang, Pelrine, Rabbany, 'Towards Better Evaluation for Dynamic Link Prediction', NeurIPS 2022 Datasets & Benchmarks, arXiv:2207.10128</sub>
- **Historical and inductive negative sampling** (established, 2022) — Same paper as EdgeBank. Shows method rankings reorder completely between random negatives and 'historical' negatives (plausible past non-edges). This is the discipline P2 imports and the direct source of P13's idea; it is a sound evaluation practice and a bad product feature.  
  <sub>Poursafaei et al., NeurIPS 2022 D&B, arXiv:2207.10128</sub>
- **TGB — Temporal Graph Benchmark** (established, 2023) — Standardised chronological splits, MRR against fixed negative sets, and the finding that recall@k on small negative samples is misleading. Source of P2's (a) and (b) disciplines.  
  <sub>Huang et al., 'Temporal Graph Benchmark for Machine Learning on Temporal Graphs', NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks, arXiv:2307.01026</sub>
- **TGB-Seq — the repeat-ratio critique** (frontier-unproven, 2025) — Argues that headline temporal-link-prediction results are inflated because 56-88% of test edges in standard benchmarks are repeats of past edges, so memorisation and recency dominate. On a wikilink graph the repeat ratio is ~0, which predicts recency-style channels (P4) will be weak. NOTE: I could not verify the exact venue/ID; treat the number as the first researcher's claim, and compute the plugin's own repeat ratio directly (it is a five-line script) rather than importing 56-88%.  
  <sub>Reported as 'TGB-Seq Benchmark', ICLR 2025 — CITATION UNVERIFIED, web-search budget exhausted this session</sub>
- **TPNet — temporal walk matrix projection** (frontier-unproven, 2024) — Cited by the first researcher as the source for the k=1 temporal walk matrix that P3 reduces to. CITATION UNVERIFIED. P3 does not actually depend on it — decayed RA is derivable from the Béres decay weight alone — so do not let an unverified citation carry the proposal.  
  <sub>Reported as NeurIPS 2024 — CITATION UNVERIFIED, web-search budget exhausted this session</sub>
- **Most-recent neighbour sampling** (established, 2020) — Sampling a node's most recent neighbours rather than uniformly at random is a consistent win across temporal GNN architectures. This is the finding P10 invokes. It applies to *sampling* from a neighbourhood you cannot afford to enumerate; the plugin's frontier cut is a *mass-ordered* selection, which is a different and better-motivated operation, so the finding does not transfer.  
  <sub>Rossi et al., 'Temporal Graph Networks for Deep Learning on Dynamic Graphs', ICML 2020 GRL+ workshop, arXiv:2006.10637 (sampling ablation). The '10,000 GPU hour' framing the first researcher used is UNVERIFIED and may refer to a different survey.</sub>
- **Session segmentation by inter-event gap** (established, 2016) — Standard practice in session-based recommendation: split an event stream into sessions at idle gaps. The specific median+3*MAD sub-session split P6 proposes is a reasonable robust-statistics heuristic but I could not verify the 'STAR' attribution; treat it as an unattributed heuristic and sweep the threshold empirically.  
  <sub>General session-based recsys practice, e.g. Hidasi et al., 'Session-based Recommendations with Recurrent Neural Networks', ICLR 2016, arXiv:1511.06939. 'STAR' attribution UNVERIFIED.</sub>
- **Git history as ground truth for edge birth times** (established, n/a) — `git log --follow -p -- <file>` plus a wikilink diff yields the exact commit time at which each link first appeared. This is the only source of true edge times available without months of logging, and it is what makes N1 the critical path rather than P1.  
  <sub>Standard tooling; no paper needed</sub>

</details>


### 1. N1. Acquire a git-versioned corpus FIRST — everything else is blocked on it

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. It is the reason any of the other thirteen items can be believed or refuted at all.

**How.** Do not build P1 and then audit it. The audit already fails on the only corpus that exists, and I measured it: /Users/justus/obsidian_atomized_intermediary/lab/vault has 528 notes and ZERO Zettelkasten-ID basenames, ZERO files with a frontmatter `date`/`created`/`updated` field, 24 ISO-dated basenames (4.5%), and mtimes collapsed onto exactly three days (367 on 2026-07-02, 160 on 2026-07-01, 1 on 2026-07-28). Tiers 1, 2, 3 and 4 of P1's cascade are all dead or near-dead there, and there is no .git. So: acquire a corpus with real history before writing any TimeIndex code. Three sources, in order of preference. (1) PUBLIC GIT-VERSIONED OBSIDIAN VAULTS. Several sizeable ones are published on GitHub with full commit history. `git log --follow -p` over each .md file, diffing the wikilink set between parent and child commits, yields exact per-edge firstSeen/removedAt — actual ground truth, not a reconstruction. This also gives the only honest test bed for P1's cascade, because you can compare each tier against truth on the SAME vault. (2) The owner's live vault exported with `rsync -aE` or `ditto` (both preserve mtime; a Finder copy does not). Memory says TCC blocks shell access to the iCloud vault, so this likely needs an Obsidian-side export or a local (non-iCloud) clone first. (3) Synthetic history, only as a negative control, never as evidence. Deliverable: a `bench/data/temporal-corpus/` with per-edge (src, dst, firstSeenCommitTime) and a provenance record in the existing manifest style.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. It is the precondition the first researcher put in a `risk` field on three separate proposals without noticing that it is the same blocker each time and therefore the actual first task.

**Kill experiment.** Self-validating: the corpus either has per-edge git-derived birth times or it does not. Acceptance gate before proceeding: at least 3,000 timestamped edges spanning at least 18 months, in at least two vaults so a result is not one person's habit. Report the repeat ratio and the degree distribution alongside, since P2 needs both.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Public vaults are not the owner's vault — a stranger's linking habits may not transfer, which is why the gate asks for two. Licence and privacy diligence is required before checking anyone else's notes into bench/data; store derived edge tables (hashed paths + timestamps), not note text.

**Needs first.** None. This is the true root of the dependency tree, ahead of P1.


### 2. N2. The thirty-minute kill test for the entire area: does writing time predict linking at all?

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. It tells you within an afternoon whether to spend a month here.

**How.** On the N1 corpus, compute P(s links t | |birth(s) - birth(t)| < w) against the base link density, for w in {1d, 7d, 30d, 90d, 1y, all}. One pass over the edge table plus a matched random-pair sample. Do the same for the *edge* axis: P(two links share a source | written within the same day). If temporal proximity carries no lift over base rate, then P3, P5, P9 and P11 are all predicted to fail, because every one of them is ultimately a bet that 'written near in time' correlates with 'belongs together'. If the lift is large, you have both a green light and a calibrated tau to start the P3 sweep from, instead of guessing 180 days.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not a feature. It is the single cheapest experiment that can falsify a whole research area, and no one proposed it. The first researcher jumped straight to building a TimeIndex; a builder asks whether the axis carries signal before building the axis.

**Kill experiment.** Self-validating. Report lift-over-base-rate with a bootstrap CI at each window. KILL CONDITION FOR THE AREA: if lift at w = 30d is below 1.5x base rate with a CI excluding 2x, close the area and report the null. That is a genuinely valuable result — it explains why a lexical channel and a tag channel both failed, and it says the vault's structure is topical rather than chronological.

**Most likely reason it fails.** A positive result may be confounded by topic: you write about one thing for a fortnight, so temporally near notes are also topically near, and the content channel already has them. Mitigate by conditioning on content similarity — report lift within content-similarity deciles. If the lift vanishes once you condition on cosine, that is the shared-tags failure mode arriving early, and it kills P3/P5/P11 before they are built.

**Needs first.** N1.


### 3. N3. Cold-start graph bootstrap: the structural channel is provably silent on the note you are writing

*moderate · high confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** The panel keeps working on a note you just created. Today, on a brand-new note, the link-graph half of the ranker contributes literally nothing and you get pure wording similarity without being told.

**How.** I traced this in the shipped code and it is exact, not approximate. For an active note with no resolved links: graph.candidates() calls pprFrom(), whose frontier starts at {active: 1}, finds this.adj.get(active) === undefined, and returns an empty accumulator (src/graph-signals.ts:153-186). graph.evidence(active, x) hits `if (!na || !nb) return { shared: [], ra: 0 }` for every candidate (src/graph-signals.ts:130). The fusion at src/index-store.ts:2628-2633 then builds zRa over an all-zero array; zNormaliser sees sd < 1e-9 and returns () => 0. So graphLift is exactly 0 for every card. The graph channel is not degraded on a new note — it is off, silently, in precisely the moment the user most wants help. The temporal fix: when the active note has degree 0 (or 1), synthesise a provisional neighbourhood from the notes edited in the same recent window — the working set — and run RA against that borrowed neighbourhood, with the receipt stated honestly as 'via what you were working on' rather than 'via [[X]]'. Gate it on degree <= 1 so it can never touch the established-note path, and cap its z-contribution below graphInfluence so it cannot outvote real evidence. In the lab vault 75 of 525 notes (14%) already have degree <= 1, where RA fires through a single neighbour at best.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not a new channel competing with RA; it is RA operating in the region where RA is defined as zero. Its correlation with the existing graph channel on established notes is irrelevant because it is switched off there by construction. Independence from content is the same argument the graph channel already won.

**Kill experiment.** Do NOT use the standard held-out protocol — it masks 20% of links from notes that keep the other 80%, so it never constructs the degree-0 case and cannot see this at all. Build a NEW slice: for each note, remove ALL of its outgoing and incoming links, then ask whether its true link targets rank in the top-10. Baseline is content-only (which is exactly what ships today for such a note). Report the size of the degree-0 population separately, and report what fraction of panel opens in real usage happen on a note with degree <= 1 — if that is under 5%, the feature is real but rare and should be sequenced accordingly.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The borrowed neighbourhood is a recency prior in disguise, and it inherits P4's failure mode: it will suggest this week's notes. The degree <= 1 gate is what keeps that contained, and the honest receipt is what keeps it from lying. Second risk: on a vault with degenerate mtimes the 'working set' is the whole vault and the feature must detect that and stay silent — reuse P1's confidence tier as the gate.

**Needs first.** P1 only for the confidence gate; the working set itself comes from mtime, which the index already stores (src/vector-math.ts:265).


### 4. N4. Link cohorts: 'you added these two links in the same sitting, but never connected them to each other'

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** 'On 14 March you linked this note to [[A]] and to [[B]] within minutes of each other. A and B are still not linked.' One click writes it.

**How.** The edge journal (P1 tier 5) records firstSeenAt per edge. Group edges by (source note, authoring session) using the same MAD-based gap split as P6, giving a COHORT: the set of link targets a user wrote out of one note in one sitting. Then look for open triangles within a cohort — targets A and B that entered through the same source in the same sitting and are not linked to each other. Score by cohort tightness (how close in time), inverse cohort size (a 40-link MOC-building session is worthless, a 3-link session is a thought), and content similarity as a floor only. The receipt is unusually strong and self-verifying in the required two seconds, because it names the source note AND the moment: the user can go look at that note and immediately recognise the sitting.

**Why it is not redundant.** RA already scores A and B as sharing neighbour s — that is exactly what RA does. The independent quantity is not the sharing, it is the SIMULTANEITY. RA cannot distinguish 'both linked from s, three years apart, for unrelated reasons' from 'both linked from s in the same four minutes'. That distinction is a strictly finer partition of the pairs RA already surfaces, which means it should be tested as a RE-RANKER OF RA'S OWN OUTPUT, not as an added channel — measure whether it lifts judged precision among pairs RA already ranks highly. Framed that way the admission-rule correlation test does not apply, and the honest question is precision at fixed recall.

**Kill experiment.** Two parts. (1) Offline: on the N1 git corpus, cohorts are directly reconstructable from commit history — links added in one commit are one cohort, for free, with no journal cold start. Measure whether open triangles inside a commit-cohort close in the FUTURE at a higher rate than open triangles among same-source pairs written far apart. That is a clean, fully offline test of the whole premise, runnable in a day on N1's data, and it needs no plugin change at all. (2) If (1) passes: blind judged precision on 20 fired suggestions against the Surprising Connections bar (P@5 1.00, 36% surprising).

**Most likely reason it fails.** Bulk edits destroy cohorts: a find-and-replace, a template application, or an import stamps hundreds of edges with one timestamp and generates a combinatorial explosion of worthless triangles. Hard-cap cohort size (drop cohorts above ~8 edges) and drop cohorts whose edges are all within one second, which is the machine-written signature. Second risk: in the plugin (as opposed to the git corpus) the journal only observes links at metadataCache 'resolved'/'changed' granularity, so 'same sitting' resolution is real but 'within minutes' may not survive Obsidian's debouncing — measure the observed timestamp resolution before promising minute-level receipts.

**Needs first.** N1 for the offline test (which is the decisive one). P1 tier-5 journal only for the shipped version.


### 5. P1. Edge-time reconstruction layer — KEEP, with three corrections and a reordered prereq

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing directly. The substrate the rest of the area is defined over.

**How.** Cascade and journal design are right; three concrete corrections. (a) THE COMBINATION RULE IS BACKWARDS. The proposal says edge time = min(source note birth, journal firstSeen). Those are bounds in opposite directions: source birth is a LOWER bound (a link cannot predate its source note) and journal firstSeen is an UPPER bound (the link existed at or before the moment you first observed it). Taking min() always returns the lower bound and throws the journal away the instant a note has any birth time, which silently disables tier 5 — the only causally correct tier — on every note. The correct object is the interval [max(birth(s), birth(t)), journalFirstSeen], with a point estimate and an explicit width; downstream decay should use the point estimate and downstream gating should use the width. (b) THE STORAGE ARITHMETIC IS WRONG. (srcId, dstId, firstSeenAt, lastSeenAt, removedAt) is 2x4-byte ids plus 3 timestamps; at float64 that is 32 bytes, at uint32 seconds-since-epoch 20 bytes, not 16. At 1M edges that is 20-32 MB, still fine, but do not size the heap budget off a wrong number. (c) 3 bits of confidence is over-engineered — nothing downstream can consume a fraction. Use one byte holding the tier ordinal, and have consumers make a binary trust/do-not-fire decision. (d) Prereq is N1, not none: the audit this proposal specifies cannot be run on any corpus currently in the repo.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct as stated — not a channel, not subject to the admission rule. I verified the claim that time never enters any score: mtime appears in src/vector-math.ts (stored per entry) and src/view.ts:496 (rendered as 'edited 3d ago'), and nowhere in the ranking path.

**Kill experiment.** The coverage-and-agreement audit is the right protocol and the +/-30-day kill condition is the right bar, but note that it is ALREADY FAILED on the lab vault before you write a line: tiers 1-3 cover 4.5% of notes and tier 4 has three distinct values across 528 notes. So run it on N1's git corpus, where truth exists, and report coverage and accuracy separately per tier as specified.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Unchanged and confirmed by direct measurement: sync and copy operations destroy ctime/mtime wholesale. The lab vault is already in that state. If public vaults look the same, the journal is the only trustworthy tier, every temporal feature carries a multi-month cold start, and the correct decision is to ship the journal quietly now (it costs nothing and starts accumulating) while deferring every feature built on it.

**Needs first.** N1. Then P1 gates P3, P5, P7, P9, P11, P14 and N4.


### 6. P2. Chronological protocol — KEEP and PROMOTE, but its own power calculation kills the lab vault

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. It is the measurement change that makes this area adjudicable, and it may also revise a number the project has already published.

**How.** The mechanism is right and I would reframe its purpose. This is not scaffolding for future proposals: it is an AUDIT OF A SHIPPED CLAIM. Random masking lets the graph channel see links written after the masked one, so RA is computed over a finished graph and the 0.664 -> 0.745 result is measured in a regime the user never occupies. The honest expectation is that both numbers fall under a time-forward split and the GAP narrows. Import EdgeBank explicitly as the memorisation floor (the first researcher described it without naming it) and report the repeat ratio, which on a wikilink graph should be near zero. Also specify the unit: recall@10 per held-out EDGE and per QUERY NOTE give different n and different CIs; pick per-edge and say so.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct as stated.

**Kill experiment.** Self-validating, and the power calculation the proposal asks for can be done right now on paper, which changes the conclusion. 465 notes, ~1327 links, newest 20% ≈ 265 held-out edges. A naive binomial CI at p ≈ 0.745, n = 265 has half-width 1.96*sqrt(0.745*0.255/265) ≈ 0.053, so a full width of ~0.105 — wider than the 0.081 the graph channel bought. A PAIRED bootstrap on the same held-out edges is the right test and is tighter, plausibly resolving ~0.05. Either way, a decayed-RA delta of the size P3 realistically produces (0.01-0.03) is NOT resolvable at n = 265. Conclusion, stated plainly: the lab vault cannot adjudicate this area at all, and no amount of protocol care fixes that. N1 is mandatory, not a mitigation.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Reframed: the risk is not that the CI is wide, it is that running this on the lab vault produces a number that LOOKS like a result and is actually noise. Guard by refusing to report any temporal delta measured on fewer than ~1,500 held-out edges.

**Needs first.** N1, P1.


### 7. P3. Time-decayed Resource Allocation — KEEP, with a consistency bug fixed and a second control added

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The panel stops treating a link you wrote three years ago and forgot as equal evidence to one you wrote last month, and stops letting a long-abandoned index note suppress good connections.

**How.** Right idea, cheapest real item in the list, and it does touch exactly the shipped code path (src/graph-signals.ts:122-146, consumed at src/index-store.ts:2595 and 2628-2633). Two corrections. (a) THE 'CANNOT LOSE BY CONSTRUCTION' CLAIM IS ONLY TRUE IF YOU CHANGE BOTH USES OF DEGREE TOGETHER. evidence() applies a HARD HUB CUT — `if (deg > HUB_DEGREE) continue` — before it ever reaches the 1/deg term. If the score uses deg_tau but the hub test keeps using raw deg, tau = infinity no longer recovers plain RA and the sweep is not a superset. Specify that deg_tau replaces deg in the hub test as well, and note that this makes the hub SET time-varying, which is the intended effect and also a new source of instability worth logging. (b) The graph is built by symmetrising resolvedLinks (src/graph-signals.ts:91-101), so an undirected edge has one time — whichever direction was written first. w(s,x) and w(x,t) are therefore the same quantity when s and t both link x, not two independent weights. The formula still works, but do not describe it as if the two legs carry independent evidence. (c) ADD A SECOND CONTROL beyond naive recency: recompute RA_tau with every edge time replaced by the TARGET NOTE's birth time. If that scores the same, the channel is a note-age prior and not an edge-age one, which matters because on most vaults P1 will only supply note birth times anyway.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct as stated, and correctly framed as a replacement rather than an addition, so the admission rule applies to the delta.

**Kill experiment.** Sweep tau in {30d, 90d, 180d, 1y, infinity} at matched fusion weight under P2, picking on paired-bootstrap recall@10 and MRR. Naive-recency control is mandatory and I am adding the note-birth-time control above. Seed the sweep from N2's measured lift curve rather than guessing 180 days. If tau = infinity wins, the feature closes cleanly.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Unchanged and correctly identified: on most vaults the 'edge time' is really the source note's birth time, so this may be a recency prior wearing a graph channel's clothes — hence two controls, not one. Added risk: the time-varying hub set means a single edit can flip a note in or out of hub status and visibly reshuffle the panel, which reads as instability to a user. Log hub-set churn per edit during the sweep.

**Needs first.** N1, N2, P1, P2.


### 8. P4. Recency prior as a third channel — DOWNGRADE to 'control first, channel maybe'

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** When two candidates are otherwise equally good, the one you touched last week is shown first.

**How.** Correct on substance; two implementation claims are wrong about the shipped code. (a) 'It can reorder, never promote' is stated as if it follows from the design, but the fusion ALREADY HAS A PROMOTION PATH: src/index-store.ts:2645-2652 lets a graph-nominated note in below minSimilarity at STRUCTURAL_FLOOR_RATIO * minSim. A recency channel must be explicitly excluded from that rescue path, which the proposal does not say and which is the exact place a careless implementation would leak. (b) 'Hard-capped weight' does not exist to be reused. The main fusion applies `lift = graphInfluence * zRa(s.ra)` UNCLAMPED (src/index-store.ts:2631). The clamp to [-2, 2] exists only in the OTHER call site (src/index-store.ts:1150). So the cap must be written, not inherited. (c) I would reframe the whole item: its primary value is as P3's mandatory control, and shipping it as a channel is the secondary, weaker outcome. The first researcher's own TGB-Seq argument predicts it fails, and I agree.

**Why it is not redundant.** Trivially orthogonal to content. But orthogonal and useful are different, and the repeat-ratio argument says a wikilink graph is the regime where recency baselines are weakest.

**Kill experiment.** Both protocols are right and the 90-day-old-query kill condition is the best-designed gate in the original list — keep it verbatim. Add: report the channel's rank correlation with N3's borrowed-neighbourhood signal, because those two will overlap heavily and only one should ship.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Unchanged: a recency channel makes the panel show this week's notes forever, destroying the compounds-as-the-vault-grows property. Compounded by the two implementation corrections above — without an explicit clamp and an explicit exclusion from the rescue path, 'reorder never promote' is a comment, not a behaviour.

**Needs first.** P2 for the link protocol; shares the open-event log with P6.


### 9. P5. Temporal co-mention channel — CORRECT SHARPLY: as written it is the shared-tags channel again

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** A card that says: this note keeps showing up in the same week as that one. Receipt: 'both appear in 2026-03-15'.

**How.** The proposal as written fails the admission rule by construction, and the first researcher missed why. Two notes co-mentioned in the SAME daily note are, by definition, two notes with a shared neighbour — and RA already scores exactly that pair, with the daily note as x. So the same-day component of this channel is RA restricted to dated hubs with a different degree penalty. That is the shared-tags situation (correlation 0.916 with the graph channel) arriving under a new name, and it will be caught by the correlation test after the work is done. NARROW IT NOW: the only part that RA provably cannot see is CROSS-DAY co-mention between notes that never share a dated neighbour — a touched on Tuesday's note, b touched on Friday's note, no note mentions both. Restrict the channel to exactly those pairs (drop every same-note co-mention, which RA owns), keep the 1/(1 + |d_a - d_b|/sigma) kernel and the IDF damping, and the independence argument becomes real rather than assumed.

**Why it is not redundant.** After the narrowing: a cross-day pair has no shared neighbour, so RA scores it 0 and PPR reaches it only through unrelated structure. Before the narrowing: not independent at all.

**Kill experiment.** Coverage first, and I confirmed the numbers by direct count on the lab vault — 24 ISO-dated notes emitting 26 of 2503 wikilinks, 1.0% of edges. That is not 'hard to measure', it is unmeasurable, and the narrowing above shrinks the eligible pair set further. So this cannot be evaluated until N1 supplies a daily-note-heavy corpus. Then: report rank correlation against BOTH content and the graph channel before reporting any recall delta, and refuse to proceed past 0.30 (the bar that cut the lexical channel). Blind judged precision against the Surprising Connections bar only if the correlation gate passes.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Even after narrowing, cross-day co-mention on a small vault produces a handful of pairs, and precision-first means it will be silent almost always.
