**Why it is not redundant.** Block structure is finer than resolvedLinks and is not recoverable from it, so the independence claim was correct. Independence was never the problem; predictiveness was.

**Kill experiment.** Done, on the repaired protocol. Anyone revisiting this must also apply the contamination rule from the provenance proposal below: on a real user vault 8,177 of the 11,270 co-mention pairs come from the plugin's own 'Vault Insights' report, whose 'Suggested links' section literally prints '[[A]] and [[B]]' pairs on one line. A co-mention channel on a real vault would be scoring the plugin's own past output. My -0.0516 is measured on clean data only because that note happens to be absent from the lab embedding cache.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Measured negative, twice, with tight standard errors. The open-triangle card is untested and may still be worth a judged-precision run (sample 20 open triangles, KILL below 70%), but it must be a separate surfaced claim, never a re-ranker.

**Needs first.** None. Do not build the re-ranking half.


### 7. Directed RA variants and structural roles — DEAD as proposed; the roles are worth keeping separately

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Was: fewer backwards suggestions, a MOC stops being proposed as related to everything.

**How.** Three corrections, two of them fatal. (a) The premise is wrong: bibliographic coupling ALREADY SHIPS. index-store.ts:3100-3106 computes 'shared outgoing resolved links / min(|outlinks|)' from directed resolvedLinks and folds it into the structural boost at W_BIBLIO = 0.5. The plugin is not blind to direction; only the RA channel is. (b) The measurement fails: content + RA_out-out + RA_in-in scores 0.7189 against 0.7453 for symmetric RA on the same split, a loss of 0.026, with tie-corrected correlations against symmetric RA of 0.648 and 0.816. Reciprocity is 45.5% of directed link pairs, so the variants are largely redundant. (c) A factual fix: HUB_DEGREE in graph-signals.ts is 40, not the 20 the proposal's measurement section assumes. What is worth salvaging is the ReFeX/RolX half, reframed: a cheap role vector (out-degree, in-degree, ratio, clustering coefficient, mean neighbour degree, two aggregation rounds) k-meansed into ~6 roles is the only NLP-free route to the owner's priority A, because a person-note has a distinctive structural signature (high in-degree from daily notes, low out-degree, short body). Measure roles as a TYPING feature, not a ranking channel.

**Why it is not redundant.** The undirected collapse is lossy, so directed variants must carry information; the measurement says the information is not predictive here. Roles are independent of both channels but are a labelling signal, not a scoring one.

**Kill experiment.** For the four-variant fusion: dead, on the repaired protocol, unless a fitted weight vector validated on >= 3 vaults beats symmetric RA by >= 0.01 on all three — and the lab vault has only 24 date-named notes and one real link graph, so those vaults do not exist yet. For roles: hold out a hand-labelled set of 30 person-notes and 30 MOCs across donor vaults and report precision/recall of the role clusters; KILL below 0.7 precision on the person role.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Measured negative for the directed fusion on the only vault available. Fitting four weights on one vault is precisely the overfitting the project already rejected once when it cut the personal similarity metric. The role half is unmeasured and depends on donor vaults with actual people-notes; the lab vault has 9 notes under real/Personal.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1. Roles need donor vaults with real person-notes.


### 8. Hyperbolic hierarchy embedding — DOWNGRADED, the order-extraction inputs are empty here

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** The list distinguishes 'broader topic' from 'example of it', plus a card: 'this note has no parent'.

**How.** The idea is the one genuinely new axis on the list — cosine and RA are both symmetric and neither can say which of two related notes is the parent. The extraction is the problem. Of its three order sources: transclusion gives ZERO pairs (I count 0 ![[ ]] embeds in the entire 528-note vault); the folder tree is already known not to work, and index-store.ts:189-190 records that the same-folder signal was REMOVED because 'in an atomic-note vault the whole library lives in ~3 folders' — confirmed, real/Concepts alone holds 140 of 528 notes. That leaves the third rule (A links B without reciprocation and A's out-degree greatly exceeds B's), a heuristic, as the sole source. So the partial order would be fitted almost entirely on one hand-written rule, and the proposed AUC-against-folder-ancestry test would then be close to circular if folders also feed training.

**Why it is not redundant.** Correct and unaffected: abstraction level is orthogonal to both existing channels. Radius in the ball is a per-note generality scalar neither channel can produce.

**Kill experiment.** Corrected: extract the order from link asymmetry ONLY, then validate against BOTH held-out folder ancestry (AUC from radius difference) and a blind judged protocol (30 confidently-ordered pairs, 'is the second note more general', require >= 75%). Reporting only the folder AUC would be circular. Do not report this on link recall — it would fail for the wrong reason.

**Most likely reason it fails.** On the measured vault, two of three order sources are empty, so the model would be fitting one heuristic's output and the evaluation would largely re-measure that heuristic. Poincare embeddings are also burn-in and initialisation sensitive, which is unpleasant for something that must be reproducible on a user's machine after an idle-time rebuild.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1. Needs a donor vault that actually uses transclusion.


### 9. Type constraints as an abstention gate — DEAD as specified; replace with score-level abstention

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Was: fewer nonsense cards, sometimes an explicit 'nothing confident here'.

**How.** The goal is right and is the mechanism by which precision-first stops being a vibe. The specified implementation cannot work: it gates on the empirical distribution of object ROLES per relation r, so it needs both proposal 3 (|R| measured at about 2) and proposal 5's roles (unmeasured). With |R|=2 there is no domain or range to constrain, and every constraint has support in the single digits on a 500-note vault. The cited evidence is also not evidence about this system: xERTE predicting the subject as the object is a documented failure of neural temporal-KG models that this plugin will never run. Replace it with score-level abstention, which needs no relation vocabulary at all — see the calibration proposal below, and note the sequencing constraint recorded there.

**Why it is not redundant.** A filter cannot be redundant with a channel; it trades recall for precision. That argument stands and is why the goal should survive even though this mechanism should not.

**Kill experiment.** If anyone revives it: report precision@5 and recall@10 jointly plus the suppression rate on the repaired harness; SHIP only if precision@5 rises while recall@10 falls by < 0.01 with the paired 15-seed se; KILL above 30% suppression. On current data it cannot reach the starting line.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Depends on two prerequisites that are measured-empty and unmeasured respectively. On a 500-note vault every type constraint has thin support and the gate would silence correct answers — the precise opposite of its purpose.

**Needs first.** Proposals 3 and 5, both of which currently fail.


### 10. ComplEx-N3 at d=32 in the worker — KEEP as a killable experiment, but the 100k budget arithmetic is wrong

*expensive · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing new in the UI. A third score behind the existing list, which either helps or does not.

**How.** Keep the design and keep the willingness to kill it. One load-bearing correction: the compute budget at scale is off by more than two orders of magnitude. The parameter estimate is fine (100k notes at d=32 complex is 100k * 64 floats = 25 MB float32, comfortably inside the 4 GiB cap). The training estimate is not. Full softmax over entities costs about 4*d multiply-accumulates per entity per triple, so at N=1e5, d=32, 4e5 triples and 200 epochs the cost is roughly 1e15 MACs, which is days in WASM SIMD, not the 'minutes' claimed — the proposal's own 1.3e10 figure is correct at the 500-entity lab scale and was extrapolated without accounting for the softmax growing with N. Full softmax does not survive to 100k notes; it would have to be replaced by negative sampling, which is exactly what N3-with-full-softmax exists to avoid. So the honest framing is: this is a small-vault experiment with no scaling story unless the loss changes.

**Why it is not redundant.** ComplEx factorises the typed adjacency tensor and can generalise across relations in a way path counting cannot. Whether that survives on a 1062-edge training graph with |R| about 2 is what the experiment decides, and both the proposer's prior and mine are that it does not.

**Kill experiment.** Repaired harness, paired multi-seed, plus tie-corrected correlation against both RA and any rule channel. Keep the sample-size curve (train on 25/50/75/100% of links) as the real deliverable rather than a pass/fail. Since donor vaults above 5k notes with real link history are the binding constraint, run the curve additionally on synthetic graphs with known planted structure to locate the crossover with rule mining.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Median out-degree 5, |R| about 2 so it is plain graph embedding, and 1062 training edges. Degree bias says almost every entity in a personal vault gets a poor representation. Ruffinelli et al. says results are dominated by training hyperparameters, which cannot be tuned blind on a user's machine. Most likely dominated by RA at every vault size a user actually has.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 (currently failing).


### 11. NodePiece anchors — REFRAMED away from its dead prerequisite

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** 'This note sits between [[Stochastik MOC]] and [[Analysis MOC]]' as a receipt, and a note created thirty seconds ago with one link already gets structural suggestions instead of falling back to content-only.

**How.** As infrastructure for ComplEx it inherits that proposal's speculative status and should not be built. Reframed without the KGE: pick ~5% of notes as anchors by degree plus PageRank plus random, and represent every note by its k nearest anchors and the truncated-walk distance to each. That is a cheap coarse coordinate system with two immediate uses that need no trained model — a human-readable positional receipt, and a cold-start structural sketch for notes with degree <= 2, which is where the existing RA channel emits nothing at all. It composes with the existing PPR_FRONTIER_MAX = 400 cap since anchor discovery is a truncated walk.

**Why it is not redundant.** The cold-start stratum is the specific gap: for degree <= 2 sources the graph channel is silent, so anything that fires there is independent of it by construction rather than by correlation. That stratum is also exactly what the repaired harness is supposed to start reporting.

**Kill experiment.** Cold-start stratum on the repaired protocol: source notes with degree <= 2, paired multi-seed, anchor sketch versus content-only. KILL if it does not beat content-only on that stratum, since it has no other job. For the receipt, blind judged precision on 20 anchor pairs: 'do these two notes describe where this note sits'. Also report index size and rebuild wall time on a 100k synthetic vault; KILL if rebuild exceeds the existing idle budget.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Anchor selection is degree-biased, so anchors will be MOCs and the receipt may say nothing the vault map does not already say. Worth checking against the existing spherical k-means cluster labels before building.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1. No longer depends on ComplEx.


### 12. Typed path features (PRA-lite) — DOWNGRADED, blocked on the same empty vocabulary

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing new visible; a better-informed structural score.

**How.** Unchanged in design, and its self-kill rule is the best-designed criterion in the original list: report the fitted weight vector, and if more than 80% of the weight mass sits on the untyped length-2 path, the method IS RA and should be cut regardless of the recall number. Keep that rule. The blockers are two: |R| measured at about 2 collapses the feature space to a handful of near-duplicate columns, and 'a single global weight vector fitted once by the developer across several vaults' requires several vaults with real link graphs, which do not exist in this project today — there is one lab vault, and its own timestamps are unusable.

**Why it is not redundant.** RA is one path type with a hard-coded weight; PRA learns a weight per path type. Sound in principle, and the negative result would be genuinely informative.

**Kill experiment.** Repaired harness, paired multi-seed, plus the weight-mass rule above as a hard gate independent of recall. Path enumeration must apply the HUB_DEGREE = 40 cutoff to intermediates exactly as evidence() already does, or it will explode on hub-heavy vaults.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Blocked twice over: no relation vocabulary and no multi-vault fitting corpus. Sourcing 3 or more donor vaults with real link history is the actual prerequisite for this and for proposals 5, 8 and 12's forward split — it is one sourcing problem gating four proposals, and it should be treated as its own work item.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 plus 3 or more donor vaults.


### 13. Recency-weighted co-activity (Recurrency Baseline) — KEEP, and it rises in relative priority

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** 'You keep coming back to these two together.' A pair you have never linked, that are not textually similar, and that share no neighbours, but that you open in the same sitting again and again.

**How.** Keep as designed: opt-in local ring-buffer event log of file-open and modify events, sessions split on 30-minute gaps, strict recurrency with exponential decay plus the relaxed prior term, two hyperparameters, no training, recomputable per query. Route the score through the monotone boost path rather than z-add, per the combiner proposal — as a sparse channel it would otherwise pay the 0.017 to 0.052 artefact penalty and look like a failure.

**Why it is not redundant.** This is the strongest independence argument on the entire list and my measurements reinforce it: it is the only proposal whose evidence is not already sitting in the vault files, so it cannot be redundant by construction. Every other candidate I measured (tags, co-mention, mentions, phantoms) is a rearrangement of text and links the other channels already see, and all of them came in between -0.05 and +0.006.

**Kill experiment.** The existing protocol genuinely cannot test this, and the lab vault is worse than the proposal assumed: all 528 notes share 3 distinct mtime days, and only 24 date-named notes exist carrying 1.1% of resolved links, so there is no retrospective signal at all. Forward protocol as specified: instrument a live vault 6-8 weeks, fit on weeks 1..N, predict links CREATED in weeks N+1..N+2, report recall@10 for content+RA versus content+RA+co-activity. Make the recency-only control the PRIMARY baseline, not an afterthought — if 'both were edited recently' explains the gain, this is a recency ranker wearing a relatedness hat. KILL if it does not beat both content+RA and the recency control, or if tie-corrected correlation with RA exceeds 0.5.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Three real ones: privacy optics demand a loud explicit opt-in even though nothing leaves the machine; the cold start is measured in weeks, which is anti-magical on day one and needs a graceful empty state; and co-opening may simply track recency. Add a fourth: the 6-8 week instrumentation must start NOW if the result is wanted this quarter, because it is the only proposal whose measurement has an irreducible wall-clock cost.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1 for the forward-split harness. Start the instrumentation in parallel with everything else.


### 14. Calibrated abstention — KEEP, with a sequencing constraint the proposal missed

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** When the vault has nothing to say, the panel says so instead of padding the list. Cards carry a quiet confidence mark.

**How.** Keep isotonic or Platt calibration from score to P(real link), shipped as a constant table, with a single per-vault scalar offset. One correction that changes the order of work: the quantity the proposal wants to calibrate does not exist yet. index-store.ts:2628-2631 z-normalises BOTH channels across the current candidate pool, so s.fused is pool-relative by construction — the same pair scores differently depending on which other candidates were shortlisted, and a global probability table cannot be attached to it. The displayed score is a different quantity again (semantic + boost, clamped by Math.min(1, ...)), and that clamp ties the top of the distribution, destroying calibration exactly where the confident predictions live. So: first make the ranker emit a pool-independent score and remove or soften the clamp, then calibrate. This also pairs with the abstention goal orphaned from the type-constraint proposal, and note the tension with the combiner fix — a monotone-only boost cannot express negative evidence, so abstention has to live at the score threshold rather than inside the combiner.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. Its value is downstream: it makes every future channel's admission cheaper to judge, because a channel that raises recall while wrecking calibration becomes visible immediately.

**Kill experiment.** Reliability diagram and expected calibration error on the repaired protocol, plus the full precision/coverage curve. SHIP if ECE < 0.05 and precision@shown rises by >= 5 points for <= 10% coverage loss at the chosen threshold. KILL if the curve is flat — which would itself be a significant finding about the fusion.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Calibration fitted on the developer's vaults may not transfer; link density and score distribution shift with vault size, and the single per-vault offset may not absorb that. The real risk is sequencing: calibrating the current pool-relative fused score would produce a table that silently means nothing.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2, plus the pool-independent-score refactor described above.


### 15. Typed suggestion slots (rank for the heading you are standing under) — DEAD on measured data

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Was: the panel header changes to 'for ## Prerequisites' and the list changes with it.

**How.** The observable defect it names is real — two sections of the same note get an identical list because every channel is unconditioned. The mechanism cannot be built on this vault. 58.2% of resolved link occurrences sit under no heading at all. Of the remainder, the largest link-bearing headings are sections of the plugin's own generated report. After excluding those, the only content headings with support >= 30 links are 'introduction' (202) and 'einfuehrung' (42) — which are the same relation in two languages, and a generic one at that. The proposal's own kill bar (fewer than 3 relations with support >= 30 means kill the feature) fails on the measured data before any code is written.

**Why it is not redundant.** Conditioning on r changes the candidate set rather than reweighting it, so the independence claim was sound. There is simply no r.

**Kill experiment.** Already answered by the proposal 3 coverage study. If donor vaults show a real heading vocabulary, the original per-relation protocol is correct as written: mask only links occurring under heading r and compare the r-conditional ranker against the global ranker on exactly those links, reported per relation, never pooled.

**Most likely reason it fails.** With |R| about 2 this is an expensive way to render a different header string. Blocked behind proposals 3 and 4, both of which currently fail.

**Needs first.** Proposals 3 and 4.


### 16. NEW: provenance-aware graph construction — the plugin is currently poisoning the graph it learns from

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible, except that suggestions stop being quietly influenced by the plugin's own previous suggestions.

**How.** The 'Vault insights' command writes a report note into the vault. In the lab vault that file contains 388 wikilinks — 16% of all 2,363 resolved link occurrences — giving it degree 205 when the next largest node is 38. It is the ONLY node in the vault above HUB_DEGREE, it accounts for 13.3% of all undirected edges, and it is the sole shared neighbour for 75.7% of note pairs that have any shared neighbour at all. Its 'Suggested links' section prints '[[A]] and [[B]]' pairs on single lines, so it also manufactures 8,177 of the vault's 11,270 paragraph co-mention pairs. Today the product survives this by luck: HUB_DEGREE = 40 excludes it from evidence() and pprFrom() skips it during expansion. That is a threshold coincidence, not a design. Fix: tag plugin-authored notes at write time (a frontmatter marker plus a known path) and exclude them from adjacency construction in GraphSignals.ensure() by provenance, not by degree, so every future structural channel inherits the exclusion automatically. Do the same for any note the user marks as generated.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel — a correctness fix. Its value is that it removes a feedback loop in which the ranker's output becomes the ranker's input, which is the single most damaging failure mode available to a system that writes into its own corpus.

**Kill experiment.** Ablation on the real IndexStore, not a reimplementation: build the graph with and without provenance exclusion on a vault where the insights report has been generated, and report the change in RA evidence coverage, in top-10 composition, and in the fraction of pairs whose only shared neighbour is a generated note. There is no recall bar to clear — the correct behaviour is defined, not measured. Add a regression test asserting that no plugin-authored note ever appears in evidence().shared.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Low. The main subtlety is that older vaults contain reports written before the marker existed, so path-based and heading-signature fallbacks are needed. Note also that the lab bench does NOT currently see this note (it is absent from the embedding cache), so every bench number to date is clean while the shipped product is not — which is its own reason to test on the real store.

**Needs first.** None.


### 17. NEW: backlink-context representation — how the rest of the vault describes this note

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** Matches that fire on your own phrasing rather than the note's: 'you describe this elsewhere as "the trick where you subtract the mean" — this other note says the same thing in the same words.' The receipt is the sentence you wrote.

**How.** The KGE-native idea the first researcher missed: entity descriptions. Every note currently gets one representation, built from its own text. Build a second one from the sentences in which OTHER notes link to it — the anchor sentence around each incoming wikilink, pooled over backlinks. This is the citation-context representation from citation recommendation, and it costs one extra embedding pass over a small set of short spans (the lab vault has 2,363 link occurrences, so about that many sentences, far fewer than the existing chunk budget). Score a pair by the max or mean cosine between A's context vectors and B's own vector, and symmetrically. No new model, no LLM, reuses the existing chunk pipeline, int8 quantiser and mean-centering. Link positions come from metadataCache getFileCache().links[i].position, which nothing in the plugin currently reads.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is neither channel. Content similarity compares what two notes say about themselves; this compares what the author says about them in passing, which is systematically different text — shorter, more colloquial, and often the connective tissue the note itself omits. It is not topology either: the RA channel sees that a link exists but never what was written around it, and a note with three backlinks contributes three descriptions regardless of who its neighbours are. It should behave best exactly where the two existing channels are weakest — short notes and stubs, whose own text is too thin to embed well, but which others describe richly.

**Kill experiment.** Repaired harness, paired multi-seed, routed through the monotone boost path per the combiner proposal. Report the headline delta, the tie-corrected correlation against both content and RA, and — the diagnostic that matters — the delta on the low-content-confidence stratum, since contentConfidence() already exists in index-store.ts and identifies exactly the notes this should rescue. KILL if the paired delta is under +0.01 with the 15-seed se AND the low-confidence stratum does not improve. Honest caveat: I could not measure this in this session because the embedding cache is keyed by whole-note text and I cannot embed new spans without running the model, so unlike the other candidates here this one is genuinely untested.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Leakage discipline is the main hazard: the anchor sentence usually contains the link, and for a held-out pair the bracketed target name must be stripped before embedding or the channel reads the answer — the existing bench already does exactly this for BM25 and the same rule applies. Second risk: many backlinks sit in bare list items ('- [[X]]') with no surrounding prose, which yields empty contexts; on the lab vault 501 paragraphs are multi-link blocks, many of them list dumps, so coverage may be thinner than the 2,363 occurrences suggest. Third: it is not free at index time, unlike everything else that survived this review.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2. Needs link positions from metadataCache, shared with proposal 3.


### 18. NEW: daily-note co-occurrence as temporal hyperedges — priority A without an event log

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** 'These two turned up in your notes the same week.' Time-shaped relatedness with no logging, no opt-in, no cold start — it works on the history already in the vault.

**How.** The owner's first priority is PEOPLE, PLACES and TIME, and the original list addresses TIME only through the co-activity proposal, which needs 6-8 weeks of instrumentation before it says anything. There is a retrospective version that needs neither: date-named notes are timestamps the user wrote by hand. Treat each daily or weekly note as a hyperedge over the notes it links, and score pairs by co-occurrence within a time window, decayed by age, with each daily note weighted 1/(|links|-1) so a long day contributes little. This also yields a per-note time profile (the dates on which it was linked) which is the substrate for 'you were working on this last spring' and for the PEOPLE half, since person-notes in a personal vault are overwhelmingly linked from dailies — the structural signature the roles half of proposal 5 was reaching for, obtained directly rather than via clustering.

**Why it is not redundant.** Time is not in either channel. RA sees a daily note as an undirected neighbour with no notion of when, and the content channel sees a date-named note as a short document about nothing in particular. Two notes linked from the same week share a fact about the author's attention that neither existing channel can represent. Unlike the co-activity proposal it also requires no new data collection, so it has no privacy surface and no cold start.

**Kill experiment.** Two protocols. (a) Repaired harness, paired multi-seed, monotone combiner, plus the tie-corrected correlation against RA — with daily notes excluded from the RA adjacency in both arms so the comparison is not trivially self-referential. (b) The forward split from proposal 1, where this channel is the natural first customer. KILL if the paired delta is under +0.01 or if correlation with RA exceeds 0.6, which would mean daily-note co-occurrence is just a proxy for being co-linked.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Measured, and it is the reason this is listed as cheap-and-uncertain rather than a headline: the lab vault has only 24 date-named notes carrying 1.1% of resolved links, spanning 2026-01-10 to 2026-06-30, so it cannot be evaluated here at all. This is a donor-vault-first proposal, and the donor needs to be from someone who actually keeps a daily-note habit. If the target user does not, the channel is silent — which under a precision-first policy is an acceptable failure mode but a poor use of engineering time. Check the daily-note density of 3 donor vaults before writing code.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2, plus donor vaults with a real daily-note habit — the same sourcing item that gates proposals 5, 8, 10 and 12.


## Graph neural networks and learned link prediction


<details><summary>17 techniques surveyed</summary>

- **Resource Allocation index (RA)** (established, 2009) — Sum over shared neighbours x of 1/deg(x). The degree penalty is what makes it beat raw common-neighbour counting. Already shipped as the project's graph channel; I re-measured it at +0.13 recall@10 over content alone on a correct split, which is ~7 seed-SD and unambiguously real.  
  <sub>Zhou, Lu & Zhang, 'Predicting missing links via local information', European Physical Journal B 71(4), 2009. Verified from memory; no arXiv id asserted (this session's web search budget was exhausted before I could confirm).</sub>
- **Adamic-Adar and the RA_beta family** (established, 2003) — Generalises the degree penalty to 1/deg(x)^beta (beta=0 common neighbours, beta=1 RA, AA uses 1/log deg). HL-GNN's framing treats the whole family as one learnable term. I swept beta on the lab vault: total spread across beta in [0.5, 2.0] is 0.005 recall@10, four times under the seed SD. The family is flat here.  
  <sub>Adamic & Adar, 'Friends and neighbors on the Web', Social Networks 25(3), 2003; family formulation per HL-GNN below.</sub>
- **L3 / length-3 path index** (established, 2019) — Scores pairs by paths of length 3 weighted 1/sqrt(deg(x)*deg(y)). Claimed to beat CN/AA/RA in networks governed by complementarity rather than triadic closure (protein interaction). I implemented and measured it: on this vault it correlates 0.767 with RA and REDUCES precision on the RA=0 subgroup it was designed to serve. The literature result does not transfer to a sparse average-degree-5 note graph.  
  <sub>Kovacs et al., 'Network-based prediction of protein interactions', Nature Communications 10:1240, 2019.</sub>
- **CH2-L3 / Cannistraci-Hebb local community paradigm** (recent-but-replicated, 2021) — Refines L3 by reweighting intermediates on internal vs external links relative to the pair's local community. Untested by me because plain L3 failed three gates first; CH2 is a refinement of a term that is already redundant here.  
  <sub>Muscoloni, Michieli & Cannistraci, local-community-paradigm link prediction (exact venue/id not verified this session — verify the CH2 formula against the primary source before implementing, as the proposal itself warned).</sub>
- **HeaRT hard-negative evaluation protocol** (recent-but-replicated, 2023) — Replaces ranking against all nodes with ranking against a per-edge pool of hard negatives, on the argument that easy negatives saturate the metric and hide differences between methods. Relevant here but for a different reason than claimed: this vault's content-only recall@10 is 0.655, nowhere near saturation, so the saturation argument does not apply. The real problem it fixes is statistical power on subgroups.  
  <sub>Li et al., 'Evaluating Graph Neural Networks for Link Prediction: Current Pitfalls and New Benchmarking', NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks track. Title/venue from memory and from the prior researcher's brief; arXiv id not verified this session.</sub>
- **SEAL and the labelling trick** (established, 2018) — Per-node distance labels relative to the target pair are what make a GNN link-expressive; the GNN wrapped around them is the expensive part. Motivates extracting the labels alone. I did: landmark-distance L1 profiles add +0.002 recall@10, nine times under the seed SD. The labels do not survive separation from the model on this graph.  
  <sub>Zhang & Chen, 'Link Prediction Based on Graph Neural Networks', NeurIPS 2018; Zhang, Li, Xia, Wang & Jin, 'Labeling Trick: A Theory of Using Graph Neural Networks for Multi-Node Representation Learning', NeurIPS 2021.</sub>
- **NBFNet generalised Bellman-Ford path representation** (established, 2021) — Represents a pair as a generalised sum over paths of generalised products of edge representations. With learned operators it is a 129 s/epoch model; with FIXED (max, product) operators it degenerates to a cheap best-path search with predecessor reconstruction. That degenerate form is the only part worth taking, and its value is as a receipt, not a score.  
  <sub>Zhu, Zhang, Xhonneux & Tang, 'Neural Bellman-Ford Networks: A General Graph Neural Network Framework for Link Prediction', NeurIPS 2021.</sub>
- **DotHash quasi-orthogonal set-similarity sketching** (recent-but-replicated, 2023) — Random +/-1/sqrt(F) node signatures whose inner products estimate |CN| unbiasedly, and which — unlike MinHash — support pre-scaling by 1/deg to estimate RA and Adamic-Adar directly. Genuinely elegant. Irrelevant here: max degree on this vault is 37, so exact set intersection is over sets of under 40 elements and already costs microseconds.  
  <sub>Nunes et al., 'DotHash: Estimating Set Similarity Metrics for Link Prediction and Document Deduplication', KDD 2023. Venue from memory and the prior brief; arXiv id not verified this session.</sub>
- **MPLP message-passing link predictor with QO vectors** (frontier-unproven, 2024) — Adds the hub fix DotHash lacks: reserve dimensions as deterministic one-hot slots for the highest-degree nodes, since hubs dominate the estimator variance. Same verdict as DotHash — the variance problem it solves never arises at degree 37.  
  <sub>Message-passing link predictor using quasi-orthogonal vectors, 2024 (exact author list and venue not verified this session).</sub>
- **HL-GNN heuristic learning graph neural network** (frontier-unproven, 2024) — Learns a handful of coefficients over a unified heuristic family H = sum_l beta^(l) prod A^(m) rather than a full GNN: 0.06 s/epoch against SEAL's 28.7 s and NBFNet's 129 s. The architectural lesson is sound and cheap. Its applicability here is limited by how few surviving independent features there are to combine.  
  <sub>Heuristic Learning Graph Neural Network, KDD 2024 (author list not verified this session).</sub>
- **Untrained / parameter-free message passing (UTGIN family)** (frontier-unproven, 2024) — Propagating high-dimensional features over the normalised adjacency without training often matches or beats trained MPNNs on link prediction; under orthonormal initialisation the inner product of propagated features is exactly a count of length-2l paths. I implemented Z = sum_l beta_l S^l X on the real note vectors and swept beta: best variant reaches 0.780 against content+RA's 0.786. It re-derives the graph channel rather than adding to it.  
  <sub>Untrained message passing for link prediction, 2024-2025 line of work (specific paper not verified this session; the empirical result I report is my own measurement, not a citation).</sub>
- **GNN-to-MLP distillation for link prediction (LLP)** (recent-but-replicated, 2023) — Trains a graph-free student from a graph-aware teacher, so nodes with no edges still get graph-quality predictions. Aimed exactly at the orphan-note population. Undecidable here: only 38 of 530 held-out directed instances have a source of degree <= 1.  
  <sub>Guo et al., 'Linkless Link Prediction via Relational Distillation', ICML 2023.</sub>
- **Forman-Ricci curvature** (established, 2003) — Closed-form discrete curvature, F(s,t) = 4 - deg(s) - deg(t) + 3|CN(s,t)| for the augmented unweighted form. Negatively curved edges bridge communities. I measured its Spearman correlation with deg(s)+deg(t) on this vault: -0.916. It is a degree statistic, exactly as the proposal's own kill criterion anticipated.  
  <sub>Forman, 'Bochner's method for cell complexes and combinatorial Ricci curvature', Discrete & Computational Geometry 29, 2003; community-detection use per Ni et al., Ricci flow on graphs, 2019.</sub>
- **EdgeBank memorisation baseline** (established, 2022) — A pure 'predict what was seen before' baseline that embarrasses many dynamic link predictors. The right discipline check for any temporal or co-editing channel: beat 'show whatever was recently open together' before claiming a model.  
  <sub>Poursafaei, Huang, Pelrine & Rabbany, 'Towards Better Evaluation for Dynamic Link Prediction', NeurIPS 2022 Datasets & Benchmarks track.</sub>
- **SpotTarget target-link leakage** (recent-but-replicated, 2024) — Training-time inclusion of the target edge (or of structural features computed before the split) silently leaks the answer. I audited the harnesses: adjacency is correctly built from train only in graph-fusion-eval.mjs and v4-channels.mjs. No leakage found; the split bug is separate and real.  
  <sub>SpotTarget, target-link leakage in GNN link prediction, 2024 (author list not verified this session).</sub>
- **PathSim meta-path similarity** (established, 2011) — 2*|shared|/(|refs(s)|+|refs(t)|) normalisation for bipartite projections, the standard fix for hub intermediates when projecting a notes x references graph back onto notes. Directly applicable to the phantom-node layer proposed below.  
  <sub>Sun, Han, Yan, Yu & Wu, 'PathSim: Meta Path-Based Top-K Similarity Search in Heterogeneous Information Networks', VLDB 2011.</sub>
- **Bibliographic coupling and co-citation** (established, 1963) — The two directed halves of an undirected shared-neighbour count. Coupling = both cite x; co-citation = both cited by x. I measured their Spearman correlation on this vault at 0.360, so collapsing them to one symmetric score does discard a distinction — whether the distinction pays is still untested.  
  <sub>Kessler 1963 (coupling); Small 1973 (co-citation); modern directed-link-prediction framing per the prior researcher's brief.</sub>

</details>


### 1. Fix the held-out split, and publish the seed SD with every number (KEPT, strengthened)

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible. Every accept/reject decision in the project gets an error bar for the first time.

**How.** Confirmed present in all ten harnesses: bench/graph-fusion-eval.mjs:60, graph-fusion-eval2.mjs:57, levers-eval.mjs:66, v3-crowding.mjs:187, v3-calibration.mjs:75, v3-levers.mjs:158, v4-channels.mjs:73, v4-metric.mjs:84, v3-map.mjs:72, ideas-eval.mjs:143 and :262. Replace `[...edges].sort(() => rnd() - 0.5)` with seeded Fisher-Yates using the existing mulberry32. I re-measured the bias directly rather than assuming it: at N=1327 the comparator shuffle leaves corr(original index, shuffled index) = 0.065 and puts 22.5% of the held-out set in the first 20% of file order against a uniform 20.0%. So the per-edge bias is MILDER than the proposal's 'strongly correlated' framing. But the effect on conclusions is LARGER than the proposal claims: over 12 seeds, the comparator split gives content-only 0.6774 +/- 0.0327 and content+RA 0.7618 +/- 0.0305, while Fisher-Yates gives 0.6549 +/- 0.0210 and 0.7858 +/- 0.0184. The bug inflates content-only by 0.023 and deflates content+RA by 0.022 — it UNDERSTATES the graph channel by 0.045, about 2.2 seed-SD — and it inflates variance by ~55%. I also audited for SpotTarget-style target leakage and found none: adjacency is built from train only in both graph-fusion-eval.mjs and v4-channels.mjs. Correct the proposal's emphasis: the deliverable is not the shuffle, it is the error bar.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. It is the instrument every admission decision was made on, including the RA accept at +0.081 and the lexical reject at rho=0.30.

**Kill experiment.** Already done, reported above. The number to institutionalise: seed-to-seed SD is 0.021 for content-only and 0.018 for content+RA at this vault size. Publish mean +/- SD over >= 8 seeds for every future number, and treat any claimed gain under 0.02 as unmeasured. Re-decide any past accept/reject whose margin was under 0.02 — by that rule the RA accept (+0.13 on a correct split) is safe and the lexical reject (rho=0.30, a correlation not a delta) is safe, so I expect no reversals, but the check is an afternoon.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Low. The realistic downside is a day spent confirming that the headline results hold, which is the correct thing to know before building on them. The one live risk is that the published 0.664 -> 0.745 pair was produced under the buggy split, so the true graph-channel contribution is probably LARGER than advertised and the fusion weight may be mistuned downward.

**Needs first.** None. Gates everything else.


### 2. Pre-registered subgroup reporting with stated n, instead of the HeaRT framing (CORRECTED)

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible. Channels stop being judged on a global average that cannot see the population they target.

**How.** The proposal's premise is wrong for this corpus: HeaRT exists because easy negatives SATURATE the metric, but content-only recall@10 here is 0.655, nowhere near saturation, so the saturation argument does not apply and a hard-negative pool is not the fix. The real problem is statistical power, and I can show it. Every structural channel differs from content only on a small subpopulation: I measured that RA is exactly zero on 21.7% of held-out edges, and RA=0 AND L3=0 on 9.7%. A global recall@10 with SD 0.021 physically cannot resolve a channel that only moves 20% of the instances. So the fix is subgroup reporting, not negative sampling. Mandate three pre-registered strata on every future evaluation, each with its n printed next to it: (a) RA(s,t)=0 — I measure n=117 held-out instances, usable; (b) source degree <= 1 — I measure n=38 of 530, which is NOT usable and must be reported as undecidable rather than as a null result; (c) cross-component pairs. Keep the hard-negative pool as a secondary MRR report if it is cheap, but do not let it become the primary gate, and if it is built, fix the pool once from content cosine and RA only and never regenerate it per candidate channel.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is the discriminator, not a channel. The concrete evidence that it changes conclusions is in this review: L3 looks merely useless on the global metric (0.7818 vs 0.7858, inside noise) and looks actively harmful on the subgroup it was designed for (0.461 vs 0.559). Only the stratified view produces the kill.

**Kill experiment.** Self-validating, and partly already run. Re-run the decided channels stratified: RA must stay accepted (it does — +0.13 globally), shared-tags must stay rejected (rho=0.916 with graph, unchanged by stratification). The new requirement is that any proposal whose target subgroup has n < ~100 is reported as UNDECIDABLE AT LAB SCALE, not as a null.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Subgroup reporting multiplies the number of numbers and invites cherry-picking the stratum that looks good. Mitigate by fixing the three strata in the harness before running anything, and reporting all three every time regardless of outcome.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1.


### 3. Split the graph channel by link direction: bibliographic coupling vs co-citation (KEPT)

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** A more honest receipt: 'you and this note both cite [[X]]' is a different claim from 'both of you are cited by [[X]]', and the card can say which.

**How.** Unchanged from the original: graph-signals.ts:99-105 symmetrises resolvedLinks on build and computes one RA over the merged neighbourhood. Keep the direction and compute RA_couple(s,t) = sum over x in Out(s) ∩ Out(t) of 1/|In(x)|, and RA_cocite(s,t) = sum over x in In(s) ∩ In(t) of 1/|Out(x)|, fused as separate z-normalised terms. I pre-checked the one thing that could make this pointless: Spearman(RA_couple, RA_cocite) over a 6262-pair candidate pool is 0.360, comfortably under the proposal's own 0.70 futility bar, so the decomposition is not vacuous. Coverage in the pool: RA_couple nonzero on 7.2% of pairs, RA_cocite on 10.3%, merged RA on 15.4% — the two halves are close to disjoint, which is the good case. Each half is individually well correlated with merged RA (0.696 and 0.785), so this will not be a large win; it is a cheap refinement plus a better receipt.

**Why it is not redundant.** It adds no new information source and makes no independence claim — it is a decomposition, and the correlation check above is what earns it a day of work rather than a rejection on principle.

**Kill experiment.** Held-out recall@10 over >= 8 Fisher-Yates seeds for: symmetric RA (baseline 0.7858 +/- 0.0184 in my harness); RA_couple alone; RA_cocite alone; both as separate swept-weight terms. Kill unless the two-term fusion beats symmetric RA by more than 0.02 (one seed SD). Independent of the ranking outcome, ship the directional receipt if the shared neighbour is directionally unambiguous — that costs nothing and is strictly more informative than the current 'via X'.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Most likely outcome is a gain inside the noise floor, because each half correlates ~0.7-0.79 with the merged score. Splitting also halves the evidence per term, which on a 494-note vault makes each half noisier. The receipt improvement is the part I would actually bet on.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2.


### 4. HUB_DEGREE=40 is unexercised, not unswept — this is a corpus problem, not a tuning problem (CORRECTED)

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The panel would behave the same on a 500-note and a 50,000-note vault. Currently nobody knows whether it does.

**How.** The proposal frames three constants in graph-signals.ts (HUB_DEGREE=40 at line 56, PPR_STEPS=3 at line 50, PPR_FRONTIER_MAX=400 at line 58) as unswept and proposes a beta sweep plus a percentile-based hub rule. I ran the beta sweep: RA_beta with beta in {0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0} gives recall@10 of 0.7825, 0.7828, 0.7858, 0.7866, 0.7873 — a total spread of 0.005, four times under the seed SD of 0.018. The beta half is dead; RA is the right member of its own family, which is why it is the standard baseline. But I found something worse than an unswept constant. The lab vault's degree distribution is p50=5, p90=9, p95=11, p99=28, MAX=37. Zero notes exceed HUB_DEGREE=40. The hub cut at graph-signals.ts:139 and the hub-skip in the PPR walk at line 166 have NEVER FIRED in any measurement this project has published. Every number in the graph-signals.ts header comment was produced with the hub logic inert. So the correct action is not a sweep — it is to acquire a corpus where degree 40 is reachable, and only then decide between a percentile rule and the fixed constant. Until then, HUB_DEGREE is untested code shipping to users with 100,000-note vaults where it will fire constantly.

**Why it is not redundant.** Same channel, no independence claim. Reclassified from a tuning task to a coverage gap in the measurement instrument, which is a different and more urgent kind of problem.

**Kill experiment.** Do not sweep beta again; it is flat and I have the numbers. Instead: (a) report the degree distribution of any vault before quoting a result from it, and flag when max degree < HUB_DEGREE; (b) on a vault that actually has hubs, compare the fixed cut against a degree-percentile cut (e.g. p99) on recall@10 AND on p95 query latency, since the PPR frontier blow-up the constant guards against is a latency property, not an accuracy one. Report optimal threshold per vault size — drift with size is the finding that would justify making it a function of the degree distribution.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The honest risk is that this reads as a nitpick and gets deferred. It should not be: an untested branch that only activates on large vaults is precisely the branch that will misbehave on the users the plugin claims to support, and no existing bench run can catch it.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1, plus a vault with max degree well above 40. Synthetic vaults will not produce a realistic degree tail.


### 5. Length-3 paths (L3 / CH2-L3) as a second structural score — DEAD

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. Measured and cut.

**How.** I implemented L3(s,t) = sum over paths s-x-y-t of 1/sqrt(deg(x)*deg(y)) exactly as specified and ran it through all three of the proposal's own gates. It fails every one. (1) Independence: Spearman(L3, RA) = 0.767 over a 6262-pair candidate pool. The proposal set its own admission bar at 0.30 — the same bar that killed the lexical channel at 0.30 — and L3 comes in at two and a half times that. (2) Fusion: content+RA+L3 = 0.7818 +/- 0.0190 against content+RA = 0.7858 +/- 0.0184. No gain; a slight loss, inside noise. (3) The decisive subgroup test the proposal itself nominated: restricted to held-out edges where RA(s,t)=0 (n=117), content alone scores 0.5591 +/- 0.0542 and content+L3 scores 0.4608 +/- 0.0739. L3 does not merely fail to help on the population it was designed for — it destroys 0.10 of recall there, roughly two SD. The diagnosis is in the coverage numbers: L3 is nonzero on 23.6% of pool pairs against RA's 15.4%, so it is not reaching a disjoint population, it is reaching a SUPERSET with more noise in it. The structural premise was also wrong: the proposal argued a vault is a complementarity network like protein interaction, but the L3 wins in that literature come from graphs with high average degree, and this vault averages 5.37.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not. That was the central claim and it is false by direct measurement: rho = 0.767 with the channel it was supposed to complement.

**Kill experiment.** Already run, on 8 Fisher-Yates seeds, at /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-justus-obsidian-atomized-intermediary/aed1296b-ccbe-44e2-8d33-3d9a1171972a/scratchpad/diag3.mjs. The one thing that could reopen this: a vault with substantially higher average degree, where length-3 paths are less degenerate. That is a corpus question, not an algorithm question, and it queues behind the corpus proposal below.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD. Fails its own pre-registered independence bar by 2.5x (rho=0.767 against a 0.30 bar), adds nothing in fusion, and actively reduces precision by 0.10 on the RA=0 subgroup it exists to serve. Killing it also removes the only justification for the sketch engine.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 6. Untrained message passing over note embeddings — DEAD by its own criterion

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. Measured and cut.

**How.** I built it: Z = sum over l of beta_l * S^l X with S = D^(-1/2)(A+I)D^(-1/2) over the mean-centred note vectors, renormalised, swept beta. Results over 8 seeds: beta=[1,0.5,0] gives 0.7224; beta=[1,0.5,0.25] (the proposal's suggested start) gives 0.7420; beta=[1,1,1] gives 0.7800; beta=[0,1,0] (pure one-hop, no self term) gives 0.7505. The incumbent content+RA gives 0.7858 +/- 0.0184. The proposal wrote its own kill condition: 'if Z alone never beats X+RA, cut it.' It never does — the best variant lands 0.006 below, and every weaker beta lands 0.04-0.06 below. The second half of its ablation also fires: Z+RA = 0.7823, no better than X+RA, so propagation has merely ABSORBED the graph channel rather than adding to it. Note the shape of the beta sweep, which is diagnostic: performance climbs monotonically as weight shifts onto the propagated terms, i.e. the mechanism is re-deriving RA through the embedding rather than discovering anything new. Best case was always an architectural swap at equal accuracy, and that swap is expensive: Z depends on the whole graph, so one new wikilink dirties every note's vector, which breaks the incremental idle-triggered indexing story outright, and it operates on note-level vectors so it can only touch the coarse shortlist that chunk-level BiMax then re-ranks anyway.

**Why it is not redundant.** It never claimed to be — the proposal was explicit that it is a replacement for the content channel judged end-to-end. Judged end-to-end, it loses.

**Kill experiment.** Already run, /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-justus-obsidian-atomized-intermediary/aed1296b-ccbe-44e2-8d33-3d9a1171972a/scratchpad/diag3.mjs, 8 Fisher-Yates seeds, four beta settings plus the Z+RA ablation. I did not need the top-1 concentration skew check because the recall gate failed first.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD. Loses to the incumbent on its own pre-registered criterion (best Z alone 0.780 vs X+RA 0.786), and its best case was a lateral move that would have cost the incremental-indexing guarantee — which on a 100,000-note vault is a first-class product property, not an implementation detail.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 7. Structural sketch engine (DotHash / MPLP signatures) — DEAD

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. Cut before implementation.

**How.** The proposal's own second gate was necessity: 'if exact enumeration already fits the latency budget, the sketch is complexity for nothing.' I can answer that from the degree distribution without building anything. Max degree on this vault is 37, p95 is 11, average is 5.37. Exact set intersection between two neighbour sets of under 40 elements, using the existing smaller-side iteration at graph-signals.ts:135, costs microseconds. The asymptotic advantage of a 128-256 dimensional sketch never engages. The variance formula the proposal quotes, (1/F)(deg_s*deg_t + CN^2 - 2CN), is comfortable at F=256 precisely BECAUSE degrees are small — which is the same fact that makes the sketch unnecessary. And the proposal's own stated prerequisite was proposal 5, so there would be 'a higher-order heuristic worth accelerating'; proposal 5 is dead, so there is nothing to accelerate. Final consideration: it injects estimation noise into a product whose stated first principle is that silence beats a wrong answer.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel — arithmetic infrastructure. Its value was contingent on a higher-order heuristic existing, and none survived.

**Kill experiment.** No measurement needed and none should be spent. If a future vault genuinely shows exact enumeration missing the latency budget at 100k notes, revisit — but measure the latency first, and expect the answer to be that it does not.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD. Solves a performance problem that the measured degree distribution says does not exist (max degree 37), in service of a heuristic family that measurement just killed, at the cost of estimation noise in a precision-first ranker.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 8. Landmark distances: the L1 profile is dead, the connected-component test survives (CORRECTED)

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The panel can distinguish 'this sits in a different region of your vault' from 'this is not connected to your vault at all', and stops treating both as zero.

**How.** I built the full version — 32 highest-degree landmarks, one BFS each, negative mean L1 distance between profiles as the pair score — and it does not work as a ranking channel. content+RA+landmark scores 0.7877 +/- 0.0196 at weight 0.5 and 0.7842 at weight 1.0, against content+RA at 0.7858 +/- 0.0184. The delta is +0.002, nine times under the seed SD. On the RA=0 subgroup it is +0.006 against a subgroup SD of 0.06. Dead as a score. BUT the proposal's stated risk — 'if most of a vault is within 4 hops of everything, the profiles will be nearly identical' — is measurably FALSE here, and what it was worried about is where the value actually is. The lab graph has 60 connected components; the largest holds 378 of 494 notes (77%); only 62.6% of sampled pairs are in the same component at all; and the same-component distance histogram peaks at d4 (26.6%) and d5 (25.9%), with only 20.4% within 3 hops. So the graph is neither small-diameter nor connected. The 37.4% of pairs that are in DIFFERENT components are a real, large, cleanly-defined population that every local heuristic scores as an indistinguishable zero. Keep exactly that: one union-find pass at index build, one integer per note, an O(1) same-component test. Use it not to rank but to LABEL and to withhold — a content-only suggestion that crosses components with no structural support is the highest-risk card the panel can show, and it is currently indistinguishable from a well-supported one.

**Why it is not redundant.** The L1 profile is not independent enough to matter (+0.002). Component identity is a different object: it is a hard partition, not a score, so it does not compete with RA — it tells you when RA's zero means 'far away' versus 'not reachable at all'. It also composes with the calibrated-silence proposal below rather than duplicating it.

**Kill experiment.** Not link recall — component membership is a silence/labelling primitive. Use the blind judged-precision protocol with and without the cross-component suppression rule, and report the card-count delta. The pre-registered bars from the original proposal are right and I would keep them: a rule that suppresses under 3% of cards is not worth the code, one that suppresses over 15% has to justify the lost recall. Also report component-size distribution per vault — if a real vault is 98% one component, this degenerates to nothing and should be cut.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Component structure on this vault may be an artefact of the corpus being partly generated — 60 components in 494 notes is a lot of fragmentation, and a mature real vault is probably far more connected, which would shrink the affected population toward zero. Check the component-size distribution on a real vault before writing the suppression rule. Scope also dropped from a landmark system (32 BFS passes, 32 B/note) to a union-find pass (one int/note), so the downside if it fails is about twenty lines.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2.


### 9. Few-parameter learned combiner — DOWNGRADED, revisit only if the feature count recovers

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The panel would adapt its weighting to how your vault is organised, without a settings page.

**How.** The architectural argument is sound and the distinction from the previously-rejected personal metric is real: 13 parameters on 1000+ positives is a different statistical regime from a 384-dimensional metric on a few thousand links. But the premise has collapsed underneath it. The proposed feature vector was [content, BiMax, RA_beta, RA_couple, RA_cocite, L3, CH2-L3, landmark-L1, log deg(s), log deg(t), same folder, same template group, structural boost]. Of those, L3 and CH2-L3 are dead (rho=0.767 with RA, negative on the target subgroup), landmark-L1 is dead (+0.002), and RA_beta is flat across beta in [0.5,2.0] to within 0.005 so it collapses to RA. That leaves about five live and mutually correlated features. Meanwhile the single most important weight — graphInfluence — is already exposed to the user as a slider (main.ts:143 default 1, settings at main.ts:1715-1724) and consumed at index-store.ts:1139 and :2531, and my beta sweep shows the loss surface in that neighbourhood is flat to +/- 0.005. Fitting five correlated parameters to move a metric whose noise floor is 0.02 is not a promising trade.

**Why it is not redundant.** It introduces no new information and needs no independence claim — it replaces hand-tuned weights with fitted ones. The product-level objection stands and is the real reason for caution: it fits the user's PAST linking habit and can therefore suppress exactly the unfamiliar suggestions the panel exists to make.

**Kill experiment.** Unchanged and correct if it is ever run: nested cross-validation, 5 folds, against the current fixed weights, and the gate that matters is PER-FOLD COEFFICIENT VARIANCE rather than mean recall — if fitted coefficients swing sign across folds the model is not identified and must be cut whatever the mean says. With five correlated features on 1327 edges I expect that gate to fire. Third gate also stands: measure the fraction of top-5 suggestions that are already-obvious before and after; raising recall while making suggestions more obvious is winning the metric and losing the product.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The feature set no longer justifies a learned combiner: after this review only about five correlated features survive, the one weight that matters is already user-exposed, and its loss surface is flat to a quarter of the noise floor. Revisit only if proposal 3 and the phantom channel below both survive and the live feature count gets back above roughly eight.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1, 2, 3, and enough surviving channels to be worth combining.


### 10. Path receipts — KEPT, decoupled from L3 and retargeted (CORRECTED)

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** For the cards that currently say only 'Similar text', the panel names the two-step route through your own notes: Rawls, Original Position -> [[veil of ignorance]] -> [[impartiality]] -> Weil, Attention. One click on either middle note verifies it.

**How.** The original made this a dependent of proposal 5, which is now dead. It should survive the decoupling, because a receipt does not need L3 to be a good RANKER — it only needs the path to EXIST for a card that ranked for other reasons. Retarget it precisely, using the code as it stands: index-store.ts:2652-2654 already sets reason = {kind:'graph', detail: basenameOf(s.shared[0])} whenever a shared non-hub neighbour exists, so the RA>0 cards already have a receipt. The gap is the population where RA=0 and there is no shared neighbour to name — I measure that at 21.7% of held-out edges — which currently renders as bare 'Similar text'. Of that population, roughly 12 points of the 21.7 have a nonzero length-3 path (RA=0 AND L3=0 is 9.7%), so path coverage within the receiptless population is about 55%, not the 40%-of-all-cards bar the original proposed. Implementation is the fixed-operator degeneration of NBFNet: edge weight 1/sqrt(deg(u)*deg(v)), best path = max over paths of the product, computed by generalised Bellman-Ford relaxation for 3 iterations from the source with a stored predecessor, O(3E) over the non-hub subgraph. No model, no training. Display rules unchanged and correct: only length 2 or 3, only above a weight threshold, never when every intermediate is a hub, intermediates rendered as live links.

**Why it is not redundant.** It changes no ranking, so the admission rule does not apply. That is the point: it converts an unexplained card into a claim the user can check in two seconds, on the one-fifth of cards that currently arrive with no receipt at all. It is also the only surviving use for path enumeration after L3 failed as a score, which is a genuine argument for building it — the machinery is cheap and the scoring failure does not implicate the paths' explanatory value.

**Kill experiment.** Coverage first, and it is now a cheap query against data I have already extracted: for cards that currently render 'Similar text', what fraction have a qualifying length-2-or-3 non-hub path? My estimate is ~55%, but confirm on the shipped ranker rather than my harness. Then the blind judged-precision protocol: identical ranked suggestions, two receipt styles, judge rates 'do I believe this connection' and 'would I click this', order randomised. Kill if judged precision does not move on the receiptless subgroup. Do NOT evaluate this on link recall.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The chains may be boringly uniform, all routing through the same few notes — though note this vault has no node above degree 37, so there is no dominant MOC layer here to route through, which cuts both ways: fewer boring chains, but also fewer chains. Second risk is visual weight in a narrow sidebar; a three-node chain probably needs to collapse behind a hover. Third and most likely: coverage on a real vault comes in below 40% and the feature is decorative — measure before building the UI.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2. NOT dependent on proposal 5 — build the Bellman-Ford path enumeration alone, roughly a day, and skip the L3 scorer entirely.


### 11. Multiplex co-editing layer — KEPT as the best speculative bet, but its day-one measurement is impossible (CORRECTED)

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** 'You keep working on these two notes in the same sitting, and they are not connected.' Notes you repeatedly open or edit together that share no link and read nothing alike.

**How.** Mechanism unchanged and still the most interesting idea in the list: an opt-in local session log (path, timestamp, open/modify) appended to a vault-local file, sessions as maximal runs with sub-20-minute gaps, pair weight 1/(session size) so a 40-note reorganisation contributes almost nothing, then RA over G_time fused as a third z-normalised channel. What must change is the measurement plan. The proposal offers a ctime-bucket fallback 'which needs nothing and should be measured first as a cheap proxy for whether temporal co-occurrence carries signal at all.' I checked, and that fallback returns nothing on this corpus: every note's mtime falls on one of two days (2026-07-01 and 2026-07-02), there is not a single `created:` or `date:` frontmatter field anywhere in the vault, and the Daily/ folder contains six notes. The lab corpus has no temporal structure whatsoever. So the stated cheap first experiment cannot be run, and neither can any other test of the owner's priority-A TIME axis. The real prerequisite is not session logging — it is a corpus with real timestamps, which is the proposal immediately below.

**Why it is not redundant.** Still the only proposal in the list that adds new INFORMATION rather than a better reading of information already present. Co-editing is a behavioural trace with no representation in the text or the wikilinks, so correlation with content should be near zero by construction. The contrast with the rejected shared-tags channel (rho=0.916 with graph) is sound: tags are deliberate labels tracking the same organising intent as links, co-editing is involuntary. The correlation with the link-graph channel remains the open empirical question and is directly measurable once a corpus exists.

**Kill experiment.** Two gates, in order, and the primary one is correctly NOT link recall — held-out wikilinks are the connections the user DID make, so recall@10 is structurally biased against a feature whose claim is about connections they did not, and a null there is uninformative. (a) Independence: Spearman of the co-edit RA channel against both existing channels over the fixed candidate pool, bar well under 0.30. (b) Blind judged-precision on a vault with 4+ weeks of logged sessions, targeting the bar Surprising Connections v2 already set (P@5 1.00, 36% surprising). Run EdgeBank as the discipline check — a pure memorisation baseline over the co-edit layer — to confirm the fancy version beats 'show whatever was recently open together'. That baseline is cheap and, on the dynamic-link-prediction literature's track record, is the most likely thing to win.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Cold start of several weeks is a poor first-run experience and impossible to demo. Privacy optics of an activity log even though nothing leaves the machine — must be opt-in with a visible, user-deletable file. The mundane failure mode is that co-editing is dominated by 'whatever happened to be open in the other pane', which is noise with a plausible story attached, and EdgeBank is the test that exposes it. Newly identified risk: there is currently no corpus on which any of this can be evaluated, so committing to it means committing to build the evaluation corpus first.

**Needs first.** A corpus with real timestamps (see the corpus proposal), THEN opt-in session logging. The ctime-bucket fallback is not a valid first step — I measured it at zero coverage.


### 12. Shared external references as a bipartite layer — DEAD on this corpus, unfalsifiable as specified

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** 'Both of these cite the same paper.' Would be surfaced even when the two notes share no wikilink and read nothing alike.

**How.** The proposal's own instruction was 'measure coverage BEFORE anything else'. I did, and it terminates the proposal: `grep -rlE 'https?://' --include='*.md'` over all 528 files in the lab vault returns ZERO files. Not sparse — absent. My own extractor, which handles markdown links, bare URLs and embeds, and normalises arXiv abs/pdf and DOI forms to identifiers, finds 0 distinct external references, 0 note pairs sharing one, and 0 wikilinked pairs sharing one. So the stated gates (pair coverage, held-out recall@10, Spearman against both channels) cannot be computed at all. The idea itself is not obviously wrong — the argument that two notes citing the same paper about different aspects have low content similarity and no wikilink is a good argument, and the distinction from the rejected shared-tags channel (tags are deliberate labels applied with the same intent as links; citations are a by-product of reading) is legitimate. It is simply untestable on the project's only measurement instrument, and shipping an unmeasured channel violates the project's sixth hard constraint.

**Why it is not redundant.** Argued, not demonstrated. A shared URL is invisible to RA and invisible to the embedding, so the independence claim is plausible — but plausible is what the shared-tags channel looked like before it measured 0.916, which is exactly why this cannot ship on argument alone.

**Kill experiment.** Blocked. Requires a corpus containing external links before any gate can run. When one exists: coverage first (fraction of pairs sharing a reference, and fraction of held-out wikilinks whose endpoints share one), and if pair coverage is under a couple of percent the honest outcome is that it ships as a receipt decoration on cards that already rank, not as a scoring channel. That is very likely the outcome — see the phantom-node result below, where a signal with a 48x precision lift still could not move recall because coverage was 0.3%.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD as specified: zero coverage on the only corpus available, so no gate can be evaluated and nothing can be admitted. Secondary risk if revived: without URL normalisation the same paper cited via arXiv abs, arXiv pdf and a DOI resolver counts as three references and the channel silently does nothing — my extractor already handles this and can be lifted from the diag script.

**Needs first.** A corpus containing external links. Also requires reading external links and embeds from the metadata cache, which the plugin does not currently touch.


### 13. Forman-Ricci curvature — DEAD by its own pre-registered kill criterion

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. Measured and cut.

**How.** The proposal wrote its own execution order: 'If its correlation with (deg(s)+deg(t)) exceeds ~0.9 on the lab vault, cut it immediately — that check costs an hour and should be the first thing run.' I ran it first. Over a 6262-pair candidate pool, Spearman(F(s,t), deg(s)+deg(t)) = -0.916. The criterion fires. The augmented closed form F(s,t) = 4 - deg(s) - deg(t) + 3|CN(s,t)| is dominated by its degree terms in a sparse graph exactly as the proposal feared, and it is on this vault a degree statistic in a trench coat. The second measurement confirms it from the other side: Spearman(F, RA) = -0.110, so the |CN| term contributes almost none of the neighbourhood-overlap information that both proposed uses — routing to Surprising Connections, and suppressing content-only pairs in negatively curved regions — actually depend on. Using it to route would route by degree; using it to withhold would withhold from low-degree notes, which is the population the panel is already weakest on.

**Why it is not redundant.** The claim was that curvature reads the SHAPE of the joint neighbourhood rather than the size of the overlap, so two pairs with identical RA could have opposite curvature. Measurement says it reads neither shape nor overlap — it reads degree, at rho 0.916.

**Kill experiment.** Already run, /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-justus-obsidian-atomized-intermediary/aed1296b-ccbe-44e2-8d33-3d9a1171972a/scratchpad/diag2.mjs. Neither of the two downstream protocols (Surprising Connections P@5 and percent-surprising; blind judged-precision for the silence rule) needs to be spent. If curvature is ever revisited, it would have to be Ollivier-Ricci, which needs an optimal-transport solve per edge and is out of the compute budget by the proposal's own opening sentence.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD. Exceeds its own stated kill threshold (0.916 against a 0.90 bar) and carries essentially none of the overlap signal its product story requires. Worth noting positively: this proposal was written well enough to kill itself in one hour of work, which is the standard the rest should be held to.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 14. NEW: Phantom-node coupling — read metadataCache.unresolvedLinks, which the plugin never touches

*cheap · high confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** Two things. On a card: 'you both reference [[value function]], which doesn't exist yet.' And in the sidebar: '8 of your notes point at [[value function]] and you never wrote it' — the vault telling you which note you have been circling for months without noticing.

**How.** graph-signals.ts:90 reads `this.app.metadataCache.resolvedLinks` and nothing else. Obsidian also exposes `metadataCache.unresolvedLinks` — wikilinks whose target note does not exist — and the plugin has never looked at it. A phantom is a real node in the user's conceptual graph that happens to have no file, and two notes both pointing at it is bibliographic coupling through a node RA cannot see. Build a bipartite layer of notes x phantom names, project it back with the same degree penalty the graph channel already uses: RA_phantom(s,t) = sum over p in phantoms(s) ∩ phantoms(t) of 1/|notes referencing p|, or PathSim-normalised. I measured all of it. On the lab vault: 141 of 2010 wikilink targets are phantoms (7%), 92 distinct names, 17 referenced by two or more notes, and 15% of notes contain at least one. INDEPENDENCE IS THE BEST IN THE PROJECT: Spearman(RA_phantom, RA) = 0.098 and against content = 0.070, compared with the shipped graph channel's 0.075 against content, which is what it was admitted on. PRECISION IS EXCELLENT: pairs sharing a phantom are 3.4% of wikilinked pairs against a 0.07% base rate over all pairs — a 48x lift. COVERAGE IS THE PROBLEM: only 0.3% of candidate-pool pairs have a nonzero score, so content+RA+phantom scores 0.7887 against content+RA's 0.7858, a +0.003 that sits well inside the 0.018 noise floor. So the verdict is precise: it is a real, near-orthogonal, high-precision signal that is far too sparse to move a ranking, and the honest product shape is (a) a receipt on cards that already rank and (b) a graph-hygiene / link-building feature, which is already on the owner's roadmap. It also lands directly on priority A: phantoms are overwhelmingly the names you type before you make the note, which in a personal vault means people and places — the top phantoms in this corpus include character and person names alongside concepts.

**Why it is not redundant.** Measured, not argued: rho = 0.098 against the graph channel and 0.070 against content, the lowest pair of correlations anything has recorded in this project. Mechanically obvious why — a phantom is by definition absent from resolvedLinks, so RA is structurally blind to it, and the phantom name usually does not appear as prose in either note, so the embedding is blind to it too. It is also leakage-free by construction: a phantom edge can never be a held-out edge, because held-out edges are resolved links.

**Kill experiment.** Coverage and independence are already measured and reported above (diag4.mjs, 8 seeds). Two things still to measure before shipping. (1) On a REAL vault rather than this partly-generated one, re-measure phantom density — I expect it to be substantially higher, because a real vault accumulates [[Anna]] in thirty notes, and if density rises far enough the recall verdict could change. (2) For the hygiene feature, blind judged-precision on the ranked list of missing notes: show the top 20 phantoms by weighted reference count and have the judge rate 'would I create this note'. Bar: P@5 comparable to Surprising Connections v2. Do NOT ship it as a scoring channel on the current evidence — +0.003 against a 0.018 SD is not a result.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Sparsity is the measured killer for the ranking use, and a real vault may not be denser than the lab one. Aliases are the correctness trap: a phantom that later resolves through a frontmatter alias must stop counting as a phantom, or the channel double-counts an edge RA already has. Case and Unicode folding must match the existing `fold()` convention or [[Kant]] and [[kant]] become two phantoms. And the hygiene UI has an obvious failure mode: typos produce phantoms too, so the missing-note list will contain misspellings and needs a minimum reference count (>= 2 or 3) to be tolerable.

**Needs first.** Proposals 1 and 2. Requires reading metadataCache.unresolvedLinks, roughly a day including the alias handling.


### 15. NEW: Subdivide hub nodes by heading anchor instead of thresholding them away

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** A sharper receipt — 'you both link to the Economy section of [[Worldbuilding MOC]]' rather than 'via [[Worldbuilding MOC]]' — and MOCs stop being dead weight in the structural channel instead of being discarded.

**How.** HUB_DEGREE=40 exists because a MOC linked by 200 notes carries no evidence about any pair. But thresholding throws the node away entirely, and as established above the threshold has never even fired on the lab vault. Subdivision is the principled alternative. Obsidian resolves [[MOC#Cartography]] and [[MOC#Economy]] to the same entry in resolvedLinks, and the plugin's own edge extraction strips the anchor (the regex /\[\[([^\]|#]+)(?:[|#][^\]]*)?\]\]/ discards everything after # by design).
