Direct check on why: p_t[x] exceeds 10*d_x/vol for only ~11% of reached nodes, so for the other 89% the subtraction is a rounding error on the score, precisely the no-op the proposal listed as its own risk.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead on the numbers, and its stated mechanism is also factually wrong about the codebase. The proposal says 'today src/graph-signals.ts scores a candidate as (PPR mass)/sqrt(deg) + RA'. That expression is `candidates()` at graph-signals.ts:205, and its only consumer is index-store.ts:2538, where it selects which notes to ADD to the Stage-2 pool. The fused rank is index-store.ts:2632, `s.fused = zSem(s.content) + graphInfluence * zRa(s.ra)` — PPR mass appears nowhere in it. There is no scoring role for AC to replace.

**Needs first.** None. Do not build it.


### 3. P2. Replace truncated PPR with heat-kernel local push — CORRECTED: instrument the constants before porting anything

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing yet. Later, possibly: multi-hop suggestions stop vanishing on vaults with big index notes.

**How.** The proposal's port is premature because nobody knows whether the three truncations ever bind. Do the 20-line version first: add three counters to GraphSignals (frontier-cap firings, hub-skip firings, max frontier width), expose them in a dev-only command, and read them on a real large vault. Only if they fire is hk-relax justified — and then port it as described (push queue over residuals, per-term thresholds from a target degree-weighted 1-norm error, t exposed as the locality dial).

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel, and does not need the admission rule. But the fidelity claim needs a graph where the truncation actually loses something.

**Kill experiment.** Already run, and it kills the lab-vault experiment. Replaying the shipped walk exactly (PPR_STEPS=3, ALPHA=0.15, HUB_DEGREE=40, FRONTIER_MAX=400) over all 440 linked notes: max frontier ever reached 317 against a cap of 400, frontier-cap fired 0 times, hub-skip fired 0 times. Max degree in the vault is 37. Rank agreement@50 against exact is therefore 100% by construction, and the proposal's own kill criterion ('kill if the current truncation already achieves >99% agreement') fires immediately. Worse, bench/graph-fusion-eval2.mjs:32 strips MOC notes with `if (/\bMOC\b/i.test(basename)) continue`, so the lab protocol has excised the very notes HUB_DEGREE exists for. Corrected protocol: (1) ship the counters, read them on the owner's real vault and on a synthetic 100k graph with a power-law degree tail; (2) only then compare hk-push to the truncation at matched wall-clock.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The honest expectation is that the counters come back zero on most vaults and this closes. HUB_DEGREE=40 is the one that probably does fire on a vault with a real MOC, and its failure mode is severe — pprFrom (graph-signals.ts:166) does `if (nbrs.size > HUB_DEGREE) continue`, which does not down-weight the hub, it deletes every path through it. A cheaper fix than hk-relax exists for that specific bug: keep walking through the hub but divide its outgoing share by an extra factor, so a MOC attenuates instead of severing.

**Needs first.** None.


### 4. P3. One polynomial-filter primitive, then a per-query diffusion scale — CORRECTED: keep the primitive as bench-only, kill the adaptive scale

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. The adaptive part would not have changed the panel.

**How.** applyFilter(theta[], x) = sum_k theta_k T^k x with T = D^-1/2 (A+I) D^-1/2 by Horner, ~60 lines. Worth writing ONCE, in bench/ not src/, because it is what makes each kernel in the bake-off 20 lines instead of 200. Keep GDC's sparsify-then-RENORMALIZE rule when you do. Delete the per-query scale selector.

**Why it is not redundant.** The primitive is not a channel. The adaptive-scale claim was that a low-degree note needs a wider radius than a hub — that is a testable prediction and it fails.

**Kill experiment.** Two things already measured. (a) The proposal predicted the gain concentrates in the low-degree bucket. Candidate-pool recall by source degree is 96.0% (deg 1-2, n=247), 98.0% (deg 3-8, n=1064), 94.3% (deg 9+, n=279) — there is no low-degree deficit to fix; if anything the HIGH-degree bucket is worst, which is the opposite of the mechanism. (b) Fixed-t results across three seeds: no t beats content+RA outside seed noise (see P1). Remaining kill bar if anyone insists on the adaptive variant: it must beat the best fixed t by >= 0.01 on three seeds AND on a second vault, and the gain must appear in the degree bucket the mechanism predicts.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The real risk is what the proposal named: one selector fitted on one 465-note vault. My measurement removes the motivation entirely, so the risk is now that someone builds it anyway because the primitive is fun. Build the primitive, spend the two hours, use it to close the area (P12), then stop.

**Needs first.** None. Prerequisite for P12.


### 5. P4. Blur-then-sharpen (BSPM) — DEAD

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing.

**How.** final = p - gamma*(p_blurred_more - p), a filter coefficient vector with a negative lobe.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not, by its author's own admission, and that was fine — it was offered as a replacement for the diffusion channel. The problem is the channel it replaces.

**Kill experiment.** Not worth running, and here is why in numbers rather than opinion. Sharpening undoes over-smoothing caused by low-pass diffusion. The shipped graph term is not a diffusion: it is `zRa(s.ra)` at index-store.ts:2632, a 1-hop degree-weighted triangle count with no low-pass to undo. And the diffusion that WOULD be sharpened does not beat RA in the first place (P1: 0.796-0.808 across every t). Sharpening a channel that is already at the family ceiling has nothing to recover. Additionally the sparsity argument is decisive: BSPM's regime is dense user-item rows; this vault has mean degree 5.36, median 5, with 22.2% of notes at degree <= 2 and 11.1% at degree 0. A second-order correction on a 5-element neighbourhood is noise with a minus sign in front of it.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead. If someone runs it anyway, the sanity check to run first is the one the proposal itself named — count how many top-10 positions change. On a 90-note pool with mean degree 5 the answer will be under 1 on average, and that is the whole result.

**Needs first.** P3's primitive, which is the only reason this is cheap to disprove.


### 6. P5. Semantic manifold diffusion — DEAD, measured, and it fails all three of its own criteria

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** It would have shown 'connects to [[Z]] via [[X]] -> [[Y]]'. The pairs it produces are not the ones you want.

**How.** I built it: k-reciprocal kNN graph over the centered jina whole-note vectors (k=10 and k=20, edge kept only if mutual, weight cos^3), row-normalised, 3-step diffusion from the query, fused by z-normalised rank. Graph sizes: k=10 gives 1773 edges and mean reciprocal degree 7.18; k=20 gives 3806 edges and mean degree 15.41.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not. This is the third time this project has learned the same lesson. CSLS failed, Mutual Proximity failed, and now manifold diffusion fails, for one reason: all three are TRANSFORMATIONS OF THE EMBEDDING, and a transformation of one information source cannot be independent of that source. The link graph works because it is a different information source — human editorial judgement that is not in the text. Write this down as a standing rule: a candidate channel must read a different input, not re-shape an existing one.

**Kill experiment.** Already run, Fisher-Yates seed 20260727, 530 held-out link instances. (1) Fused recall@10: content 0.674, content+MD 0.660 (k=10) and 0.668 (k=20) — the channel makes ranking WORSE than content alone, before you even ask about the +0.02 bar. (2) The discriminating test the proposal itself specified — recall on held-out links whose target sits outside the source's content top-50 (n=46) — content 0.000, content+RA 0.370, content+MD 0.022 (k=10) and 0.000 (k=20). The channel claims to live exactly there and it is empty there. Adding it on top of RA drags that subset down from 0.370 to 0.326 (k=10) and 0.239 (k=20). (3) Spearman(content, manifold-diffusion) over the reached pool: 0.633 (k=10), 0.637 (k=20), against the proposal's own 0.5 kill line.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead. The mechanism is transitive smoothing of a space in which the query's own neighbourhood is already the answer, so it returns a blurred version of the cosine ranking — which is what a 0.63 correlation and a below-baseline recall look like. The hub amplification the proposal worried about is real but secondary; the k-reciprocal filter worked (mean reciprocal degree 7.18 at k=10, only 1 isolated note) and the channel still failed.

**Needs first.** None. Do not build it.


### 7. P6. Bridge notes via Balanced Forman curvature — KEEP, and it is the strongest idea in the document

*cheap · high confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** '[[Cosine Similarity]] is the only thing connecting your German maths notes to your English ML notes.' A passive card, and the inverse: two clusters one link away from touching.

**How.** Per-edge Balanced Forman curvature from local quantities the adjacency already gives: the two degrees, triangles on the edge, 4-cycles through it. Strongly negative = bottleneck. Rank by curvature x size-of-smaller-side, label the two sides with the existing spherical-k-means clusters and c-TF-IDF labels from src/vault-map.ts. Compute on idle after indexing, cache per edge, invalidate edges within 2 hops of a change. ONE CORRECTION TO THE MECHANISM, from the measurement: the top-curvature edges are dominated by index notes — 4 of my top 10 are 'Machine Learning MOC <-> Zettelkasten Index', 'Theoretische Informatik MOC <-> Zettelkasten Index', 'Datenbanken MOC <-> Zettelkasten Index', 'Vector Database <-> Datenbanken MOC'. 'Your MOC connects your areas' is trivially true and reads as a bug. Exclude or heavily penalise edges with an index/MOC endpoint (detectable by out-degree relative to in-degree, or by the existing hub heuristic) before ranking.

**Why it is not redundant.** This one measured as genuinely independent, which is rare in this document. Pearson(BFC, 1/du + 1/dv) = 0.171 and Pearson(BFC, #triangles) = 0.296 across all 1327 edges. RA is a triangle count; curvature is the ABSENCE of triangles and 4-cycles; the near-zero correlation with the triangle count confirms they are measuring different things rather than the same thing with a sign flip. Nothing in the shipped system — content, RA, PPR, de-crowding — can express 'this connection is structurally load-bearing'.

**Kill experiment.** Partly run. Distribution: 607/1327 edges (45.7%) negatively curved, min -1.78, median 0.08, max 2.21. After the MOC exclusion, the remaining top edges are readable and non-obvious: 'Matritzen <-> Neural Networks' (a German linear-algebra note bridging to English ML), 'Cosine Similarity <-> Skalarprodukt' (the same concept in two languages, in two areas of the vault), 'Recursion <-> Verbindungen die mir auffallen', 'Ensemble Learning <-> Multi-Head Attention', 'Gradient Descent <-> Policy Gradient'. Remaining protocol, unchanged from the proposal and correct: top-20 bridges vs 20 degree-matched controls, shuffled, blind-judged 'these are two areas I think of as separate and this note joins them'. Ship at >= 0.8 on bridges and <= 0.3 on controls. Mechanical cross-check needing no human: delete the edge, measure the drop in mutual PPR mass between the two sides and the change in connected-component count. Note the lab harnesses strip MOC notes, so this must run on the UNFILTERED vault.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The proposal's stated risk — that on a small vault every edge is a bridge — is measurably overstated at 45.7% but not absent: half the edges being flagged means the RANKING is doing all the work, not the sign. If the size-of-smaller-side weighting plus MOC exclusion does not produce a clean top-20, retest at 5k notes before discarding, since bridges genuinely compound with vault size. Second risk: 12.4% of edges have zero shared neighbours at all, and those will pile up at the negative end for the boring reason that they are pendant links to leaf notes; require both sides to have size >= 3.

**Needs first.** src/vault-map.ts cluster labels for the presentation half. Nothing else.


### 8. P7 (was 8). Path receipts: show the chain, not just the shared neighbour — KEEP, and the code confirms the gap exactly

*cheap · high confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Instead of 'via [[Index]]', the card shows the route: [[this note]] -> [[Fermentation]] -> [[Gut flora]] -> [[that note]].

**How.** Bounded Dijkstra from the source over edge weights -log P(u->v) under the row-normalised transition matrix, capped at 4 hops and a few thousand settled nodes, once per DISPLAYED card (~5 per render). Add log(deg) to the edge cost so a path through a MOC is expensive, and refuse to render a path whose every intermediate is a hub. Below a mass threshold, show no receipt and drop the card.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel — it is the explanation layer, and the code shows precisely why it is load-bearing. index-store.ts:2645 computes `rescued = s.nominated && s.ra > 0 && s.shared.length > 0`, and index-store.ts:2653 sets `reason = { kind: 'graph', detail: basenameOf(s.shared[0]) }`. Both require ra > 0, i.e. a SHARED NEIGHBOUR. A note the walk nominated from 2 or 3 hops with no shared neighbour has ra === 0, so it cannot be rescued below the similarity floor and renders as plain 'Similar text' with no receipt at all. Under the precision-first rule that means the multi-hop half of the nomination channel is currently unshippable, exactly as the proposal claims.

**Kill experiment.** Correct the coverage denominator — measure over the cards the panel actually RENDERS, not over the whole graph-reachable pool, because that pool is much larger than what gets shown. And size the prize honestly first: the graph nominator contributes 5.8% of held-out link instances beyond the content shortlist (measured, three FY seeds, shipped funnel of 60 shortlist + 30 nominations), and the ra === 0 subset of that is what this unlocks. So run the cheap sizing first: of nominated cards that reach the panel, what fraction have ra === 0? Then the three tests as written: (1) coverage, need >= 0.5 of those pairs to have a path above threshold; (2) blind A/B, chain receipt vs single-neighbour receipt, ship at >= 60% on pairs where they differ; (3) p95 under 30 ms per card. Kill on any.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The proposal's own risk is confirmed by my curvature run: the most structurally salient paths in this vault go straight through 'Zettelkasten Index' and the MOC notes. 'Connected via your index note' is worse than no receipt, so the log(deg) penalty and the all-hubs refusal are mandatory, not optional. Second risk, and it is the one that matters commercially: the prize is bounded by 5.8% of link instances, so this is a precision-preserving unlock of a small slice, not a recall win. Judge it as a quality feature.

**Needs first.** None.


### 9. P8 (was 9). Time-respecting diffusion — CORRECTED: the killer test cannot be run on the lab vault, and the directional premise is already falsified

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** 'This came out of' (older notes upstream) and 'this led to' (newer notes downstream) as separate sections.

**How.** Two directed graphs from the same edges, split by whether the target predates the source; diffuse on each. Prefer dates parsed from daily-note titles, then ctime.

**Why it is not redundant.** Two corrections. (1) FACTUAL: the proposal says 'src/graph-signals.ts symmetrizes resolvedLinks unconditionally — every bit of directional and temporal information is currently discarded before any scoring happens'. Directional information is NOT discarded system-wide: index-store.ts:3101-3106 computes bibliographic coupling over OUTGOING resolved links only, `shared outlinks / min(|outlinks|)`, and it is in the shipped structural boost as `W_BIBLIO * biblio`. (2) MEASURED: I tested whether splitting the graph by direction helps at all, by running co-citation (shared in-neighbours, 1/outdeg-weighted) and bibliographic coupling (shared out-neighbours, 1/indeg-weighted) as separate fused channels. Three FY seeds: content+co-citation 0.711, content+biblio 0.737, content+RA 0.796, content+RA+cocit+biblio 0.770. Splitting the graph by direction DESTROYS signal rather than adding it — the symmetrized RA is strictly better than either half and better than all three together. The reason is visible in the data: 53.6% of directed links are reciprocal, so the split halves the evidence on every edge while separating almost nothing.

**Kill experiment.** The proposal's own killer test, run first as instructed, and it returns a number that means nothing. Fraction of directed wikilinks pointing at an older-ctime target: 1218/1869 = 65.2%, in the ambiguous band. But the SAME fraction under mtime is 1218/1869 = 65.2%, identical to the last link, because ctime === mtime for every single file in the lab vault. That is the signature of a bulk-copied or generated corpus: the timestamps encode the corpus BUILD order, not the user's authoring history, so 65.2% is measuring `cp -r`. Corrected protocol: the timestamp-reliability check must run on the owner's real vault. Per the project's own notes that vault is iCloud-stored and TCC-blocked from shell access, so it has to be a dev-only command inside the plugin that prints (a) the older-target fraction, (b) the fraction of files where ctime === mtime, and (c) the count of distinct ctimes. Only if (b) is low and (a) is >= 0.70 does anything else follow, and even then it must beat the directional-split result above.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Two independent things have to go right and one of them has already gone wrong. ctime is unreliable through iCloud sync, migrations and bulk imports — the lab vault is itself the proof. And even with perfect timestamps, the measured evidence says directional splits of THIS graph lose to the symmetrized version. The idea is not obviously wrong in principle; it is that the vault does not carry the data and the graph does not reward the split.

**Needs first.** A timestamp-reliability command that runs inside the plugin on the real vault. Do not spend an hour on the lab vault; the answer there is noise.


### 10. P9 (was 10). GraphWave role signatures — CORRECTED: drop the eigenbasis prerequisite, keep the gate

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** '[[X]] plays the same role here as [[Y]]' — both the hub of a project, both a dead-end reference. Also 'this note has quietly become a hub; it may want splitting.'

**How.** Correct the mechanism to remove its dependency on P10's spectral basis, which I am marking dead. Heat wavelets psi_s = exp(-s*L) e_i do not need an eigendecomposition: compute them by K sparse mat-vecs per node (Chebyshev or plain truncated series), which is exactly P3's applyFilter. Sample the empirical characteristic function of each wavelet at ~10 grid points for 2-3 scales, giving a ~60-dim structural signature; int8 it, 60 bytes/note, negligible. Cheaper fallback worth measuring in the same session because it is 20 lines: the sorted degree profile of the 1-hop and 2-hop neighbourhood. On a graph this close to a tree that probably captures most of what the wavelets capture, and if it does, ship it instead.

**Why it is not redundant.** Anti-proximity by design, and the correlation gate is the right first unit test: |Spearman(role distance, RA)| must be < 0.2 or the implementation is wrong.

**Kill experiment.** Keep all three tests as written — synthetic barbell/cycle automorphism check, top-20 role twins vs 20 degree-matched controls blind-judged at >= 0.7 with controls <= 0.3, and the correlation gate. Add one cheap pre-check that decides whether to start: measured, 22.2% of the lab vault has degree <= 2 and 11.1% has degree 0. Before building anything, compute the degree profiles and check how many distinct signatures exist among the deg<=2 notes. If a quarter of the vault collapses to three signatures, test (2) cannot pass and you have spent twenty minutes instead of a week.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The degeneracy the proposal named, quantified: on a vault where 22% of notes have degree <= 2, their wavelet distributions are near-identical and the signature is a noisy re-encoding of degree. The synthetic test will pass regardless because it uses a synthetic graph — treat it as a correctness check on the code, never as evidence. Second risk, now smaller: the proposal worried about eigenvalue multiplicity on a disconnected graph; measured, the vault has 61 components of which 55 are singletons, so after dropping degree-0 notes there are 6 real components. Doing this by mat-vec instead of eigendecomposition sidesteps the issue entirely.

**Needs first.** P3's applyFilter primitive. NOT P10.


### 11. P10 (was 11). Spectral compression to r-dim dot products — DEAD as a scoring accelerator

*expensive · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing.

**How.** Top-r eigenpairs of the symmetric normalized adjacency at index time, int8 U stored next to the note vectors, every polynomial filter becomes an r-dim dot product.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel — infrastructure. Which is the problem: it is infrastructure for a family that measured as closed.

**Kill experiment.** Do not run it. The justification is arithmetic rather than an experiment. What this accelerates is diffusion scoring; the winning channel is RA, which is a set intersection over the source's neighbours costing O(deg^2) — at mean degree 5.36 that is roughly 29 operations per pair, and the shipped funnel evaluates it over ~90 candidates (index-store.ts:2516, width = max(topK*4, DEFAULT_SHORTLIST=60), plus max(8, width/2)=30 nominations). Building a rank-256 eigenbasis over 100k notes to accelerate 2,600 integer operations is inverted engineering. Separately, the diffusion family it would accelerate does not beat RA (P1, P12).

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead for this purpose. One factual correction in its favour, in case it is revived for a DIFFERENT purpose: the proposal claims vault graphs are 'extremely disconnected — hundreds of components' and that deflation is mandatory. Measured: 61 components, 55 of them singletons, largest holding 378 of 494 notes (76.4%). Drop the degree-0 notes and you have six components. So the deflation problem is much smaller than feared — which matters if the eigenbasis is ever wanted for vault-map layout or P9's signatures, where it would be judged on its own merits.

**Needs first.** None. Do not build it for ranking.


### 12. P11 (was 12). Kernel bake-off harness — KEEP, and I have already run most of it; the answer is the negative result

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing. It closes an area and writes down why.

**How.** bench/kernel-bakeoff.mjs driving the real IndexStore, one row per kernel: recall@10, Spearman with content, Spearman with RA, top-1 concentration skew, ms/query, and recall on the outside-content-top-50 subset. Keep the log-transform variants. THREE CORRECTIONS. (a) The baseline is wrong: 0.745 comes from the defective shuffle (P0). On a correct Fisher-Yates split the same code gives content+RA 0.800, so the ship bar is >= 0.81, not >= 0.755. (b) The prediction that commute time is 'a degree prior wearing a hat' with R^2 > 0.9 is FALSE at vault scale — measured R^2 = 0.374 (Pearson 0.611) between commute time and 1/d_u + 1/d_v over 70,067 unlinked pairs in the 378-note largest component, computed from an exact dense Laplacian pseudo-inverse. von Luxburg's asymptotics have not kicked in at 500 notes, so that kernel was not dead a priori. (c) The prediction that the regularized commute-time kernel is the honest candidate is also false — measured below.

**Why it is not redundant.** It IS the independence test, automated, and it turns the admission rule into a table instead of a judgement call.

**Kill experiment.** Already run for nine kernels. Mean over three Fisher-Yates seeds, 1590 held-out link instances, with recall on the outside-content-top-50 subset (n=145) and Spearman with RA over the graph-reachable pool: content 0.673 / 0.000 / -; content+RA 0.796 / 0.379 / 1.000; +Adamic-Adar 0.796 / 0.372 / 1.000; +common-neighbours 0.787 / 0.345 / 0.995; +Katz 0.787 / 0.345 / 0.655; +regularized-commute-time 0.792 / 0.269 / 0.626; +SimRank 0.784 / 0.248 / 0.629; +co-citation 0.711 / 0.200 / 0.703; +bibliographic-coupling 0.737 / 0.159 / 0.685; +autocovariance and raw t-step walk 0.796-0.808 (P1). NOTHING BEATS RA, and every kernel that differs from RA in ranking (Spearman 0.62-0.70) is worse on the discriminating subset — which is the tell: they differ from RA by being wrong, not by knowing something else. Remaining rows worth adding before writing the finding down: the log-transform variants, the free-energy/RSP distance at two temperatures, and P12's non-backtracking operator. Then write the negative result next to CSLS, Mutual Proximity and shared-tags.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The proposer budgeted for a clean null and got one. The real risk now is the opposite: someone reads +0.012 on a cherry-picked t as a win. Guard against it in the harness itself — report three seeds, report the seed spread next to the mean, and refuse to report a max over a swept hyperparameter without also reporting the mean over the sweep.

**Needs first.** P0 (the split fix) is mandatory or the table measures a biased sample. P3's applyFilter makes each row 20 lines.


### 13. P12 (ADDED). Non-backtracking walk — the one operator left that is not a polynomial in A

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing directly. It is the last shot at the diffusion family before it is closed.

**How.** Every kernel in P11 is a function of A or D^-1 A, and on a locally tree-like graph short walks are dominated by degree echoes: a 2-step walk returns to the source, a 3-step walk re-finds the 1-hop neighbours. That is why they all cluster at 0.78-0.80 and why odd and even t behave so differently (measured Spearman(RA, AC_t) alternates 0.83 / 0.39 / 0.80 / 0.48 / 0.75). The Hashimoto non-backtracking operator B acts on DIRECTED EDGES and forbids immediate reversal, removing exactly those echoes; it is the one construct in this area that cannot be written as a polynomial in A. Implementation: index the 2|E| directed edges (2,654 on the lab vault, ~2M at 100k notes), build the successor lists once, and run t steps of mass on edge-space with the reversal excluded, then project back to nodes. Two Int32Arrays and a Float32Array; ~80 lines against the CSR store.

**Why it is not redundant.** The measured failure mode of this whole area is that every candidate is a smoothed re-reading of the same 1-and-2-hop neighbourhood RA already reads. Non-backtracking is the only operator with a structural reason to see something else, and the vault sits squarely in the sparse regime (mean degree 5.36, global clustering 0.342, 76% of notes in one component) where the non-backtracking spectrum is known to separate structure that the adjacency spectrum cannot.

**Kill experiment.** One more row in P11's table, on the corrected Fisher-Yates split, three seeds. Report recall@10, recall on the outside-content-top-50 subset, and Spearman with RA. Ship bar: >= 0.81 recall@10 AND >= 0.40 on the discriminating subset AND Spearman with RA < 0.6. Kill on any one. Budget it at half a day; if it fails, the area is closed and P11's negative result is written with a clear conscience.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The honest prior after nine kernels landing in a 0.02 band is that this lands there too, and I would not bet on it. Its theoretical advantage is about SPECTRAL separation for clustering, which is not the same task as pair scoring, and the transfer is an assumption. Propose it only because it costs half a day and because closing an area on 'we tried everything polynomial' is weaker than closing it on 'we tried everything polynomial and the one thing that is not'.

**Needs first.** P11's harness. Benefits from P13's CSR store but does not need it at lab scale.


### 14. P13 (was 13). CSR graph store with incremental edge deltas — KEEP, but the motivation in the code is not the one stated

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The Vault Insights report stops taking minutes on a large vault, and saving a note stops dropping the link graph on the floor.

**How.** Path->int32 interning (the same trick as src/title-index.ts), CSR arrays (Int32Array indptr, Int32Array indices, Float32Array weights), built once and PATCHED per changed file. CORRECTED PRIORITISATION, from reading the call sites. The stated motivation is per-save cost, but GraphSignals.invalidate() (graph-signals.ts:79) only drops the maps and ensure() rebuilds lazily on next query, so the worst case is one O(E) rebuild per panel render, not per keystroke. The real cost is elsewhere and larger: main.ts:482 calls store.invalidateGraph() on EVERY metadataCache 'changed' event with no debounce, and index-store.ts:2849 runs `this.graph.candidates(p, 20)` — a fresh 3-hop walk — for EVERY note in the vault inside surprisingConnections(), then index-store.ts:2868-2887 does an O(|needTopTen| x N) full-dimension content pass on the main thread. At 100k notes that second loop alone is ~10^10 dot products. Do the work in this order: (1) debounce invalidateGraph, ~10 lines; (2) fix the surprising-connections scaling (see P14); (3) then CSR.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. It is the enabling cost reduction, and it removes the pressure that produced HUB_DEGREE=40 and PPR_FRONTIER_MAX=400.

**Kill experiment.** Correctness first and non-negotiable: bit-identical RA scores and identical top-20 rankings against the current implementation on the lab vault — this is a refactor and must be provably neutral. Then microbenchmarks at 1k/10k/100k synthetic vaults with power-law degrees: full-build ms, per-save patch ms, RSS, and — the number that actually matters — wall-clock for a full surprisingConnections() sweep. Target per-save patch under 5 ms at 100k. Note the lab vault cannot show any of this: 494 notes and 1327 edges rebuild in well under a millisecond, so this must be measured on synthetic graphs.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Interned ids need tombstoning or compaction across renames and deletes, or the id space leaks over a long session. And it is a refactor with no user-visible gain, so it loses every prioritisation argument against a feature — which is exactly why the debounce and the insights-sweep fix should go first: they are cheap, they are visible, and they buy the time to do the refactor properly. One incidental find while reading: src/index-store.ts contains a stray NUL byte around offset 125711, which makes plain grep treat the file as binary and silently skip it. Worth cleaning up; it will hide things from anyone reviewing this work.

**Needs first.** None.


### 15. P14 (ADDED). Make the surprising-connections sweep survive a large vault by reusing the k-means clusters

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Vault Insights — the report carrying the feature measured as the magic slot — keeps working past a few thousand notes instead of freezing the app.

**How.** surprisingConnections() gates a pair on 'each note is outside the other's content top-10', and computes that top-10 exactly, at index-store.ts:2868-2887, with a full O(|needTopTen| x N) pass of full-dimension cosines on the main thread. The comment there correctly explains why the old N x N matrix was removed, but the replacement is still quadratic in the worst case because needTopTen grows with the candidate pairs. Replace the exact top-10 gate with the cluster assignment that spherical k-means in src/vault-map.ts already computes on idle: a candidate is 'obvious' if it shares the source's cluster, which is an O(1) lookup. Keep the exact cosine only for the CONTENT_FLOOR check on the pairs that survive, which is a few hundred comparisons instead of tens of millions.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. It is the scaling fix for the one shipped surface where graph algorithms already run vault-wide, and it is in this area because the gate it replaces is the same 'is this obvious' predicate the whole document keeps re-deriving.

**Kill experiment.** Two parts. (1) Fidelity: on the lab vault, what fraction of pairs get the same obvious/non-obvious verdict from the cluster gate as from the exact top-10 gate? Need >= 0.9 agreement, and specifically the FALSE-NEGATIVE rate (a genuinely obvious pair the cluster gate lets through) must be under 0.05, because that is the direction that produces an embarrassing card. (2) Outcome: re-run the existing blind judged-precision and surprise-rate protocol on the cluster-gated output and require it to hold at the established bar (P@5 1.00, 36% surprising). (3) Wall-clock for a full sweep at 1k/10k/100k synthetic vaults, before and after. Kill the substitution if (1) or (2) fails and instead cap the sweep by sampling notes, which is worse but honest.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The clusters are coarser than a top-10, so a large cluster will mark genuinely non-obvious cross-topic pairs as obvious and suppress them — the failure is silent lost recall on exactly the feature's best output. The defence is to gate only on SMALL clusters and fall back to the exact top-10 within an oversized cluster. Second risk: the k-means assignment is refreshed on idle and can be stale relative to the index, so a recently-edited note may be gated against an old cluster; acceptable, since the report is itself an idle-time artefact.

**Needs first.** Existing spherical k-means assignments from src/vault-map.ts. Pairs naturally with P13.


### 16. P15 (was 14). Effective-resistance leverage instead of HUB_DEGREE — DEAD, killed by its own test

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing would have changed.

**How.** Per-edge statistical leverage l_uv = w_uv * R_eff(u,v) as a continuous replacement for the HUB_DEGREE cliff, plus a hygiene view of load-bearing links.

**Why it is not redundant.** It claimed leverage is not merely degree. It is.

**Kill experiment.** The proposal's own killer test, run first exactly as it specified, from an exact dense Laplacian pseudo-inverse on the 378-note largest component: Pearson(l_uv, 1/d_u + 1/d_v) = 0.935 over 1186 edges, against its own stop-at-0.9 line. It fires. And the qualitative failure is worse than the correlation: the top-leverage edges all sit at l = 1.000 and are pendant edges to leaf notes — 'LLM <-> T2000 Studienarbeit', 'Dynamic Programming <-> 2026-03-03', 'Stochastic Gradient Descent <-> 2026-06-11'. Leverage on a sparse near-tree finds leaves, so the hygiene view would read 'the link to your orphan daily note is the only thing holding your vault together', which is technically true and useless. Note also that the constant it replaces never fires: max degree on the lab vault is 37 against HUB_DEGREE = 40, zero hub-skips in 440 walks.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Dead by measurement. The interesting wrinkle the proposal anticipated — that a 500-note graph might be far enough from the asymptotic regime for leverage to survive — is half-true and the halves point opposite ways: commute time between UNLINKED pairs is only weakly degree-determined at this scale (R^2 = 0.374, see P11), but leverage on EXISTING EDGES is almost entirely degree-determined (r = 0.935). That distinction is worth writing down, because it means 'von Luxburg kills it' is the right verdict for edge leverage and the wrong verdict for commute-time-like pair kernels.

**Needs first.** None. Do not build it. If HUB_DEGREE needs fixing, the cheap correct fix is in P2: attenuate the hub's outgoing share instead of severing every path through it.


### 17. P16 (ADDED). Let the graph modulate the content scores instead of adding to them: label propagation on the induced pool subgraph

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Cards whose neighbours in the shortlist are all strongly related get promoted even when they share no neighbour with the note you are reading.

**How.** The shipped fusion is additive and per-candidate: index-store.ts:2632, `s.fused = zSem(s.content) + graphInfluence * zRa(s.ra)`. It can express 'this candidate has graph evidence' and nothing else. Instead, take the ~90-note candidate pool the funnel already built, build the induced link subgraph on it (edges among the candidates themselves, which the adjacency already has), and run 3 steps of f <- (1-mu)*base + mu * D^-1 A f, seeded by base = z(content) + z(RA). This is the manifold-ranking operator applied to the LINK graph on the pool rather than the kNN graph on the vectors — the distinction that made P5 fail and might make this work. Cost: a 90-node subgraph, three mat-vecs, microseconds, no new storage, ~30 lines.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not an additional channel and does not need the admission rule — it is a different FUSION OPERATOR for the two channels already admitted. The thing it can express that addition cannot: a candidate with zero RA to the source but strong links to three other high-scoring candidates gets promoted. That is second-order evidence about the pool's own structure, which neither z(content) nor z(RA) sees. It is also the only proposal here aimed at where the measured headroom actually is: pool recall is 97.0% while recall@10 is 0.80, so 17 points sit in re-ranking a pool you already have, not in retrieval.

**Kill experiment.** Already run, three Fisher-Yates seeds, 1590 held-out link instances, on the real shipped funnel geometry (60-note content shortlist + 30 graph nominations). mu=0 (the shipped additive fusion, pool-restricted) 0.805, mu=0.15 0.811, mu=0.3 0.814, mu=0.5 0.810, mu=0.7 0.787. On the outside-content-top-50 subset (n=145): 0.297 / 0.303 / 0.290 / 0.276 / 0.228. So: +0.009 at mu=0.3, consistent in direction across all three seeds, but BELOW the +0.01 admission bar and slightly NEGATIVE on the discriminating subset. Verdict as it stands: not shippable. What would change that, and is worth one more afternoon: seed the propagation with content only (not content+RA) so the graph does all the structural work through the operator instead of twice; and try the symmetric normalisation D^-1/2 A D^-1/2 with GDC's renormalize rule instead of the row-stochastic form. Kill if neither variant reaches +0.02 with the subset number not falling.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The measured +0.009 is the same magnitude as everything else in this document that turned out to be noise, and I want to be consistent about that: I am reporting it as a marginal, not a win. The mechanism also has a specific failure mode — propagation over the pool subgraph pulls candidates toward whatever CLUSTER dominates the shortlist, which is the same over-smoothing that makes high mu collapse (0.787 at mu=0.7). If it ships at all it ships at mu <= 0.3 with the value pinned, not exposed as a setting.

**Needs first.** None. Runs entirely inside the existing rank() over the existing pool.


## Hierarchy, community structure and hyperbolic space


<details><summary>19 techniques surveyed</summary>

- **Nested degree-corrected SBM with MDL model selection** (established, 2014-2017) — Fits a hierarchy of blocks to a graph by minimising a description length, so it returns ONE block when there is no structure. Gives per-pair expected edge counts from block affinities omega_rs and degree terms.  
  <sub>Peixoto, 'Hierarchical block structures and high-resolution model selection in large networks', Phys. Rev. X 4, 011047 (2014); 'Nonparametric Bayesian inference of the microcanonical stochastic block model', Phys. Rev. E 95, 012317 (2017). arXiv ids not verified this session (web-search budget exhausted).</sub>
- **Leiden with the Constant Potts Model** (established, 2011-2019) — Community detection that guarantees every returned community is internally connected (Louvain does not), with a resolution parameter that has no resolution limit. The practical entry point for any partition in this project.  
  <sub>Traag, Waltman, van Eck, 'From Louvain to Leiden: guaranteeing well-connected communities', Scientific Reports 9:5233 (2019); Traag, Van Dooren, Nesterov, 'Narrow scope for resolution-limit-free community detection', Phys. Rev. E 84, 016114 (2011).</sub>
- **Descriptive vs inferential community detection** (established, 2023) — Shows modularity-style methods find communities in random graphs, and gives the DL-versus-null test that decides whether a partition is real. This is the gate a map needs before it draws anything.  
  <sub>Peixoto, 'Descriptive vs. inferential community detection in networks: pitfalls, myths and half-truths', Cambridge Elements in Structure and Dynamics of Complex Networks (2023).</sub>
- **Link communities (edge partitioning)** (established, 2010) — Partitions EDGES, not nodes, by the Jaccard similarity of inclusive neighbourhoods, single-linkage, cut at maximum partition density. A node inherits every community its edges belong to, so membership is naturally overlapping and hierarchical.  
  <sub>Ahn, Bagrow, Lehmann, 'Link communities reveal multiscale complexity in networks', Nature 466, 761-764 (2010).</sub>
- **Cartographic roles: within-module degree z-score and participation coefficient** (established, 2005) — Given any partition, labels each node by how its links spread across modules. Separates 'local hub' from 'connector', which is exactly the distinction a fixed degree threshold cannot make.  
  <sub>Guimera & Amaral, 'Functional cartography of complex metabolic networks', Nature 433, 895-900 (2005).</sub>
- **Hierarchical random graph + MCMC missing-link prediction** (established, 2008) — Samples dendrograms by Metropolis-Hastings on a likelihood over internal-node connection probabilities; averages p_LCA over samples to predict missing links. Reported to beat local predictors on disassortative structure.  
  <sub>Clauset, Moore, Newman, 'Hierarchical structure and the prediction of missing links in networks', Nature 453, 98-101 (2008).</sub>
- **Trophic levels and trophic incoherence on general directed graphs** (established, 2020) — Assigns each node a continuous level by solving a Laplacian-like SPD system, with no requirement for source nodes, plus a single global number F0 saying how hierarchical the digraph is at all.  
  <sub>MacKay, Johnson, Sansom, 'How directed is a directed network?', Royal Society Open Science 7:201138 (2020).</sub>
- **Ollivier-Ricci curvature on graphs, and Forman-Ricci as the O(1) surrogate** (established, 2009-2019) — Ollivier: 1 - W1(m_u,m_v)/d(u,v) over neighbourhood measures; negative on bridges, positive inside cliques. Forman: a combinatorial O(1) formula from degrees that correlates strongly with Ollivier on sparse graphs.  
  <sub>Ollivier, J. Funct. Anal. 256 (2009); Ni et al., 'Ricci curvature of the Internet topology', IEEE INFOCOM (2015); Sia, Jonckheere, Bogdan, 'Ollivier-Ricci curvature-based method to community detection', Sci. Rep. 9 (2019); Sreejith et al., 'Forman curvature for complex networks', J. Stat. Mech. (2016).</sub>
- **k-core and onion decomposition** (established, 2003-2016) — O(E) exact peeling that gives each node a coreness, refined by the peeling layer within each core. Parameter-free, deterministic, ~40 lines. The correct cheap baseline for any 'how general is this note' score.  
  <sub>Batagelj & Zaversnik, 'An O(m) algorithm for cores decomposition of networks' (2003); Hebert-Dufresne, Grochow, Allard, 'Multi-scale structure and topological anomaly detection via a new network statistic: the onion decomposition', Sci. Rep. 6:31708 (2016).</sub>
- **Poincare maps (hyperbolic 2-D layout from a kNN graph)** (recent-but-replicated, 2020) — kNN graph over feature vectors, global connectivity distances (RFA), then distance-matching in the Poincare ball by Riemannian SGD. Radius reads as hierarchy depth when the data really is a branching process.  
  <sub>Klimovskaia, Lopez-Paz, Bottou, Nickel, 'Poincare maps for analyzing complex hierarchies in single-cell data', Nature Communications 11:2966 (2020).</sub>
- **HypHC and Dasgupta's cost** (recent-but-replicated, 2016-2020) — Continuous relaxation of Dasgupta's hierarchical-clustering cost optimised in the Poincare disk, decoded to a binary tree. Relevant mainly because it establishes what the baseline is: average-linkage has a constant-factor guarantee on the revenue form of the same objective.  
  <sub>Chami, Gu, Chatziafratis, Re, 'From Trees to Continuous Embeddings and Back: Hyperbolic Hierarchical Clustering', NeurIPS 2020; Dasgupta, 'A cost function for similarity-based hierarchical clustering', STOC 2016; Moseley & Wang, NeurIPS 2017.</sub>
- **S1/H2 latent geometry and Mercator** (established, 2008-2019) — Fits hidden degree kappa and angle theta per node giving a calibrated connection probability; maps to H2 with radius = popularity. Mercator initialises angles from a model-corrected Laplacian eigenmap.  
  <sub>Serrano, Krioukov, Boguna, Phys. Rev. Lett. 100, 078701 (2008); Garcia-Perez, Allard, Serrano, Boguna, 'Mercator: uncovering faithful hyperbolic embeddings of complex networks', New J. Phys. 21, 123033 (2019).</sub>
- **Gromov delta-hyperbolicity with landmark estimation** (established, 2015-2016) — Four-point condition over sampled node quadruples measures tree-likeness. The scalable estimator uses a few hundred BFS landmarks rather than sampling quadruples uniformly.  
  <sub>Gromov (1987); Kennedy, Saniee, Narayan, 'On the hyperbolicity of large-scale networks and its estimation', IEEE BigData 2016; Fournier, Ismail, Vigneron, 'Computing the Gromov hyperbolicity of a discrete metric space', Inf. Process. Lett. (2015).</sub>
- **Predictor-family complementarity for link prediction (stacking)** (established, 2020) — 203 predictors across 550 networks; topological, embedding and model-based families are complementary, and no single family dominates. The published prior for expecting a model-based channel to be low-correlated with a local one.  
  <sub>Ghasemian, Hosseinmardi, Galstyan, Airoldi, Clauset, 'Stacking models for nearly optimal link prediction in complex networks', PNAS 117(38) (2020).</sub>
- **Consensus / co-association clustering with abstention** (established, 2002-2012) — Runs a clusterer many times, builds a co-association matrix, and only commits an assignment above an agreement threshold. Gives a map the ability to say 'this note does not clearly belong anywhere'.  
  <sub>Strehl & Ghosh, JMLR 3 (2002); Lancichinetti & Fortunato, 'Consensus clustering in complex networks', Sci. Rep. 2:336 (2012).</sub>
- **Structural entropy / encoding tree** (established, 2016) — Deterministic greedy construction of a nested partition by minimising structural entropy. Same output object as a nested SBM without sampling, useful as the cheap fallback path.  
  <sub>Li & Pan, 'Structural information and dynamical complexity of networks', IEEE Trans. Inf. Theory 62(6) (2016).</sub>
- **Isotonic regression / Platt scaling for probability calibration** (established, 2002-2005) — Maps an uncalibrated score to a calibrated probability with 1-2 effective parameters, fit from the user's own links as labels. The cheap route to 'a threshold below which the panel says nothing'.  
  <sub>Zadrozny & Elkan, KDD 2002; Niculescu-Mizil & Caruana, 'Predicting good probabilities with supervised learning', ICML 2005.</sub>
- **Evaluation instruments: AMI, omega index, Robinson-Foulds, hierarchical LFR** (established, 1981-2010) — AMI for partition agreement corrected for chance; omega index for OVERLAPPING partition agreement (AMI does not apply to link communities); Robinson-Foulds for tree stability across seeds; hierarchical LFR for a known-answer sanity gate.  
  <sub>Vinh, Epps, Bailey, JMLR 11 (2010); Collins & Dent, J. Classification (1988) [omega index]; Robinson & Foulds, Math. Biosci. (1981); Lancichinetti, Fortunato, Radicchi, Phys. Rev. E 78, 046110 (2008).</sub>
- **UNVERIFIED: the 'NeurIPS 2025 hierarchical retrieval needs linear Euclidean dimension' claim** (frontier-unproven, 2025?) — Cited by the original proposal 5 as the justification for going hyperbolic. I could not verify this paper exists or says this (web-search budget exhausted this session). Treat as unverified and do not let it carry the argument for proposal 5.  
  <sub>No verifiable source found. Flagged so it is not used as a load-bearing citation.</sub>

</details>


### 1. [CORRECTED] Block affinity as a NOMINATOR and a weak prior, not a third score — nested DC-SBM over the link graph

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Notes that share no links and no wording still reach the panel, because the plugin has worked out which regions of your vault talk to each other. Receipt: 'via your <cluster label> region, which links to <other label> 8 times'.

**How.** Three corrections to the original, all forced by the shipped code. (1) THE SCORE IS A DEGREE PRIOR. log(e_rs*k_s*k_t/(e_r*e_s)) decomposes into log(omega_rs) + log(k_s) + log(k_t). Inside a single block pair the ranking is EXACTLY the degree product, i.e. prefer hubs — which this project already measured as harmful (PPR as a score cost jina 0.026 recall and pushed top-1 concentration skew 2.3 -> 3.6). Drop the degree terms; keep omega_rs = e_rs/(e_r*e_s) only. That leaves at most B^2 distinct values, so it cannot rank within a block pair. That is the proof it is a nominator and a prior, not a ranker. (2) IT MUST NOMINATE OR IT DOES NOTHING. index-store.ts rank() z-normalises both channels over the CANDIDATE POOL only (shortlist width = max(topK*4, DEFAULT_SHORTLIST=60), plus graph nominations from GraphSignals.candidates()). A channel 'defined for every pair' never sees a pair that did not enter the pool. So: nominate the top-M unlinked notes from the query's own block and its two highest-omega partner blocks, exactly as PPR nominates today, then let RA and content judge. Optionally add z(log omega_rs) as a small additive term with weight <= graphInfluence. (3) DO NOT IMPLEMENT AGGLOMERATIVE MDL FROM SCRATCH. Naive greedy pairwise merging is O(N^2) and dies at 100k. Initialise with Leiden (proposal 3), then do block-level merges plus single-node moves under the microcanonical DL — Peixoto's own agglomerative initialisation. This makes proposal 3 a hard prerequisite, not a sibling. (4) FIT ON THE MASKED GRAPH. bench/v3-levers.mjs builds adj from train edges only; a partition fitted on the full graph leaks the held-out edges straight into e_rs.

**Why it is not redundant.** The RA-blind claim in the original is overstated: measured on this vault, 31% of held-out link pairs have zero shared neighbours, not 'the overwhelming majority'. That 31% is the entire headroom, and PPR nomination already reaches much of it (non-obvious recovery 0.181 -> 0.644). So the realistic prize is a few points on a 31% slice, i.e. +0.01 to +0.02 overall. The Ghasemian complementarity prior is still the right reason to expect low correlation with RA, but state the ceiling honestly before spending three weeks.

**Kill experiment.** Held-out protocol, but measured against the SHIPPED stack (content + RA score + PPR/RA nomination = 0.745), not against content+RA. Admission: Spearman with RA below 0.3 over the candidate pool AND +0.02 recall@10. Primary diagnostic: recall on the 31% zero-shared-neighbour slice, plus the overlap between block-nominated and PPR-nominated candidate sets (if overlap > 0.7 it is PPR with extra steps). Report the number of blocks found and the DL improvement over the one-block null as the null indicator — a channel that finds 1 block is a clean negative, not a bug. Sanity gate: hierarchical LFR n=2000, AMI > 0.8, before blaming the vault. Ship decision on the real IndexStore, not bench/.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Mean undirected degree on the lab vault is about 5.4 (494 notes, 1327 links), at or below the sparse-graph detectability threshold, so the honest MDL answer is likely one or two blocks and a null result. The worse risk is the original's form shipping unchanged and reintroducing the degree/hubness prior the project already rejected.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 (Leiden init) and proposal 13 (incremental maintenance) are both hard prerequisites.


### 2. [DEAD] HRG consensus-dendrogram missing-link channel

*expensive · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Would have been the same surface as the block channel, with an MCMC ensemble underneath.

**How.** Clauset-Moore-Newman dendrogram MCMC, ~200 sampled trees, score = mean p_LCA(i,j). Retained here only so it is not re-proposed later.

**Why it is not redundant.** By the original author's own admission it is not independent of proposal 1 — both are hierarchy-model predictors on the same graph.

**Kill experiment.** Not worth designing. If anyone insists, the only cheap discriminator is the 5-initialisation mixing check (pairwise Spearman > 0.9 of the score tables); it will fail on a 1327-edge graph and that ends it in a day.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD, four independent reasons. (1) No incremental path: GraphSignals.invalidate() drops and rebuilds on every metadata change, and an MCMC chain cannot be maintained across a save — every edit invalidates the ensemble. (2) Scale: CMN themselves cap it at a few thousand vertices with roughly O(n^2) mixing; the requirement is 100k. The 'run it inside Leiden communities and fall back to SBM across communities' rescue means two engines, two calibrations and two failure modes for one feature. (3) Granularity: p_LCA is constant for every pair under the same LCA, so it cannot resolve within the shortlist, which is the only place resolution matters. (4) Redundancy: it predicts the same thing as proposal 1 at ten times the cost. Kill it.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 3. [KEEP, with corrections] Link-community layer on the Vault Map, with an inferential significance gate

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** A toggle on the Vault Map: colour by what notes SAY (today's k-means) or by how they LINK. The product is the disagreement — where your writing and your structure have drifted apart.

**How.** Leiden with the Constant Potts Model over resolvedLinks, gamma swept, keeping partitions stable across a plateau (concrete rule: a gamma window of at least 20% multiplicative width over which the community count does not change). Gate before display: description length of the graph under the DC-SBM induced by the partition versus a single-block DC null; below the null, the toggle is disabled and the map says 'no clear link communities in this vault'. Corrections: (a) implement Leiden, not Louvain — the refinement phase is the whole point, because a disconnected 'community' on a map is precisely the lie vault-map.ts's header forbids; budget 400-600 lines. (b) Run on the largest connected component and render the rest as an explicit 'unlinked' bucket rather than as tiny singleton clusters. (c) The disagreement set is the deliverable, so build the diff view, not just the second colouring. (d) This is the partition every other proposal here depends on, so it ships first.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a ranking channel, so the admission rule does not apply. The redundancy question is against the existing content k-means, and that is a measurement, not an argument.

**Kill experiment.** Keep all three of the original's tests, they are well designed: AMI against content k-means (cut if > 0.8); purity against folders versus the content clustering's 0.65 and the 0.29 shuffled floor recorded in vault-map.ts; null calibration on 20 degree-preserving rewirings, gate must reject in at least 19. ADD the one that decides whether the feature is worth a UI: 20 notes drawn from the disagreement set, blind-judged by the owner as 'yes, that is a real seam in my vault' — bar 60%. AMI < 0.8 only proves the two colourings differ; it does not prove the difference is meaningful.

**Most likely reason it fails.** On a sparse personal vault the DL gate may reject on most vaults, making this visible only to heavy linkers. That is the correct behaviour and cheap to find out, but measure it before building the toggle UI.

**Needs first.** None. Prerequisite for proposals 1, 6, 9, 11, 16.


### 4. [KEEP, with corrections] Generality axis from trophic levels on the DIRECTED link graph

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Each related-note card is tagged 'broader', 'narrower' or 'sibling', so you can see whether the plugin is offering the parent concept or a specific instance.

**How.** Recover the direction graph-signals.ts currently throws away (it calls link(a,b) and link(b,a) and stores a Set, so both direction AND multiplicity are lost). Build directed weighted A from resolvedLinks counts, set Lambda = diag(k_in + k_out), v = k_in - k_out, solve (Lambda - A - A^T) h = v. Corrections that matter: (1) THAT MATRIX IS A LAPLACIAN AND IT IS SINGULAR ONCE PER CONNECTED COMPONENT, not once globally. A real vault graph is a giant component plus a long tail of small ones. Solve per component, pin the mean per component, and NEVER compare h across components — otherwise 'broader/narrower' fires on pairs whose levels are not on the same scale, which will be a large fraction of cards. (2) Jacobi-preconditioned CG, 200-500 iterations, and assert the residual; a Laplacian over long link chains is ill-conditioned. (3) Use link multiplicity as weight — resolvedLinks gives counts and the current Set discards them. (4) Compute F0 per component and gate labels on it globally. One float per note, recomputed in milliseconds, no persistence, no index migration.

**Why it is not redundant.** Orthogonal by construction: every shipped channel is undirected or direction-agnostic, so nothing in the current system can tell 'A is the general case of B' from the reverse. And it re-labels rather than re-ranks, so it cannot cost recall.

**Kill experiment.** Keep the separate directional protocol, with two fixes. (a) Report accuracy PER ground-truth source, never only on the union: folder ancestry is already the axis vault-map.ts measures purity against, so it is a sympathetic and partly circular source; heading containment is sparse. The blind-judged pairs are the only clean signal, so raise them from 30 to 60 and stratify by |delta| so you learn where the threshold should sit. (b) The confound control is NOT log in-degree — it is the onion layer from proposal 18 and the log in/out-degree ratio. Kill if directional accuracy is below 65%, or if partial correlation with onion layer exceeds 0.85 (then ship coreness and delete the CG solver).

**Most likely reason it fails.** Link direction in a personal vault may be a writing-order artefact rather than a semantic one — people link outward from whatever they happen to be editing. F0 exposes that honestly. Second risk: h is coreness in disguise, which proposal 18 tests for the price of 40 lines.

**Needs first.** Proposal 18 should run first as the control. Feeds proposals 6 and 11.


### 5. [CORRECTED] Hyperbolic Vault Map — the measurement baseline is wrong, and that is the whole experiment

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** The Vault Map gains a centre: broad hub-like notes near the middle, specific notes near the rim, topics as wedges that keep their internal branching when you zoom.

**How.** Pipeline as described (symmetrised kNN graph k=15 over mean-centered whole-note vectors, global connectivity distances, distance-matching by Riemannian SGD, root at the medoid) with four engineering corrections. (1) THE BASELINE IS THE EXPERIMENT. Comparing a nonlinear kNN-graph layout against 2-D PCA guarantees a win that has nothing to do with hyperbolic geometry — any graph layout beats a linear projection on trustworthiness. The controlled comparison is the SAME pipeline (same kNN graph, same distances, same loss, same seed) fit in 2-D EUCLIDEAN versus fit in the Poincare ball. If Euclidean also clears the gate, ship Euclidean and skip the manifold. (2) Fit in the Lorentz model in float64 (JS numbers are float64 regardless; Float32Array is a storage format), convert to the ball for display, clamp norm at 1-1e-5. The stated Float32 worry is misdirected — the real hazard is gradient blow-up near the boundary. (3) The medoid path is the DEFAULT above ~5k notes, not a fallback: a global distance matrix is O(N^2) memory and dies well inside the 4 GiB heap. vault-map.ts already computes spherical k-means with a fixed seed, so the medoids are free. (4) Determinism is a shipping requirement here, not a nicety: five seeds must give Procrustes-aligned coordinate correlation >= 0.95, or the map reshuffles between sessions.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is a view, not a channel. Note for the record that Poincare embeddings are already on this project's research-rejected list, so this is a re-entry and needs new evidence — and the new evidence offered (a NeurIPS 2025 hierarchical-retrieval dimension result) is a citation I could not verify in this session. Do not let it carry the argument.

**Kill experiment.** (a) Trustworthiness and continuity at k=10 against 384-dim cosine neighbourhoods, hyperbolic versus the EUCLIDEAN CONTROL, not versus PCA; require +0.03 over the control to justify the manifold, and report the PCA number separately as context. (b) The radius must mean something: Spearman of radial coordinate against five generality proxies — folder depth, in-degree, note length, trophic level h, and onion layer — reaching 0.4 on at least two. If (a) passes and (b) fails, ship the layout with the radial story removed from the UI copy.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The most likely outcome is that the Euclidean control wins the trustworthiness gate and the hyperbolic step buys nothing — which is still a better map than today's PCA, so run the control first and take the cheap win. Second risk: a personal vault is a graph with cycles, not a tree, so the radius has nothing to mean.

**Needs first.** Proposal 8 (cheap tree-likeness diagnostic) first.


### 6. [KEEP, with a measurement leak fixed] Automatic Maps of Content generated from the inferred hierarchy

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** A command: 'Draft a Map of Content for this cluster'. It produces a markdown index note — title, members grouped into sub-sections, each with the reason it was included. Nothing is written unless you ask.

**How.** As proposed — cut the nested partition where groups hold 5-15 notes; title from the existing c-TF-IDF labeller in vault-map.ts; members ordered by trophic level so general notes head the list; per-member 'because' string from evidence the funnel already computes (shared neighbour, shared keyphrase). Detect existing MOCs by high out-degree / low in-degree / title matching a cluster label, and present a DIFF against them rather than a duplicate note. Every emitted string is a title, a keyphrase or a template, so no generative model is touched.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel; an artifact. Its claim is that grouping from link structure plus ordering from link direction produces a list similarity cannot.

**Kill experiment.** Both protocols are right, with one leak closed and one baseline added. LEAK: choosing which cluster to regenerate by majority membership of the MOC's own held-out members uses the answer to pick the question and inflates recall. Fix: pick the cluster by the MOC NOTE's own block membership; when the MOC is left near-isolated after its out-links are deleted (which it will be), pick by c-TF-IDF label match against the MOC title, decided and written down BEFORE members are looked at. BASELINE: the original names 'notes most similar to the cluster label'. Add the harder one — concept search with the title channel already scores member-P@10 0.783 on this exact MOC ground truth. That is the number to beat. If hierarchy cannot beat 0.783, ship concept search with a markdown template around it and skip the partition entirely; that is a legitimate and much cheaper outcome. Keep the blind judged-precision pass (>= 80% 'belongs', >= 1 'I would not have put that there and it is right' per MOC).

**Most likely reason it fails.** The 0.783 concept-search baseline may already do the job, in which case hierarchy earns nothing here. Second risk: generated MOCs that read as filler cost more trust than they earn — the diff-against-existing framing is the containment and should ship first, not second.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 or 12 for the partition; proposal 4 for the ordering; existing c-TF-IDF labeller.


### 7. [CORRECTED] Surprising Connections v3 — buy the coverage with PPR, not with hyperbolic geometry

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** A longer feed of surprising pairs at the same precision: pairs the vault's structure says belong together, which you have never linked and which do not sound alike, and which need not share a single neighbour.

**How.** The original's engine is close to self-contradictory: the hyperbolic layout of proposal 5 is fitted to a kNN graph over CONTENT vectors, so 'the geometry says yes while the wording says no' can only happen through the graph-distance step (RFA or geodesics) — which is PPR with extra machinery and a manifold. Corrected version, one day instead of a month: keep v2's shipped filters (hub endpoints barred, bridge degree <= 25, content-cos floor 0.15, mutual non-obviousness, bridge-sentence receipts) and relax ONLY the '>= 1 shared neighbour' requirement to 'reachable within 3 hops with rooted-PageRank mass above a floor' — which GraphSignals.pprFrom() already computes and already truncates safely. One variable changes, so the experiment is interpretable. If that fails to add coverage at fixed precision, no geometry will rescue it; if it succeeds, the hyperbolic version has to beat it, not beat v2.

**Why it is not redundant.** 31% of held-out link pairs share zero neighbours, and v2 is blind to all of them by construction. PPR reach is the shipped mechanism that already covers that region (non-obvious recovery 0.181 -> 0.644) and is not yet wired into Surprising Connections.

**Kill experiment.** The existing judged-precision protocol. Since v2 already scores P@5 = 1.00 there is no precision headroom, so the PRIMARY metric is pairs-found-per-1000-notes at the score gate that holds precision >= 0.8, i.e. coverage. Report Jaccard overlap with v2's output; below 0.4 to count as new. Also report the surprising fraction (bar >= 30% against v2's 36%) and re-check the isolated-areas leak that the adversarial pass caught, because widening the candidate space is exactly how that class of bug returns.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Widening from shared-neighbour to 3-hop reach may simply lower precision with no coverage gain at the gate — that is the honest null and it costs a day. The hyperbolic form's risk is worse: it depends entirely on proposal 5 producing angular structure that is not the content PCA replotted, and its own measurement cannot distinguish those two cases.

**Needs first.** None for the corrected form. Proposal 5 or 14 only for the hyperbolic variant.


### 8. [CORRECTED] Vault shape report — right instrument, wrong estimator and wrong home

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing, normally. The quiet check that decides whether the hierarchy features are honest on this particular vault, so a settings page can say 'your vault is not tree-like enough for the hyperbolic map' instead of drawing one that lies.

**How.** Corrections. (1) THE SAMPLING SCHEME DOES NOT SCALE. 20,000 random 4-tuples needs BFS from up to 80,000 sources; on a 100k-node graph in JS that is minutes to hours, not an idle nicety. Use the standard landmark estimator: sample 200-500 landmarks, one BFS each (O(L*E)), then evaluate Gromov's four-point condition on quadruples drawn FROM the landmark set using exact landmark-landmark distances. (2) Restrict to the largest connected component; unreachable pairs have infinite distance and silently corrupt the statistic. (3) Report the mean and a far-tail quantile (say 99th), not the max — max delta is one bad quadruple. (4) DO NOT SHIP IT IN INDEXSTORE YET. Ten persisted floats cost an INDEX_VERSION bump (currently 8) on a file that also carries the int8 chunk vectors, and buy nothing until a feature reads them. Build it in bench/ as an instrument; promote to the store only when proposal 4 or 5 actually gates on it. Alongside delta: F0, degree tail exponent, largest-component fraction, and the same delta on the content-kNN graph.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is a measurement, and it is the cheapest way to decide whether proposals 1, 5, 12 and 14 are worth building at all.

**Kill experiment.** The stated cross-vault correlation study (delta versus proposal 5's trustworthiness lift, over ~10 vaults) is underpowered and will not reach significance — say so up front and treat the number as a prior, not a gate. The test that IS decisive and cheap: run the estimator on a synthetic balanced tree (delta should be ~0), an Erdos-Renyi graph of the same size and density (delta should be large), and the real vault, and check the vault lands between them by a margin. If it does not, the estimator is wrong.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Known limitation to state before anyone treats the output as authoritative: normalised delta on sparse low-degree graphs is close to uninformative — most sparse real networks look tree-like by this measure. The honest expected outcome is a diagnostic you read, not a gate that fires.

**Needs first.** None. Build before proposals 5 and 12.


### 9. [CORRECTED] Community-conditioned candidate generation, and a coarse index in the RIGHT space

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Faster results on big vaults, and fewer misses on notes that sit in a small tight corner of the vault.

**How.** (a) RECALL — keep as proposed: after the coarse cosine shortlist, append the top-M notes from the query's own link community that the shortlist missed, then run BiMax and the structural boost unchanged. This gives the partition the nominate-but-do-not-score role that measurement already proved is the safe one. (b) SPEED — the original is in the wrong space and must be replaced. It proposes using the LINK partition as a coarse index for a CONTENT cosine scan; pruning a metric search requires a partition OF THE SPACE BEING PRUNED, and link communities are not a partition of embedding space, so the pruning guarantee is empty and the silent recall loss lands wherever links and text disagree. The correct coarse index is IVF over CONTENT centroids — and vault-map.ts already computes exactly that (spherical k-means, k-means++, 3 restarts, fixed seed) on the centered means, so this is reuse, not a new algorithm. nlist ~ sqrt(N), probe the top few lists plus the query's own, keep COARSE_FLOOR unchanged.

**Why it is not redundant.** Neither part scores, so the admission rule does not apply. Judge on recall-neutrality and wall-clock.

**Kill experiment.** (a) Held-out protocol at fixed fusion weights, measured against the SHIPPED nominator GraphSignals.candidates() (PPR mass + RA). The first number to report is the overlap between community-nominated and PPR-nominated sets: above 0.7 and it adds nothing. Must not decrease recall@10; report the zero-shared-neighbour slice separately. (b) Pure speed: recall@10 loss below 0.005 at >= 3x coarse-stage speedup on a synthetic 100k vault, measured by driving the real IndexStore. Report recall broken down BY CLUSTER SIZE and by note degree — pruned search kills recall exactly on small clusters and it averages out of the aggregate.

**Most likely reason it fails.** (a) is likely to be largely redundant with PPR nomination, which the overlap number exposes in an hour. (b) is the classic silent-recall killer; the per-cluster-size breakdown is not optional reporting, it is the acceptance criterion.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 for (a). None for (b) — it reuses the existing k-means.


### 10. [CORRECTED] Curvature: ship Forman, use it for bridges, and expect the RA reweighting to fail

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** A short list of load-bearing links — remove one and two areas of your thinking stop talking to each other. And, if it measures out, evidence flowing through a genuine bridge counts for more than evidence inside a clique of near-duplicates.

**How.** Corrections. (1) HUB_DEGREE = 40 IS MISREAD in the original. It is not a cap on which edges exist; it filters shared neighbours in GraphSignals.evidence() and blocks frontier expansion in pprFrom(). 'Edges with degree under 40' therefore leaves hub edges with no curvature at all — and hub edges are where the bridges are. Either cap the transport support at the 64 lowest-degree neighbours and sample the rest, or accept undefined-on-hubs and say so in the UI. (2) EXACT OT IS NOT AFFORDABLE AT INDEX TIME. W1 needs pairwise graph distances inside both neighbourhoods, i.e. a local BFS per endpoint: at 100k notes and ~300k edges that is 300k local BFS plus 300k transport solves. Ship Forman-Ricci first — O(1) per edge from degrees and triangle counts, no OT, no BFS — and only reach for Ollivier if Forman clears the bar. (3) USE 2 DOES NOT NEED CURVATURE AT ALL IN v1. The same load-bearing list falls out of proposal 3's partition: inter-community edges ranked by how much they are the only connection between their two communities. Cheaper, legible, and the receipt writes itself. Curvature is the refinement.

**Why it is not redundant.** Use 1 is a reweighting of a shipped channel, so it is admitted or rejected as a delta on the existing protocol with no correlation argument. Use 2 is a hygiene output judged on its own.

**Kill experiment.** Use 1: held-out recall@10, curvature-weighted RA versus plain RA, same seed, same fusion; bar +0.01. Run the confound check FIRST, before writing the transport code — partial correlation of curvature with 1/deg(u) + 1/deg(v). On a graph with mean degree 5.4 most neighbourhoods have two to six members and W1 is nearly determined by the degree pair, in which case RA's own 1/deg term already contains it. Use 2: 30 flagged links blind-judged 'if this vanished would you lose a real connection', bar 70%, and compare the curvature list against the partition-boundary list — if they agree on 80% of the top 20, ship the partition version and drop curvature.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The RA reweighting is most likely degree in disguise on a sparse vault and will miss +0.01. That is fine, because the afternoon-long partial correlation check finds out before any transport code is written. The load-bearing-links view survives independently.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3 for the partition-boundary version of use 2.


### 11. [CORRECTED] Meeting-level LABELS — keep. Diversification — cut.

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Related notes are grouped under headings that say how far away they are in the vault's structure: 'same thread', 'same area', 'far side of the vault'.

**How.** Precompute each note's root-to-leaf block path; meeting level ell(s,t) = depth of the deepest common block; bucket cards into three tiers with boundaries taken from the vault's own distribution of ell over LINKED pairs, so the tiers are calibrated per vault. Correction A: suppress the labels entirely when the fitted hierarchy has two or fewer usable levels — on a 465-note vault with mean degree 5.4 that is the likely case, so measure the depth distribution before building any UI. Correction B: CUT the diversification cap. It trades a measured metric (recall@10) for an unmeasured preference, in a project whose stated rule is precision-first, and on a sidebar showing five to ten cards a cap of four per finest block will either do nothing or evict the correct answer. If it ever ships it ships off by default with the measured recall delta printed in the setting description.

**Why it is not redundant.** Stated plainly and correctly by the original: as a SCORE, ell would correlate heavily with RA and would fail the admission rule. It is a label. Do not let it get smuggled back in as a channel.

**Kill experiment.** Labels: 50 pairs, blind, owner's own 'same thread / same area / far' judgement, bar 70% agreement — a tier label wrong a third of the time is worse than no tier label. Report the ell depth distribution first; if 90% of pairs land in one tier the feature is decorative. Diversification, if anyone insists on trying it: judged P@10 before and after on 20 query notes with no drop, plus a measured rise in distinct-block coverage, plus zero drop in held-out recall@10 measured on the same protocol rather than assumed free.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The hierarchy on a personal vault may be two levels deep, in which case there are no meaningful tiers to name and the whole feature is a rename of 'has shared neighbours'.

**Needs first.** Proposal 1, 3 or 12.


### 12. [CORRECTED] One dendrogram over the vault from the fused similarity — build average-linkage, cut HypHC

*cheap · high confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** A single tree of your whole vault, built from both what notes say and how they link — the backbone the MOC generator, the map and the 'same thread' labels all read from.

**How.** Keep the goal, cut the optimiser. Build average-linkage (or Ward) over the sparse fused top-k similarity graph: about 60 lines, deterministic by construction, no seeds, no manifold, no boundary numerics, and Moseley-Wang gives it a constant-factor guarantee on the revenue form of Dasgupta's objective — so it is the thing to ship, not a strawman. HypHC becomes a later experiment, justified only if the MOC protocol shows tree QUALITY rather than Dasgupta cost is the binding constraint. ONE CODE-GROUNDED CORRECTION THAT APPLIES TO BOTH: the fused score in index-store.ts rank() is z-normalised over the candidate POOL, so it is not comparable across query notes. A global tree built from per-row pool-relative scores is built from a quantity that means different things in different rows. Define a pool-independent fused similarity explicitly — raw BiMax plus graphInfluence * RA with a fixed RA scale — and build the tree from that, or the tree is nonsense in a way no metric will reveal.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. Its distinctive contribution is that it is the only construction here using BOTH channels to build one hierarchy: the link methods see only links, content clustering sees only text.

**Kill experiment.** Dasgupta cost of the tree versus Ward on the same sparse matrix, reported for context only. The DECIDING test is proposal 6's judged MOC protocol, because the tree is not the product.
