Determinism: average-linkage is deterministic given a tie-break rule, so state the tie-break; for any HypHC variant, five seeds must give Robinson-Foulds distance below 10% of maximum or it cannot ship at all.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The corrected form is nearly risk-free and cheap; the risk was in the original scope. HypHC as proposed is speculative: a strong 20-line baseline, small expected margin, seed instability, and boundary numerics, for a structure whose consumers only need 'a' hierarchy. Do not start there.

**Needs first.** Proposal 8 only if the HypHC variant is ever attempted.


### 13. [KEEP, promoted to prerequisite] Incremental partition maintenance with stable labels

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Cluster colours, names and 'same thread' groupings stay put as you write. They do not reshuffle every time you save, and they do not stall the app while a big vault re-partitions.

**How.** As proposed — diff resolvedLinks on edit, mark the two endpoints and their communities dirty, re-run Leiden's local-moving phase on the dirty subgraph plus its one-hop boundary, schedule a full refinement at low idle priority every N edits, and maintain a persistent community-id-to-label map matched across refits by maximum Jaccard overlap (greedy, Hungarian if it matters). Two corrections. (1) THIS IS NOT OPTIONAL INFRASTRUCTURE, IT IS THE GATE on proposals 1, 3, 6, 9 and 11. GraphSignals.invalidate() currently drops the whole adjacency on any metadata change and rebuilds lazily in O(E); that works only because RA and PPR are stateless. A partition is not stateless — without this, every link edit costs a full Leiden run. (2) DO NOT PERSIST IT FIRST. index.json / index.bin are at INDEX_VERSION 8 and carry the int8 chunk vectors; a migration for a partition you can recompute is a bad trade. Recompute at load in the worker (Leiden on 100k nodes / 300k edges is roughly 1-3 s once) and maintain in memory. Persist only if cold-start time is measured to be the problem.

**Why it is not redundant.** Pure infrastructure. A hierarchy that renumbers itself on each save produces a map that reshuffles under the user, which vault-map.ts already treats as a correctness requirement.

**Kill experiment.** Keep all three, they are well specified. (a) Drift: after 500 simulated edits applied incrementally, AMI against a from-scratch refit >= 0.9, and force a full refit below that. (b) Label churn: fraction of notes whose displayed cluster label changes per 100 edits, < 2%. (c) Wall-clock: incremental update under 50 ms at p99 on a synthetic 100k vault, driving the real IndexStore. Prove each by ablation — turn off Jaccard matching and confirm churn explodes. ADD: time to first map after a cold start on the same 100k vault, which is the number that decides whether persistence is needed at all.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Local moving after edits drifts into locally-optimal, globally-wrong partitions, and the failure is silent and slow — which is why (a) has to be a scheduled assertion in the running plugin, not a one-off benchmark.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3.


### 14. [CORRECTED] Calibrated link probability and an abstention threshold — keep the prize, cut the S1 fit

*cheap · high confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Cards carry an actual probability rather than a rank, and because it is a probability there is a threshold below which the panel simply says nothing at all.

**How.** The prize is real and the mechanism is wrong. p_ij = 1/(1+(R*dtheta/(mu*kappa_i*kappa_j))^beta) has the same defect as proposal 1's score: the kappa product is a degree prior, so the induced ranking is mostly popularity, already measured as harmful here. beta estimation on 1327 edges will not be stable. And initialising angles from content PCA makes contamination by the content channel the default outcome. Corrected plan: (STEP 1, one day) run the original's own kill test — fit the angles, measure circular correlation against the content PCA angle; above 0.9 the refinement did nothing and the S1 branch closes. (STEP 2, the deliverable) get calibration without any geometry: fit isotonic regression, or a two-parameter logistic, from the fused score to observed link frequency, using the user's own links as labels on the masked-graph protocol. One to two effective parameters, so it does NOT need the compounding-with-vault-size property whose absence killed personal metric learning — it is a calibration map, not a learned metric. Optional second feature for the fit: the block-pair affinity log(omega_rs) from proposal 1, which is where the hierarchy genuinely earns its place.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a similarity channel at all — it is a monotone map applied after fusion, so it cannot change ranking and cannot correlate with anything. What it adds is the one thing no shipped channel can express: 'I have nothing for you'. It also supplies the vault-size-invariant absolute threshold that Mutual Proximity was rejected for failing to deliver.

**Kill experiment.** Expected calibration error below 0.1 on bucketed held-out link frequency. Then the number that actually matters: fixed-threshold drift across nested vault sizes, measured exactly as bench/v3-calibration.mjs measured Mutual Proximity — bar: beat cosine's 0.018 drift (MP scored 0.030). Report how many links are needed before the fit stabilises (bootstrap over subsets of the user's links); if it needs more than ~200 it is not usable on a new vault and must fall back to a fixed prior.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Isotonic on a small link set overfits; use a two-parameter logistic below a few hundred links and only switch to isotonic above it. The S1 branch's risk is that the kill test fires immediately — which is the point of running it first and budgeting one day.

**Needs first.** None for the calibration deliverable. Proposal 8 for the S1 branch, if anyone pursues it.


### 15. [NEW] Link communities: a note belongs to several threads, not one cluster

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Each note shows the threads it takes part in — 'this note is part of: Fourier analysis, exam prep' — instead of being forced into a single coloured blob. On the map, notes in two threads sit on the seam between them rather than being assigned by a coin flip.

**How.** Ahn-Bagrow-Lehmann link communities, on Obsidian's resolvedLinks. For each pair of edges sharing a node k, similarity S(e_ik, e_jk) = |N+(i) ∩ N+(j)| / |N+(i) ∪ N+(j)| over inclusive neighbourhoods; single-linkage hierarchical clustering over EDGES; cut at the maximum of partition density D = (2/M) * sum_c m_c * (m_c - (n_c - 1)) / ((n_c - 2)(n_c - 1)). A note inherits the communities of its incident edges, so membership is overlapping by construction and hierarchical for free (the linkage tree gives every coarser level). Cost is O(sum_k deg(k)^2) edge pairs; cap or sample incident edges at nodes above degree ~200, which is the same hub-capping precedent GraphSignals already sets with HUB_DEGREE. Store per note the community ids of its incident edges and the fraction of its edges in each. Names come from the existing c-TF-IDF labeller.

**Why it is not redundant.** It is not a ranking channel and should not be admitted as one — edge-neighbourhood Jaccard is close kin to shared-neighbour counting and would almost certainly correlate with RA above 0.3. Its contribution is REPRESENTATIONAL: every structure the plugin currently has — k-means clusters, folders, SBM blocks, Leiden communities — forces a note into exactly one group, and the most common truth about a personal note is that it is legitimately about three things. This is the only construction in this area that fixes that, and it is what makes proposal 11's 'same thread' label honest for a note in three threads.

**Kill experiment.** (1) Overlap rate: fraction of notes with >= 2 communities. Below 10% the vault does not need this and the feature is cut on the spot. (2) Against multi-label ground truth: for notes carrying two or more top-level tag namespaces, does link-community membership recover the multi-label structure better than the single k-means assignment? Use the omega index (AMI does not apply to overlapping partitions). Report this as a sanity check only, not proof — tags correlate 0.916 with the graph channel, so it is a sympathetic ground truth. (3) The deciding test: 30 notes, owner marks whether the listed threads are right and whether any obvious thread is missing, bar 70% correct with fewer than 20% missing threads.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Single-linkage is chain-prone: one high-similarity edge pair can merge two unrelated threads, and on a sparse vault graph that is a live hazard. Mitigation is the partition-density cut plus an explicit maximum community size, both measured by ablation. Second risk: on a 1327-edge vault the overlap rate may be too low to matter, which test (1) settles in an afternoon.

**Needs first.** None. Independent of proposal 3, and worth running head to head against it on the map.


### 16. [NEW] Cartographic roles — and a principled replacement for the hard-coded HUB_DEGREE = 40

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Notes get a role from how their links sit relative to your vault's areas: 'connector' (its links reach many different areas), 'local hub' (heavily linked inside one area), 'peripheral'. The hygiene view lists the connectors — the notes actually holding your vault together.

**How.** Guimera-Amaral cartography over any partition (proposal 3). Per note: within-module degree z-score z_i = (k_i^within - mean_module) / sd_module, and participation coefficient P_i = 1 - sum_c (k_ic / k_i)^2. Collapse the 7-region map to three useful buckets: z > 2.5 -> hub, P > 0.6 -> connector, otherwise peripheral. Two floats per note, O(E) to compute, recomputable on the fly, no persistence. The engineering payoff is the second use: GraphSignals uses a GLOBAL constant HUB_DEGREE = 40 in evidence() and pprFrom() to decide what counts as an index note. That constant is wrong in both directions — a degree-60 note whose links are all inside one area is a legitimate topic note that RA should still count, while a degree-20 note spread over eight areas is exactly the MOC-like index the filter is trying to exclude. Replacing the degree threshold with a participation threshold is a two-line change inside a shipped, already-measured channel.

**Why it is not redundant.** The filter change is not a new channel — it is a delta on an existing one, evaluated on the existing protocol with no correlation argument needed, which makes it unusually cheap to settle. The role pill is a label.

**Kill experiment.** Filter change: held-out recall@10 with participation-based hub exclusion versus fixed HUB_DEGREE = 40, same seed, same fusion, both in bench and then on the real IndexStore; bar +0.01. Report how many notes change hub status — below 5 the vault cannot tell the two rules apart and the change is cosmetic. Confound: partial correlation of P_i with degree; above 0.85 and it is the degree threshold with more steps. Sweep the participation threshold and report the recall curve rather than a single point, because the current 40 was itself never swept. Role labels: 20 flagged connectors blind-judged 'is this note doing the job of connecting two areas', bar 70%.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Participation coefficient inherits every weakness of the partition it is computed on, so if proposal 3's DL gate rejects on a vault, roles are undefined there and must fall back to the current degree rule. Also, on notes with degree below about 5 the coefficient is quantised into a handful of values and is close to meaningless — exclude them explicitly rather than letting them land in a bucket.

**Needs first.** Proposal 3.


### 17. [NEW] Consensus clustering with abstention — let the Vault Map leave notes grey

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** On the map, a note is coloured and named only when the vault's structure actually agrees about where it belongs. Notes that genuinely sit between areas stay grey and unlabelled instead of being forced into whichever cluster won a coin flip. The legend gains 'unassigned'.

**How.** Run the existing spherical k-means R = 20 times with different seeds and k drawn from {k*-2 ... k*+2} around suggestK(n), and add the link-Leiden partitions from proposal 3's gamma sweep as extra ensemble members. Build the co-association matrix SPARSELY, over each note's top-50 content neighbours only — O(N * 50), not O(N^2), which matters because the adversarial pass already caught a dense N x N Map costing 469 MB at 3k notes and about 21 GB at 20k. Assign a note to its consensus cluster only when its maximum co-association fraction >= tau (start at 0.7); otherwise mark it unassigned. Compute the c-TF-IDF label from confidently-assigned members only, which also stops boundary notes dragging a label. Everything stays deterministic: the seed set is fixed, so the ensemble is reproducible.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel. It is the project's own precision-first rule applied to the one shipped surface that currently cannot abstain — the panel has minSimilarity, but the map colours every note, so its stated failure mode ('a map that misleads') is presently unguarded. It is also already on the owner's own next-lever list ('co-association ensembles with ABSTENTION for clustering'), which suggests it was missed rather than rejected.

**Kill experiment.** (1) Purity on confidently-assigned notes versus the current 0.65, against the 0.29 shuffled floor: require >= 0.75 at <= 25% abstention. The abstention must BUY purity, not merely hide the hard cases — so report the purity-versus-abstention curve, not one point. (2) Decision stability: run the pipeline on two disjoint halves of the seed set and require the assigned/unassigned verdict to agree on >= 90% of notes. (3) Label churn under proposal 13's incremental refits, which abstention should reduce — report the delta with and without. (4) Ablation: tau = 0 must reproduce today's map exactly, or the implementation silently changed something else.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Abstention rate could come out high enough that the map looks empty, which reads as a broken feature rather than an honest one — hence the <= 25% cap as an acceptance criterion rather than an observation. Second risk: k-means restarts with a fixed seed set may be too correlated to disagree about anything, making the co-association matrix nearly binary; adding the link-Leiden members and varying k is the mitigation, and the abstention rate itself reveals if it failed.

**Needs first.** None. Improved by proposal 3.


### 18. [NEW] k-core / onion decomposition — the 40-line generality axis, and the control every fancier score must beat

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Notes are marked as core or leaf: 'this is one of the notes your vault is built around' versus 'this is a tip'. It reads as depth rather than popularity, because a core note is one that survives when you repeatedly strip away everything with few connections.

**How.** Batagelj-Zaversnik core decomposition by repeated peeling: exact, O(E), parameter-free, deterministic, about 40 lines. The onion refinement records the peeling LAYER within each core, turning a coarse coreness into a fine integer per note. One int per note, recomputed in milliseconds from the adjacency GraphSignals already builds — no persistence, no INDEX_VERSION migration, no incremental machinery, and therefore immune to every scaling objection raised against proposals 1, 2, 5 and 12. Two uses: a 'core/leaf' pill in the panel and hygiene view, and the mandatory control variable for every other abstractness score in this area.

**Why it is not redundant.** It must NEVER be a ranking channel — coreness is a monotone function of degree in expectation and would fail the admission rule immediately, the same way a hubness prior does. Its value is (a) as the honest control: if trophic level h, hyperbolic radius r, or SBM block degree correlates above 0.85 with onion layer, the fancier method bought nothing and should be deleted; and (b) as the shippable fallback when they do, because it delivers most of the user-facing story for a fortieth of the code.

**Kill experiment.** (a) As a control: partial correlation of h (proposal 4) and of the hyperbolic radius (proposal 5) against onion layer, computed before either of those features gets UI work. Above 0.85 kills the fancier score outright. (b) As a feature: the same directional protocol as proposal 4 — 60 blind-judged pairs, 'is A broader than B', where the coreness/onion-layer difference predicts the direction. If coreness alone lands within 5 accuracy points of h, ship coreness and delete the conjugate-gradient solver. Also report the distribution of onion layers: if 80% of the vault sits in the 1-core the vault has no core-periphery structure and neither this nor h has anything to say.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Coreness is degree-correlated by construction, so as a standalone feature it risks being a popularity pill with a better name — which is exactly why the blind directional judgement, not the correlation, decides whether it ships to users. On a very sparse vault most notes land in the 1-core or 2-core and the axis collapses; the layer distribution check catches that in minutes.

**Needs first.** None. Build FIRST, before proposals 4 and 5, because it is their control.


## Retrieval architecture: the engine underneath


<details><summary>19 techniques surveyed</summary>

- **MUVERA / Fixed Dimensional Encodings** (recent-but-replicated, 2024) — Compresses a multi-vector document into one fixed-length vector whose inner product approximates Chamfer/MaxSim similarity, so a single-vector ANN scan can rank by a multi-vector objective. Built for ColBERT-scale documents (hundreds of token vectors). At this plugin's scale (3-21 vectors per note, adaptive cap 20 body chunks at 100k notes, see ADAPTIVE_CHUNK_TIERS in src/index-store.ts:229) the exact max-over-units it approximates is affordable directly, which is why I demote it to a fallback arm.  
  <sub>Jayaram et al., 'MUVERA: Multi-Vector Retrieval via Fixed Dimensional Encodings', NeurIPS 2024 (arXiv id cited by the prior researcher as 2405.19504 — I could not verify it this session, web search budget exhausted)</sub>
- **Binary embedding quantization with rescoring cascade** (established, 2024) — Sign-threshold each dimension after centering, retrieve by Hamming distance over packed words, rescore the survivors with the full-precision vectors. Published retention figures (~96%) come mostly from 768-1024-dim models; MiniLM is 384-dim, so the low-dim case is the open question. Centering is what makes sign-thresholding work, and the plugin already computes the centroid (computeCentroid in src/vector-math.ts:86).  
  <sub>sentence-transformers embedding-quantization documentation and accompanying blog post, HuggingFace, 2024</sub>
- **RaBitQ** (recent-but-replicated, 2024) — Randomly rotates the vector before binarizing and stores one scalar correction per vector, giving an actual error bound on the estimated inner product — which product quantization does not have. The fallback if plain sign-thresholding loses too much at 384 dims.  
  <sub>Gao and Long, 'RaBitQ: Quantizing High-Dimensional Vectors with a Theoretical Error Bound for Approximate Nearest Neighbor Search', SIGMOD 2024</sub>
- **Static pruning of dense indexes via matrix decomposition** (recent-but-replicated, 2025) — Fits a rotation (PCA/SVD) on the corpus and stores only the leading coefficients, so cosine is preserved exactly at full rank and truncation error is bounded by the tail eigenvalue mass. The per-vault analogue is what proposal 3 wants. Note the plugin has already measured the opposite operation on the same corpus: all-but-the-top (removing the leading directions) hurt, R@10 0.693 to 0.657.  
  <sub>'Static Pruning in Dense Retrieval using Matrix Decomposition', ECIR 2025 (arXiv id not verified this session); the counter-measurement is Mu and Viswanath, 'All-but-the-Top', ICLR 2018, measured and rejected in lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 197</sub>
- **Single-vector capacity bound for set retrieval (LIMIT)** (recent-but-replicated, 2025) — Shows that a single-vector dual encoder provably cannot realize all top-k sets past a dimension-dependent bound. This is the reason to be sceptical of aggressive PCA truncation to 64 dims: the loss is not smooth fidelity, it is a hard reduction in the number of realizable top-k sets, and it should land on the long-tail non-obvious matches first.  
  <sub>Weller et al., DeepMind, LIMIT benchmark, 2025 (already cited in lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md Lens 4)</sub>
- **Resource Allocation link prediction** (established, 2009) — Scores a pair by the sum over shared neighbours x of 1/deg(x). Already shipped in src/graph-signals.ts on the wikilink graph; proposal 4 asks whether the same functional form pays on a content-derived mutual-kNN graph.  
  <sub>Zhou, Lu and Zhang, 'Predicting missing links via local information', Eur. Phys. J. B, 2009</sub>
- **Mutual k-NN graph construction** (established, 2011) — Keeps an edge only when both endpoints are in each other's top-k, which suppresses hubs at the source. The lab research doc already reviewed this and its own recommendation was to use it as a display gate with a receipt, not as a ranker.  
  <sub>Ozaki et al., 'Using the Mutual k-Nearest Neighbor Graphs for Semi-supervised Classification of Natural Language Data', CoNLL 2011 (reviewed in lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 198)</sub>
- **k-reciprocal encoding re-ranking** (established, 2017) — Expands each neighbour set with the half-size reciprocal sets of its members before intersecting, which is what makes reciprocal-neighbour re-ranking robust rather than brittle. The strengthening step proposals 4 and 5 both lean on.  
  <sub>Zhong et al., 'Re-ranking Person Re-identification with k-reciprocal Encoding', CVPR 2017, arXiv:1701.08398</sub>
- **NN-Descent** (established, 2011) — Builds an approximate k-NN graph in roughly O(N^1.14) by iteratively improving neighbour lists from neighbours-of-neighbours, for any similarity measure. This is the missing prerequisite for proposals 4 and 5: an exact mutual-kNN graph at 100k notes is 5e9 pairs, which neither proposal costed.  
  <sub>Dong, Charikar and Li, 'Efficient K-Nearest Neighbor Graph Construction for Generic Similarity Measures', WWW 2011</sub>
- **Convex-combination fusion of retrieval scores** (established, 2023) — alpha*s1 + (1-alpha)*s2 beats reciprocal-rank fusion in-domain and out-of-domain and is sample-efficient at one parameter. The plugin's z-sum with graphInfluence=1 is exactly the alpha=0.5 case; RRF was measured on this vault and lost (0.729 vs 0.782).  
  <sub>Bruch et al., 'An Analysis of Fusion Functions for Hybrid Retrieval', ACM TOIS 2023, arXiv:2210.11934 (already cited in lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 217)</sub>
- **DIME / ECLIPSE dimension importance estimation** (frontier-unproven, 2024-2025) — Chooses per-query which embedding dimensions matter, scoring each dimension by its separation between pseudo-relevant and (in ECLIPSE) pseudo-irrelevant sets, then masking the rest before rescoring. ECLIPSE's contrast against a pseudo-irrelevant centroid is the part with the large reported effect.  
  <sub>Faggioli et al., 'Dimension Importance Estimation for Dense Information Retrieval', SIGIR 2024; ECLIPSE, SIGIR 2025 (arXiv id cited as 2412.14967, unverified this session)</sub>
- **Rocchio pseudo-relevance feedback / dataless classification** (established, 2014) — Builds a prototype from the top-k retrieved items and re-ranks against it. Measured positively on this project already: concept-search member-P@10 0.650 to 0.683 on jina. The positive half of proposal 11's prior.  
  <sub>Song and Roth, 'On Dataless Hierarchical Text Classification', AAAI 2014 (cited in lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md Lens 4; measured in bench/v3-concept.mjs)</sub>
- **Attribution for Siamese encoders (integrated Jacobian)** (recent-but-replicated, 2023) — Decomposes a Siamese cosine into a token-by-token matrix that provably sums to the model's actual score. Needs gradients, which onnxruntime-web does not provide, so only the forward-only occlusion approximation is buildable — and occlusion is the unstable version.  
  <sub>Moeller et al., 'An Attribution Method for Siamese Encoders', EMNLP 2023, arXiv:2310.05703; follow-up 'Approximate Attributions for Off-the-Shelf Siamese Transformers', EACL 2024</sub>
- **TopK sparse autoencoders** (frontier-unproven, 2024) — Trains an overcomplete dictionary with a hard top-k activation, giving sparse named latents. Every published result comes from corpora orders of magnitude larger than a personal vault, and the plugin has an explicit prior rejection of on-device training (DEC, rejected for 'no renderer training path; self-training amplifies its own errors').  
  <sub>Gao et al., 'Scaling and evaluating sparse autoencoders', OpenAI, 2024, arXiv:2406.04093</sub>
- **Shallow cross-encoder reranking under a latency budget** (established, 2024) — At a tight latency budget a shallow cross-encoder outperforms a deeper one. The result is real; the problem is that every available checkpoint is trained on asymmetric query-to-passage data, and the repo's own model A/B found retrieval-tuned models rank symmetric note-to-note similarity worse (BGE-M3 0.35 vs MiniLM 0.43).  
  <sub>MacAvaney and Tonellotto, 'A Reproducibility Study of PLAID' / 'Shallow Cross-Encoders for Low-Latency Retrieval', ECIR 2024, arXiv:2403.20222</sub>
- **Inference-free learned sparse retrieval (SPLADE-doc)** (established, 2024) — Precomputes document-side term weights over the whole vocabulary at index time, including terms absent from the text, so the match is on expansion rather than overlap and no model runs at query time. The expansion vocabulary is learned from English MS MARCO query-to-passage data, which is the worst case for a bilingual German compound vault.  
  <sub>Lassance et al., 'SPLADE-v3', 2024, arXiv:2403.06789; efficiency/inference-free variants in the same line</sub>
- **Conformal risk control** (established, 2022) — Chooses a threshold with a finite-sample guarantee on a monotone loss such as false-discovery proportion of the displayed set. Requires exchangeability between calibration and deployment pairs, which is exactly what a vault violates: the calibration positives are links the user already made, and the product exists to surface the ones they did not.  
  <sub>Angelopoulos et al., 'Conformal Risk Control', 2022, arXiv:2208.02814</sub>
- **Paired bootstrap significance testing for IR** (established, 2017-2019) — Resamples the query set with replacement and recomputes the delta between two systems on the same resample, giving a confidence interval on the paired difference. The pairing is what makes it far tighter than comparing two independent intervals — necessary here because the lab vault has only 236 held-out links, so the binomial SE on recall@10 is about 0.028.  
  <sub>Sakai, 'Statistical Reform in Information Retrieval?', SIGIR Forum 2014; Urbano et al., 'Statistical Significance Testing in Information Retrieval', SIGIR 2019; Fuhr, 'Some Common Mistakes In IR Evaluation, And How They Can Be Avoided', SIGIR Forum 2017</sub>
- **Hubness and k-occurrence skewness diagnostics** (established, 2010) — Quantifies how often a point appears in others' top-k lists. Relevant because reverse-kNN asymmetry (being in someone's top-k when they are not in yours) is information that a symmetric cosine discards, and because this vault's measured skew is 0.57, well below the 2.5-3 band where the literature says hubness corrections help.  
  <sub>Radovanovic, Nanopoulos and Ivanovic, 'Hubs in Space: Popular Nearest Neighbors in High-Dimensional Data', JMLR 2010 (measured on this vault in lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 199)</sub>

</details>


### 1. Fix the Stage-1 objective mismatch — but with exact idea-level MaxSim, not an FDE approximation

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Notes that relate to you through one paragraph stop getting dropped before they are ever considered. Nothing new appears in the panel; it just stops missing things.

**How.** CORRECTED. The premise is right and I verified it in the code: rank() (src/index-store.ts:2492) builds the shortlist by cosineSimilarity(selfMean, centeredMean(entry)) and Stage 2 scores by biMax over chunk vectors — two different objectives — and the default width is 60 (DEFAULT_SHORTLIST, src/index-store.ts:145), which at 100k notes is a 0.06% cut. But the proposed fix is imported from the wrong scale. MUVERA's FDE exists because ColBERT documents carry hundreds of token vectors, making an exact max-over-units scan prohibitive. Here a note carries 3-21 vectors (ADAPTIVE_CHUNK_TIERS caps body chunks at 20 above 10k notes) and the plugin ALREADY computes an idea layer (ideaOf per row, aggregateIdeaVectors, ideaMaxSim at src/index-store.ts:3004) of roughly 3-8 vectors per note. So compute the thing FDE approximates. Store the per-note idea vectors as a second small int8 block (about 8 x 384 bytes = 3 KB/note worst case, and this is the same block proposal 2 binarizes). Stage 1 becomes: max over the active note's idea vectors of max over the candidate's idea vectors — BiMax's own objective at idea granularity, exact, not estimated. It degrades to today's behaviour exactly on a 1-idea note, which is precisely where FDE degenerates into a noisy copy of the mean. Pay for the 5x dot count with proposal 2's sign vectors at the idea level (48 bytes per idea, Hamming popcount), which brings the scan back under today's cost. Keep FDE as arm (c) of the experiment so the comparison is honest, not as the primary.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a channel; the admission rule does not apply. It removes a recall leak inside the existing funnel and is measured against a different yardstick: agreement with exhaustive BiMax.

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED, and the order matters. FIRST, before writing any encoder: compute exhaustive BiMax for all ~108k pairs on the 465-note vault offline, and measure what fraction of each note's oracle BiMax top-10 the CURRENT mean-cosine Stage 1 already captures AT THE SHIPPED WIDTH OF 60 (the original proposal only tested 50/100/200 and never the default). If that is already above 0.95 there is no leak, the proposal dies for the cost of one script, and that is the best outcome available. If there is a leak, compare (a) current mean-cosine, (b) exact idea-max, (c) FDE, at widths 60/100/200, reporting per-note-length deciles. Then rerun held-out links end to end through the real rank() — per the project's own integration-test rule, a reimplemented harness measures the wrong code. KILL the FDE arm if exact idea-max matches or beats it, which I expect. KILL the whole item if the width-60 oracle recall is already above 0.95.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The most likely outcome is that there is no meaningful leak, because the shortlist is already relaxed by two mechanisms the proposal ignored: COARSE_FLOOR_CENTERED is -0.1 (so almost nothing is floored out) and the graph channel injects up to width/2 extra structural nominations that never passed Stage 1 at all (src/index-store.ts:2535-2551). Second risk: idea vectors are derived from centered chunk rows, so every centroid recomputation invalidates them — which is unmanageable until my proposal 16 lands.

**Needs first.** Idea grouping already exists and is persisted (ideaOf in the manifest). Needs proposal 16 first, or the derived block thrashes.


### 2. Binary Stage 0, and the case for NOT shipping an ANN index

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Large vaults get fast: the panel fills in the same fraction of a second at 100,000 notes as at 500, and the index on disk gets smaller rather than bigger.

**How.** CORRECTED — the conclusion is right, both supporting arguments are wrong. (a) The plugin does not use IndexedDB. It persists to configDir/index.json plus index.bin via vault.adapter.writeBinary/read and holds everything in RAM as typed-array views into one ArrayBuffer (serializeIndex/deserializeIndex in src/vector-math.ts). So the WebANNS IndexedDB-round-trip argument is a red herring. The real and stronger argument against HNSW: Stage 1 is ALREADY a linear scan over an in-RAM contiguous blob, and the incremental idle-indexing model means an HNSW graph would be under continuous repair for a speedup on a path that is not the bottleneck. Keep the recommendation, which is a live course correction — the roadmap currently has 'an ANN index for 100k notes' queued for v5. (b) The speedup is claimed against the wrong number. At 100k notes the fp32 mean scan is about 38M multiply-adds, tens of milliseconds. Meanwhile centeredMeans is a 153 MB shadow Map fully rebuilt on every entries change (recomputeCentroid, src/index-store.ts:2264, called from four sites), and load() JSON.parses the manifest TWICE (line 1361 for the header check, then again inside deserializeIndex at line 1392) while that manifest carries every chunk's raw text. Hamming Stage 0 saves 30 ms on a path where load takes minutes. So: do this AFTER proposals 15 and 16, and binarize the IDEA vectors from proposal 1, not only the note means — that is where it buys recall rather than just speed. 48 bytes per idea, roughly 24 MB at 100k notes.

**Why it is not redundant.** Pure infrastructure. Changes no ranking and adds no channel.

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED. (1) Recall of the exact top-50 by the binary prefilter at cut sizes 500/1000/2000, on the real vault and on a 100k synthetic vault built from real note text — and separately at the IDEA level, which is the arm that matters. (2) Wall clock p50/p95 for a full panel refresh at 5k/20k/100k, WASM only, reported ALONGSIDE the load time and the per-edit recentring time so the reader can see which one dominates. (3) Held-out link recall@10 end to end must be statistically unchanged versus exact scan — judged by the paired bootstrap from proposal 18, not by eyeballing a delta smaller than the 0.028 standard error. KILL IT if the binary prefilter loses more than 1% of the exact top-10 at a 20x cut.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Binary quantization quality is dimension-hungry and MiniLM is 384-dim, below the 768-1024 range that produced the published ~96% retention figures; RaBitQ or a larger cut both erode the speedup. Bigger risk: shipping this first would be optimizing a path that is already fast while the load path OOMs, which is what the numbers above say is actually happening at 100k.

**Needs first.** Proposals 15 and 16 first. Mean-centering must be materialized at index time, which proposal 16 does anyway.


### 3. Vault-fitted PCA cascade: measure the spectrum before writing the cascade

*cheap · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** The index shrinks by more than half and the first results appear sooner, with no change in what is shown.

**How.** CORRECTED in gate order and risk, mechanism kept. Fit a PCA on the centered note vectors during idle using the power-iteration code in src/vault-map.ts:141 (pca2, currently hardcoded to 2 components — generalizing it to k is a small change), store the 384x384 rotation once (590 KB), store each vector's top-k coefficients. Orthonormality preserves cosine exactly at full rank and bounds truncation error by the tail eigenvalue mass. Stage 0 becomes a 64-dim int8 dot, Stage 1 promotes to 192, BiMax uses full precision. THE CORRECTION IS THAT THE FIRST DELIVERABLE IS A NUMBER, NOT A CASCADE: the retained-variance curve of the centered note means on three real vaults is about twenty lines of code against vectors that already exist. If the curve is flat, the premise is dead and nothing gets built. Report the curve in the debug panel either way — it is a genuinely interesting statistic about a vault.

**Why it is not redundant.** Infrastructure, not a channel. An alternative to proposal 2 on the same budget, and composable with it (PCA-then-binarize is what RaBitQ's rotation is doing anyway).

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED on the kill criterion. Retained variance at 32/64/128/192 on three real vaults, first and alone. Then recall of the exact top-50 by the truncated stage at each width. Then held-out link recall@10 end to end. THE KILL CRITERION MUST BE MEASURED ON THE NON-OBVIOUS SLICE, not on pooled recall@10: 30.7% of the user's links have targets outside the source's content top-10 (lab/results/graph-fusion-eval2.json), that slice is the product's whole reason for existing, and pooled recall is dominated by the obvious 69%, so a cascade could lose the discoveries and still look fine. KILL IT if 64 dims loses more than 2% of exact top-50 recall, if the non-obvious slice loses anything at all, or if the retained-variance curve is flat.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The plugin already measured the closest relative of this operation on this exact corpus and it lost: all-but-the-top (removing the leading principal directions) took R@10 from 0.693 to 0.657 and RAISED hubness skew (lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 197). Truncation-from-the-tail is the opposite surgery, so that result does not condemn it, but it does say this corpus punishes principal-axis surgery. Second, and this is the risk the original missed: the LIMIT capacity bound says a single-vector encoder's realizable top-k sets are dimension-bounded, so cutting to 64 dims is not a smooth fidelity loss — it removes representable orderings, and the tail-most orderings are exactly the non-obvious ones. Third, the refit-invalidation problem is under-costed: recomputeCentroid already rebuilds every centered mean on every entries change, and adding a rotation makes it O(N x D^2).

**Needs first.** pca2 in src/vault-map.ts generalized past 2 components. Proposal 16 for the invalidation discipline.


### 4. Content Resource Allocation over a mutual-kNN graph — lab measurement only, no plugin code

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Two notes that quietly share the same small circle of neighbours surface even when they do not read alike, the same way two notes that link to the same third note already do.

**How.** CORRECTED to a lab-only experiment, and downgraded. The idea is legitimate and the researcher's own honesty about the correlation risk is the right instinct. Two things they missed. FIRST, the project has already reviewed this exact object: lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 198 lists mutual-kNN (Ozaki et al., CoNLL 2011) and its own conclusion was 'untested; recommended as a display gate with a receipt, NOT a ranker'. That is a documented prior against the ranker framing, from the same research pass that produced the shipped design. SECOND, and this is what makes the stated cost of 'moderate' wrong: building the mutual-kNN graph is O(N^2). At 100k notes that is 5e9 pairs x 384 dims. The proposal waves it off as a by-product of vault insights, but vault insights runs rank() per note, which is itself O(N^2) and already caused a measured 469 MB / 21 GB blowup in surprisingConnections that had to be fixed. Making this shippable requires NN-Descent or equivalent — that is, an approximate-neighbour index, which proposal 2 explicitly recommends against shipping. The two proposals are in direct conflict and neither notices. So: run it offline on the 465-note vault where 108k pairs is trivial, and treat 'we solved kNN-graph construction at 100k' as a separate, unfunded prerequisite for any build.

**Why it is not redundant.** This is the honest hard case and the researcher framed it correctly. The genuinely strong argument they did not make is coverage, not independence: 31% of held-out link pairs share ZERO common neighbours in the wikilink graph, where link-RA is identically zero. Content-RA is non-zero on exactly that population. That is a better hypothesis to test than 'RA is non-monotone in cosine'.

**Kill experiment.** Existing protocol with the admission gate applied first and hard, plus one addition. Kendall/Spearman rank correlation of content-RA against (a) content and (b) link-RA over the same candidate pools that produced the 0.075 and 0.916 figures. Gate: |rho| < 0.35 against both. THEN, before the recall run, the coverage test: restricted to the 31% of held-out pairs with zero shared wikilink neighbours, does content-RA rank the true target better than content alone? That slice is where the channel would earn its place and it is where link-RA is structurally blind. Then held-out recall@10 with three-channel fusion, judged by paired bootstrap (proposal 18) — the raw delta will be inside the noise otherwise. KILL IT if rho > 0.35 with content, or if the zero-shared-neighbour slice shows no lift.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Most likely outcome is rho of 0.4 to 0.6 with content and a wash on recall, because the mutual-kNN graph is a deterministic function of the same cosines the content channel already ranks by. On top of that, even a passing result is not shippable until the O(N^2) construction problem is solved, and solving it means shipping the approximate-neighbour index that proposal 2 argues against. Worth one afternoon in the bench; not worth a line of plugin code before it passes.

**Needs first.** Lab only. A shipped version needs NN-Descent or equivalent, which is unfunded and conflicts with proposal 2.


### 5. Content-graph diffusion as a nominator — run the reach metric, then stop

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** The panel can reach notes two hops away in meaning, related to something related to you, that a direct similarity search structurally cannot see.

**How.** CORRECTED and downgraded to a single decisive experiment. The architectural instinct (nominate, do not score) is exactly right and is the lesson the project already learned from PPR. But two measured priors on this vault point the other way and the researcher missed both. MUSAE — multi-scale propagation of node ATTRIBUTES over a graph, which is structurally the same operation — was measured here at 0.737 and does NOT beat plain content+AA at 0.769. And L3 (length-3 paths), the theoretically correct tool for the 31% of pairs that share no neighbours, was measured and rejected at 0.606 with no lift when fused. Two independent measurements saying multi-hop propagation does not pay on this corpus. What survives is the one question neither answered: reach. So run only that. Build the k-reciprocal affinity on the 465-note vault offline, row-normalize, 3-5 steps of f <- alpha*S*f + (1-alpha)*e_a from the active note, and ask the single question below. Do not write the plugin path, do not tune sigma and alpha, do not touch the candidate pool, until reach passes.

**Why it is not redundant.** It produces no score, so it cannot correlate with one. It changes the candidate pool in a direction neither existing nominator reaches: link-graph PPR nominates by citation structure, content Stage 1 by direct proximity.

**Kill experiment.** ONE metric, and it decides everything. On the 465-note vault: what fraction of held-out link targets sit OUTSIDE the content top-100 but INSIDE the diffusion top-100? Report it with a bootstrap CI, because on 236 held-out links a 2% claim is 5 links and is not distinguishable from zero. If it passes, then and only then: end-to-end held-out recall@10 with the enlarged pool, plus the precision cost of adding 50 candidates per query. KILL IT if reach adds less than 2% of held-out targets with a CI excluding zero, or if end-to-end recall@10 drops.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Diffusion on a personal vault most likely rediscovers the k-means clusters the vault map already exposes, and the two measured priors above (MUSAE 0.737, L3 0.606) both point that way. It also inherits proposal 4's O(N^2) graph-construction blocker, so even a pass is not shippable on its own terms. And sigma and alpha are two per-vault hyperparameters; if the result is sensitive to them it is not shippable at any reach number.

**Needs first.** Proposal 4's mutual-kNN graph, which is itself lab-only. Rooted-PageRank code exists in src/graph-signals.ts and generalizes.


### 6. Receipts in two tiers: ship the free sentence-level one first, then earn the word-level one

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** Each card shows the two sentences that matched, in the notes' own words. Later, if it earns its place, it narrows to the exact words.

**How.** CORRECTED into two tiers, because the free tier already exists and was not proposed. TIER 1, zero forward passes: biMax already selects the winning chunk pair, and the plugin already persists every chunk's text (chunkTexts, on by default via showSummary). So the receipt is a lookup, not an inference — show the two matching sentences. The project's own 2026-07-27 note says exactly this: 'Receipts: still worth building, correctly sized. Detail extraction can run off the chunkTexts the index already keeps (no vault re-read).' Cost is plumbing the winning (i, j) pair out of biMax, which today returns only a number (src/index-store.ts:2934). TIER 2, occlusion word attribution, only if it beats tier 1 in the same blind judging. THE LATENCY ARITHMETIC IN THE ORIGINAL IS WRONG: it claims 30 masked re-embeds at 300-600 ms on WASM, but the same document cites Xenova's benchmark of 378 ms for ONE batch-1 WASM pass, and the plugin runs q8 on WASM in a single worker that idle indexing is already using. Realistic is 1.5-4 s, so the stated 800 ms p95 gate is unreachable on WASM and tier 2 is WebGPU-gated in practice — which makes it gated on proposal 12, which is gated on a first-run probe. Say that plainly instead of listing it as a prereq.

**Why it is not redundant.** Not a ranking channel; it is the receipt, and the strongest version of the existing 'via <note>' promise. It also makes every other proposal here debuggable by a human.

**Kill experiment.** Blind judged, tier 1 as the baseline that tier 2 must beat — the original had no baseline, which is the flaw that would have let a 4-second feature ship against a free one. Sample 60 shown pairs across two vaults. Judge each receipt CORRECT / VACUOUS / MISLEADING with the ranking hidden. Ship tier 1 at >= 80% correct and < 5% misleading. Ship tier 2 ONLY if it beats tier 1 on correct by a margin whose bootstrap CI excludes zero AND does not raise misleading. Latency gates: tier 1 must add under 20 ms (it is a string lookup); tier 2 p95 under 800 ms, which on the numbers above means WebGPU only.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Tier 2's real risk is not latency, it is that occlusion attribution on transformers is unstable — masking a token shifts the whole contextual representation, so the attributed drop can be an artifact of breaking the sentence. Expect a high VACUOUS rate on generic vocabulary. The mitigation (same-POS filler instead of deletion, plus a margin over the second-best word) needs a POS tagger the plugin does not have and will not ship, so in practice the mitigation is the margin alone. If tier 1 scores 85% correct, tier 2 has almost no headroom and should be cut on cost.

**Needs first.** Tier 1: plumb the winning chunk pair out of biMax. Tier 2: proposal 12.


### 7. DEAD — Vault-fitted sparse autoencoder

*expensive · speculative confidence · magic: yes*

**What you get.** (Proposed: the panel names the concept two notes share, and a new view lists the vault's concepts.)

**How.** Marked dead. For the record, the proposal was: train a 384 -> 4096 TopK (k=16) sparse autoencoder on the vault's own centered chunk vectors in a Web Worker with Adam, name each latent by c-TF-IDF over its top-activating chunks, and get both a receipt and a Jaccard-over-latents retrieval channel. Nothing about it is technically incoherent; it is dead on the project's own constraints and its own measurements, listed under risk.

**Why it is not redundant.** The channel arm is a reparameterization of the same centered chunk vectors and is at real risk of the admission rule, as the proposer honestly conceded. The naming arm competes directly with c-TF-IDF cluster labels that already ship at zero training cost.

**Kill experiment.** Not applicable — killed before measurement. If anyone revives it, the binding gate is not retrieval quality, it is the naming precision bar: 40 randomly sampled latents blind-judged FITS / PARTIAL / WRONG, and it must clear 60% FIT on a vault the developer does not own, because the failure mode is a confident false claim about the user's own thinking.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD, four reasons. (1) It puts a training loop in the renderer. The project has already research-rejected DEC with the stated reason 'no renderer training path; self-training amplifies its own errors' — an SAE is the same commitment with 3M parameters instead of a clustering head, competing for the same idle budget the embedding worker already consumes. (2) Its quality gate is a blind human judging protocol run per vault, which the user cannot run; there is no automated signal that tells the plugin whether a given user's 4096 latents are 400 good ones and 3600 noise, and shipping the noisy ones violates precision-first in the most damaging possible way — a false claim about how the user thinks. (3) 20k chunks is one to three orders of magnitude below every corpus these results come from; dead latents and feature splitting are the normal outcome at that scale even with careful tuning, and there is no tuning loop available on device. (4) The proposer's own concession is the decisive one: it may be strictly worse than the k-means plus c-TF-IDF labels the plugin already has for free.

**Needs first.** None — do not build.


### 8. DEAD as a product — cross-encoder reranking, one lab measurement only

*expensive · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** (Proposed: an optional 'sharpen' pass that re-reads the top results and reorders them.)

**How.** Marked dead as a shippable feature; keep exactly one offline measurement because the upside, if it existed, would be large. The proposal was: int8 ONNX shallow cross-encoder over the winning chunk pair biMax already selects, capped at 256 tokens, scored symmetrically in both directions. THE SIZE FIGURE IS WRONG AND IT CHANGES THE VERDICT. A 17 MB TinyBERT-class checkpoint is English-only. This vault is German and English, and the proposal itself concedes the multilingual requirement pushes toward bge-reranker-v2-m3 — which is roughly 560 MB at int8, not 17 MB. That is a second heavy model, which hard constraint 3 forbids in as many words ('the only heavy model is a small multilingual sentence-embedding model'). The lab measurement that IS worth running: offline in Python, score the winning chunk pairs on the 465-note vault with a multilingual cross-encoder, and report held-out recall@10 and non-obvious-slice recall. If it lifts recall by more than 0.06 (about 2 standard errors) the roadmap changes and someone can argue about the download. If it does not, the question is closed forever for the price of an afternoon.

**Why it is not redundant.** A cross-encoder attends across both texts jointly, which a bi-encoder mathematically cannot represent at any embedding quality. That argument is sound; it is the only reason to spend the afternoon.

**Kill experiment.** Lab only, no plugin code. Held-out link recall@10 and non-obvious-slice recall for funnel-alone versus funnel-then-rerank, on the 465-note vault, with paired bootstrap CIs (proposal 18). Do NOT bother with blind precision judging or WASM latency measurement until the offline recall number clears +0.06 — the original's kill bar of +0.02 is below one standard error on 236 held-out links and would have produced a false positive roughly a third of the time.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD as a product, three reasons. (1) A second heavy model download contradicts hard constraint 3, and the multilingual requirement makes it 560 MB rather than the 17 MB quoted. (2) The strongest evidence in the whole list is against it and it is the project's own: BGE-M3 windows 0.35, BGE-M3 whole-note 0.21, against MiniLM 0.43 — every off-the-shelf reranker carries exactly the query-to-document training distribution that produced those numbers, so it may confidently reorder in the wrong direction. (3) The latency gate of 2 s p95 on WASM will not be met: 40 forward passes at 256 tokens with a q8 model in a worker that is also indexing, against a published 378 ms for one batch-1 pass, is seconds not milliseconds.

**Needs first.** None — lab measurement only.


### 9. Fit the fusion weight that already exists

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** On a densely linked vault the link signal counts for more; on a vault with almost no links it quietly counts for nothing. You never touch a slider, and one slider disappears from settings.

**How.** CORRECTED on one factual error and one methodological one, but keep it — this is the best cost/benefit item on the list. FACTUAL: the ranker does not hardcode 50/50 and there is no new parameter to introduce. rank() computes fused = z(content) + graphInfluence * z(RA) (src/index-store.ts:2630-2634), where graphInfluence is a persisted user setting defaulting to 1 (src/main.ts:143). In convex terms that IS alpha = 0.5, so the effect is as described, but the parameter is already plumbed, persisted and user-visible. The work is therefore much smaller than presented: fit the existing setting and remove its slider. Fitting: at index time hold out 10% of the vault's resolved wikilinks, sweep graphInfluence over 11 values, keep the argmax of recall@10, clamp to a sane band, store it in the manifest, refit on link-count growth. Fewer than ~50 links skips the fit and uses content only. Coordinate ascent generalizes it to future channels. Note also that this is already on the project's own list: lab/RESEARCH-embedding-papers.md line 217 says 'our z-sum is the alpha=0.5 special case; fitting alpha on the vault's own links is nearly free'.

**Why it is not redundant.** Adds no channel. It fixes the fusion of channels already admitted, and it builds the per-vault self-calibration loop every later channel will need.

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED — the original kill bar was inside the noise. The lab vault has 236 held-out links (lab/results/graph-fusion-eval2.json), so the binomial standard error on recall@10 at 0.745 is about 0.028; a threshold of '0.01 recall' is four times inside it. Replace with: nested cross-validation, fit on link split A, evaluate on disjoint split B, 10 seeds, and require the PAIRED bootstrap CI on the delta to exclude zero. Then repeat on three or four vaults of different link density. STABILITY IS THE PRIMARY GATE, not the delta: report the across-split standard deviation of the fitted weight and KILL IT if that exceeds 0.15, because an unstable fitted weight is worse than a stable wrong one and it is also the failure mode that the rejected personal-metric work exhibited.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The personal-metric result is the warning and it is more specific than the proposer allowed: fitting on the user's links gave +0.028 alone but only +0.0056 once the graph channel was present, and the LEARNING CURVE WAS FLAT (266 links 0.685, 1062 links 0.693) — it did not compound with vault size, which was its whole premise. One scalar is the weakest possible member of that family and sample-efficiency at one parameter is the published headline, so the defence holds. But the honest expected outcome is a fitted weight near 1.0 on most vaults and a delta inside the noise, with the real payoff being the graceful degradation on link-sparse vaults and one fewer slider.

**Needs first.** None. A held-out-links splitter exists in the bench harnesses; this moves a small version into the plugin.


### 10. Calibrated cutoff — but measure the label-free baseline before importing conformal machinery

*moderate · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** When your vault genuinely has eight related notes you see eight. When it has none you see none, instead of two weak guesses.

**How.** CORRECTED, and downgraded, because the proposal's own self-criticism is fatal in a way it does not follow through on. The exchangeability violation is not a technicality: the calibration positives are the links the user ALREADY MADE, and the plugin exists to surface the 30.7% of relations they have not. Calibrating 'is this a good suggestion' on 'did the user already link it' labels the non-obvious discoveries as negatives, which pushes the threshold up and suppresses exactly the cards the product is for. That is a labelling error, not a distribution shift. The corrected plan: first measure the far cheaper thing conformal is competing against — a per-vault quantile of the fused score over a random sample of note pairs. That needs NO labels at all, therefore no exchangeability assumption, costs one pass over a few thousand random pairs on idle, and gives 'show a card only if it beats what 99.9% of random pairs score in THIS vault'. Only if that fails does the conformal machinery earn its complexity. Also note the premise may be smaller than assumed: the fixed-threshold drift across nested vault sizes was measured at 0.018 for plain cosine (bench/v3-calibration.mjs), which is not obviously a problem worth a finite-sample guarantee.

**Why it is not redundant.** It does not rank; it decides how much of the ranking to show, which is the precision-first principle made numerical. It also makes every future channel cheaper to admit, because a channel that adds recall but hurts precision gets caught by the cutoff instead of by a human retuning a constant.

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED to include the right baseline. On four or more vaults of different size, density and language mix, compare THREE cutoffs: fixed 0.2, the label-free random-pair quantile, and the conformal threshold at target alpha in {0.1, 0.2, 0.3}. Report realized precision of the displayed set against held-out links, mean cards shown, and threshold drift across nested vault sizes (the existing baseline number to beat is cosine's 0.018). Then the sensitivity analysis the original correctly asked for: hold out links from the most recent 20% of notes by TIME rather than at random. KILL the conformal arm if it does not beat the label-free quantile on realized precision, or if realized precision misses its target by more than 0.1 on any vault, or if the time-split shows large drift — in which case the guarantee is decorative and must not be claimed in the UI.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DOWNGRADED because the calibration set is systematically wrong in a direction that harms the feature, as above. Second: the project already measured Mutual Proximity specifically to obtain calibrated [0,1] scores and rejected it — it cost recall (0.745 to 0.706 with graph) and was WORSE at calibration, the thing it was for. That is one prior attempt at exactly this goal, failed on measurement. Third: shipping a user-facing setting phrased as a risk level ('rarely / sometimes') makes a statistical promise the exchangeability violation means the plugin cannot keep.

**Needs first.** Shares almost all its code with proposal 9's splitter.


### 11. Per-note dimension selection (DIME/ECLIPSE) at rank time

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Nothing visible. Results get slightly sharper on notes with a strong single topic.

**How.** KEEP essentially as proposed — it is genuinely cheap to test and the proposer's own scepticism is correctly calibrated. For the active note take its top-5 content neighbours as pseudo-relevant, a fixed random sample of 200 notes as pseudo-irrelevant, score each dimension by |mean_rel(d) - mean_irrel(d)| / (std_irrel(d) + eps), zero the bottom fraction in both the active and candidate vectors, renormalize, rescore the shortlist. One 205 x 384 pass plus a masked rescore of ~100 candidates. Two additions from the local record: the pseudo-relevance half has a POSITIVE local prior (Rocchio feedback moved concept-search member-P@10 from 0.650 to 0.683 on jina), and the composition order the proposer worried about is achievable — subtractTemplateDirections() runs inside recomputeCentroid() (src/index-store.ts:2289), i.e. at centering time, strictly before rank(), so DIME composed after de-crowding is the natural order rather than something to engineer.

**Why it is not redundant.** Weakly independent at best, and the proposer said so plainly. It is a per-query reweighting of the same vectors and will correlate strongly with content by construction. It is here because it costs almost nothing to test, composes with the FDE/PCA/BiMax stages independently, and is a tuning knob rather than a channel.

**Kill experiment.** Held-out link recall@10 with and without, at mask fractions 25/50/75%, on both vaults and both models, with paired bootstrap CIs. Separately on the Surprising Connections precision protocol, and separately on the NON-OBVIOUS slice — the worry is that dimension masking sharpens obvious matches and destroys non-obvious ones, and pooled recall@10 cannot see that because the obvious 69% dominates it. KILL IT if delta recall <= 0, if surprising-connections precision@5 drops at all, or if the non-obvious slice drops.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The nearest measured relative on this corpus lost: all-but-the-top, which is also 'zero some directions', took R@10 from 0.693 to 0.657 AND raised hubness skew. Second, pseudo-relevance from the top-5 amplifies whatever the top-5 got wrong; on a note whose top-5 are template siblings the selected dimensions ARE the template direction, the exact thing de-crowding removes — compose after de-crowding and check the interaction explicitly rather than assuming it.

**Needs first.** None.


### 12. WebGPU by default — but the probe as designed cannot work, because the two devices load different weights

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** First indexing of a big vault finishes overnight instead of over a week, and everything that reads notes on demand feels instant.

**How.** CORRECTED on the fact that breaks the proposed probe. The premise checks out: settings default to device 'auto' (src/main.ts:122), and runtimeBuildFor() returns 'wasm' unless devicePref is explicitly 'webgpu' (src/embeddings.ts:109-111), so WebGPU really is opt-in. But the worker chooses DIFFERENT WEIGHTS PER DEVICE: dtype is fp32 on WebGPU and q8 on WASM (src/worker/embed-worker.ts:195). So flipping the provider is not a provider swap — it is a different model download (roughly 4x the bytes) with different numerics, and the proposal's own correctness gate (per-vector cosine >= 0.999 between providers) FAILS BY CONSTRUCTION because it would be comparing q8-on-WASM against fp32-on-WebGPU. The probe must hold dtype fixed: fp32-on-WASM versus fp32-on-WebGPU to isolate the provider, with q8-versus-fp32 handled separately as a QUALITY question with its own held-out-link measurement, not a speed one. THE FIRST TASK IS SMALLER AND MORE URGENT THAN THE PROBE: the manifest gates rebuilds on modelId only (load(), src/index-store.ts:1364) and records nothing about device or dtype, so the index is ALREADY device-dependent and a user toggling the device today silently mixes fp32 and q8 vectors in one index. Record device and dtype in StoredIndexHeader and invalidate on change. That is a handful of lines and it is a correctness fix regardless of whether the auto-default ships.

**Why it is not redundant.** Pure infrastructure. It is also the enabling budget for the tier-2 receipts and for the passage-selection feature, both of which are latency-bound.

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED. (0) The dtype/device manifest fix, verified by a test that toggling the device forces a rebuild. (1) Numerical agreement AT FIXED DTYPE: per-vector cosine between WASM-fp32 and WebGPU-fp32 over 500 chunks on three or more GPU vendors; must pass at >= 0.999 or the index becomes provider-dependent. (2) SEPARATELY, the quality question the original conflated with it: held-out link recall@10 for q8 versus fp32 weights, because if q8 is measurably worse then the download-size tradeoff is a product decision and not a device decision. (3) First-index wall clock at 5k/20k/100k on three machines. (4) No UI frame longer than 50 ms during indexing. KILL the auto-default (keep it opt-in) if agreement fails on any vendor or the probe misclassifies on any tested machine.

**Most likely reason it fails.** Driver and vendor variance is the whole risk: Linux/Mesa, older Intel iGPUs and Safari are all places where WebGPU is present, slow, and occasionally wrong. Beyond that, the omission above means the honest user-facing cost is a second, larger model download to switch device plus a mandatory full re-index — which makes 'automatic and evidence-based' a much heavier action than the proposal implies, and probably means the probe should run BEFORE the first index rather than as a flip.

**Needs first.** ORT session plumbing already supports both providers. The manifest device/dtype field is a prerequisite for even measuring this safely.


### 13. Select a passage, get its relatives

*cheap · high confidence · magic: partly*

**What you get.** Highlight a paragraph, and the panel shows the passages elsewhere in your vault that say the same thing, with the matching text shown, not just the note title.

**How.** KEEP — this is the strongest cost/benefit item on the list and the mechanism is right. Passage vectors already exist and are currently only intermediates inside biMax, never surfaced. On selection or command, embed the selected text once, run the existing funnel with that vector as the source, BiMax against passage vectors only, render note title plus the winning passage. Suppress hits from the active note. Two small corrections. First, the plumbing is closer than 'a new entry point' suggests: rankForContext already exists as a path that takes a source other than the active file, so the work is generalizing the source vector rather than building a second funnel. Second, this composes directly with the tier-1 receipts in proposal 6 — both need the winning chunk index out of biMax and both render stored chunk text, so build them together.

**Why it is not redundant.** A new entry point, not a new channel, so the admission rule does not bite. It monetizes indexing work already paid for.

**Kill experiment.** CORRECTED on the latency gate and on how the model-gating risk is settled. Blind judged precision: 50 selections across both vaults, stratified 25 prose / 25 list-or-structured, top-3 passages each, judged SAME-THING / RELATED-TOPIC-ONLY / UNRELATED. Bar: P@3 >= 0.7 on SAME-THING, and >= 0.4 on the structured stratum or the feature says it only works on prose. LATENCY: the original's 400 ms p95 on WASM is optimistic against the 378 ms it quotes for a single batch-1 WASM pass; budget the embed at 200-400 ms plus the funnel and set the honest gate at 700 ms, or bind the feature to an explicit command so nothing waits on a keystroke. MINIMUM SELECTION LENGTH MUST BE MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: report P@3 by selection-length decile and set the floor from the data.

**Most likely reason it fails.** A short selection against a document is the same out-of-distribution problem that gates concept search, and that one is measured: the same pipeline scores 0.783 on jina and 0.417 on MiniLM, which is the shipped default. So the realistic outcome is that this feature is good on jina, mediocre on MiniLM, and has to say so — the same model-gating conversation, on a feature far more visible than concept search. Budget for shipping it gated rather than for it working everywhere.

**Needs first.** Passage vectors already stored. Needs an editor-selection hook and a source-vector entry point into rank(); shares the biMax chunk-pair plumbing with proposal 6.


### 14. DEAD — inference-free SPLADE; run the rare-term slice on the BM25 data you already have

*cheap · speculative confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** (Proposed: notes sharing a precise technical term stop being missed by a purely semantic ranker.)

**How.** Marked dead as proposed. The proposer was already half-convinced and the local record finishes the argument: a lexical channel was measured on this exact vault and content+lexical scored 0.602 against content alone at 0.664 — it actively HURT — with rank correlation 0.304 against content (bench/v4-channels.mjs). SPLADE's counter-argument is that expansion is a different object from overlap, but SPLADE-doc's expansion vocabulary is learned from English MS MARCO query-to-passage data, which is the worst available prior for a bilingual German-compound vault; and it is a second ~110M-parameter download, contradicting hard constraint 3. WHAT TO DO INSTEAD, and it needs no new model at all: the proposer's own rare-term slice analysis can be run TODAY against the BM25 channel already implemented in bench/v4-channels.mjs. Slice the held-out links by whether source and target share a term with document frequency <= 3. If the lexical channel's value is concentrated there, build a rare-term-only exact-match channel — an inverted index over the vault's own ~30k-term vocabulary restricted to low-df terms, no model, no download, kilobytes of code — and put THAT through the admission gate. That is the honest descendant and it costs an afternoon.

**Why it is not redundant.** The rare-term substitute has a real independence argument that the full lexical channel does not: a df<=3 term match is close to a hard identity constraint (a person's name, a filename, a notation) and is not a smooth function of cosine, whereas full BM25 is dominated by mid-frequency terms that the embedding already encodes — which is exactly why the full channel correlated at 0.304 and lost.

**Kill experiment.** No SPLADE. Reuse the existing BM25 channel. Slice held-out link recall by rare-term sharing (df <= 3) and report the lexical channel's lift restricted to that slice with a bootstrap CI — note the slice will be small, so the CI is the whole point. If the lift is real, build the rare-term-only channel and run the standard admission gate: rank correlation against content and against link-RA (gate |rho| < 0.35), then delta held-out recall@10 under three-channel fusion with paired bootstrap. KILL the whole line if the rare-term slice shows no concentration.

**Most likely reason it fails.** DEAD as proposed: a second ~110M model download contradicts hard constraint 3 on a plugin whose selling point is one small local model; the closest measured relative on this vault made ranking worse and correlated at 0.304; and the expansion vocabulary's training distribution is English query-to-passage, the same distribution that already produced the repo's worst model A/B results. The rare-term substitute's own risk is that the slice turns out to be tiny — a few dozen held-out links — so no result on it will be statistically distinguishable from zero, in which case the answer is 'unmeasurable at this corpus size' and it goes on the list of things that need a bigger corpus first.

**Needs first.** None. The BM25 channel already exists in bench/v4-channels.mjs.


### 15. NEW — get chunk text out of the JSON manifest: this, not ANN, is what breaks at 100k

*cheap · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** A very large vault opens in seconds instead of stalling, and Obsidian stops using several gigabytes of memory when the plugin loads.

**How.** Every one of the twelve proposals above argues about tens of megabytes while the manifest is two orders of magnitude larger, and nobody looked. showSummary defaults to true (src/main.ts:136), which sets hasChunkText, which persists EVERY chunk's raw text (~480 chars, MAX_CHUNK_CHARS) into index.json. At 100k notes with the adaptive cap that is on the order of a million strings and several hundred megabytes of JSON. And load() parses it TWICE: JSON.parse(json) at src/index-store.ts:1361 for the header check, then deserializeIndex(json, blob) at line 1392 parses the same string again (src/vector-math.ts:463). Two full parses, two full string heaps, synchronously, in the renderer. The fix, all cheap: (a) split the manifest into a tiny header file carrying version/modelId/dims/dtype/device/totalBytes/entryCount, so the staleness check needs no full parse and a stale index is discarded without ever materializing it; (b) move chunkTexts into the binary blob as a UTF-8 region with per-entry offsets, decoded lazily through the same LRU discipline DequantCache already implements for vectors — a card renders one chunk's text, never a million; (c) parse the entry manifest once and pass the parsed object into deserializeIndex instead of the string. (b) also shrinks the index on disk, since UTF-8 bytes beat JSON-escaped strings.

**Why it is not redundant.** Pure infrastructure, no channel, no ranking change. It is the prerequisite that makes the 100k-note claim in hard constraint 4 true rather than aspirational, and it is what buys the memory budget proposals 1, 2 and 3 all want to spend.

**Kill experiment.** Not a retrieval experiment. Build a 100k-note synthetic vault from real note text (the bench already has vault-walking and stub-loading machinery). Measure cold-load wall clock and peak renderer RSS at 5k / 20k / 100k, with and without the change, showSummary on. GATE: 100k must load in under 10 s and stay under 1.5 GiB peak, which is roughly what the 4 GiB WASM cap leaves once the model and the vector blob are resident. Assert byte-identical rank() output on the lab vault before and after, and assert that a stale index (wrong modelId) is discarded without a full manifest parse. KILL IT if the split does not cut peak RSS by at least 40% at 20k notes — which would mean the vectors, not the text, dominate and the diagnosis is wrong.

**Most likely reason it fails.** The honest risk is that I am wrong about the magnitude because real vaults have far fewer chunks per note than the cap suggests, in which case the fix is still correct but far less urgent — that is precisely why the first deliverable is the RSS measurement at 20k and not the refactor. Secondary risk: moving text into the blob means a label edit rewrites the blob, which the current design deliberately avoids (persistManifestOnly exists so a lazy label drain does not copy multi-MB vectors). That has to be preserved, probably by giving text its own third file rather than sharing the vector blob.

**Needs first.** None. Touches serializeIndex/deserializeIndex in src/vector-math.ts and load/persist in src/index-store.ts.


### 16. NEW — incremental centering: stop rebuilding every centered vector on every edit

*moderate · high confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Editing a note in a large vault stops making Obsidian hitch.

**How.** recomputeCentroid() (src/index-store.ts:2264) clears centeredMeans and allocates a fresh centered Float32Array for every note in the vault, then re-derives every template group inside subtractTemplateDirections(), and it is called from four sites including the incremental-embed path. At 100k x 384 that is about 153 MB of allocation and a full template re-derivation per note edit, synchronously. The fix: (a) center at WRITE time and store the centered mean in the blob — the map view already consumes the centered vector (vaultMapInput, line 1017), so nothing needs the raw one at rank time; (b) maintain the centroid as a running sum plus count so a single note edit is an O(D) update rather than O(N x D); (c) re-center the whole corpus only when the centroid has drifted past a threshold (say cosine between old and new below 0.9995), run as an idle job with a progress state exactly like indexing; (d) give template directions the same treatment, incrementally by heading-group rather than from scratch. The deeper reason this matters: every derived representation the other proposals want — idea vectors (1), sign bits (2), PCA coefficients (3) — is invalidated by a centroid change. Without hysteresis all three thrash on every keystroke-triggered reindex, which is why this is a prerequisite and not a nice-to-have.

**Why it is not redundant.** Infrastructure. It changes no ranking and adds no channel; it is what makes derived-representation caching possible at all.

**Kill experiment.** Wall clock of a single-note edit at 5k / 20k / 100k, with and without, measured as main-thread block time rather than total time. CORRECTNESS FIRST: assert rank() output is byte-identical to the current path on the lab vault while the drift threshold has not fired, and that held-out link recall@10 is statistically unchanged (paired bootstrap) when it does fire — the risk being that a slightly stale centroid quietly degrades ranking between refreshes. Sweep the drift threshold over three values and report recall at each. KILL IT if any drift threshold that gives an acceptable refresh rate also changes held-out recall@10 with a CI excluding zero, or if any single-edit main-thread block still exceeds 50 ms at 100k.

**Most likely reason it fails.** A stale centroid means the panel ranks in slightly different geometry than a freshly-centred vault would, and the plugin's precision-first stance means silently drifting scores are worse than a visible pause. The threshold sweep is what settles that, and the honest bad outcome is that the acceptable threshold is so tight it fires constantly on an actively-edited vault, leaving only the O(D) running-sum win. Second risk: storing centered vectors makes the blob depend on the centroid, so a re-centre becomes a full blob rewrite — mitigate by storing raw vectors in the blob and the centering as a separate small correction, at the cost of some of the win.

**Needs first.** None, but proposals 1, 2 and 3 all depend on it.


### 17. NEW — content-addressed chunk vectors: stop re-embedding text that has not changed

*moderate · medium confidence · magic: no*

**What you get.** Editing one paragraph re-indexes that paragraph instead of the whole note, and a vault full of notes made from the same template indexes in a fraction of the time.

**How.** embedNoteDegrading() embeds EVERY chunk of a note on any mtime change (src/index-store.ts:2123, use.map(c => chunkEmbedInput(c))). Editing one paragraph of a 17-chunk note pays 17 forward passes for 16 unchanged ones. Worse, a template-heavy vault embeds the same boilerplate text once per note: the plugin already knows templates exist — it spends real effort subtracting their shared direction in vector space — but pays full inference price for them at embed time. Fix: key each chunk vector by a 128-bit hash of its exact embed input (chunkEmbedInput(c), so the heading breadcrumb and folded tags are included) plus modelId plus dtype. Keep a vault-global hash -> (blob offset) map, refcounted, rebuilt from the manifest in one pass on load so a crash cannot corrupt it. On re-embed, only chunks whose hash is absent go to the worker. Storage is one 16-byte hash per chunk; the vectors are already stored. This attacks the same number proposal 12 attacks with WebGPU, but with arithmetic instead of a hardware bet, it works on every machine, and the two multiply.
